# Compatibility gap map (Unity 6000.0.71f1)

What Unbound does not yet do as the Editor does, found by auditing the engine itself rather than
waiting for a game to hit it. `ROADMAP.md` says what is done and probed; this file lists what is
left, ranked, so work can go down it.

Audit of 2026-10-29: read-only, every subsystem; nothing was measured against the Editor, so each
row still needs a probe before and after it is fixed. Paths are relative to `managed/UnityEngine/`
unless they start with `crates/` or `native/`.

## API declaration follow-ups

AssetBundleManifest typed reader and independent clone ownership are covered by
`tests/projects/manifestadapters` on Unity 6000.0.71f1, with 913 exact controls across
three bundle formats, array mutation and unload/reload. Other declaration debt stays
in [API_DECLARATION_FOLLOWUPS.md](API_DECLARATION_FOLLOWUPS.md).

## Known insignificant residuals

By the user's decision of 2026-11-01, small differences with no visible effect or
game-logic change do not block package admission. Raw values and comparator
tolerances stay unchanged; strict verifiers still report these failures.
Roughly 1-3/255 in isolated pixels is an example, not a blanket tolerance.
Visible or functional systematic biases, wrong functional answers, changes in
survival, death/event timing or ordering, and actual bugs remain actionable.
A numeric lifetime-field difference alone does not establish a functional change;
its effect on the measured histories still matters. No Native captures or deep research are spent solely
to eliminate a listed insignificant residual.

| humanoid / 6000.0.71f1 | `blendTransition.step18.body` pivotWeight, pivotPosition; `blend*.step5`, `blendTransition.step24`, `blendAdditive.step17`, `mask*.step5`/`step17`, `maskOverride{UpperRoot,NoFootIK}.step16`, `additiveTree.step5`/`step17`, `settings*.step4`/`step5`/`step17`, `settingsFeetRight.step11` `.motion` velocity; `stateMirror*`, `additiveMirror`, `transitionMirror`, `ik{,Clip}Mirror` `.step5`/`step10`/`step11`/`step14`/`step15`/`step17` `.motion` velocity (mirrored states, the same small steps); `exit*`, `crossFadeFixed`, `crossFadeOffset`, `playState`, `events`, `eventsMirror`, `additiveMirrorClip`, `longState`, `longStateB`, `emptyExit`, `emptyCond`, `emptyBack` `.motion` velocity on the same kind of small steps; `settingsBakeOffset.step15`, `settingsCycle.step23` `.body` pivotPosition[0] (4.6e-4 of 1.86, 4.0e-4 of 0.42, pivotWeight matching); `ik`, `ikB`, `ikMirror`, `ikClipMirror` `.pose` muscles[41]/[43] (left Arm Twist In-Out and Forearm Twist In-Out) on most steps | pivotWeight `0.4642` versus `0.4632` and pivotPosition up to `0.056` (of about 31) at the update where the clip's motion reverses (both feet slips about 1e-7); velocity components up to 3.8e-4 absolute (7e-3 relative on a component of about 0.055 beside one of about 52) where the step is small; muscles 41 and 43 up to 0.0144 (0.0028 on `ikB`), equal and opposite, always a whole multiple (1 to 4; 4 on `ikMirror.step6`) of 0.0036 (HumanA) or 0.00093 (HumanB), with every transform's locals matching | Float precision: the pivot weight is the ratio of two near-zero slips there, and the next update matches again; velocity is the small delta over the delta time; the two settings pivotPositions differ by about 4e-4 on their x component, which is small beside the others (about 26), with the pivot weight matching. No effect on the root's path (rootPosition, deltaPosition match). Measured 2026-11-06 (Unbound traced against the reference): (1) velocity: the deltaPosition errors are about one float step of the clip-space root motion (values about 1, 1.2e-7) times the root's scale 100 and humanScale 1.05, about 1.25e-5 world, of either sign and without bias (whole steps on the large forward z component, fractions of one on x and y); velocity multiplies them by 30, and whichever component is small beside the others fails the relative check (y about 0.9 beside z about 40, or z on `blendTransition.step24`, `settingsFeetRight.step11` and `stateMirrorB`). (2) pivot: at `blendTransition.step18` both feet slips are 1.4e-7 and 1.6e-7 (clip units, a few float steps of the foot positions), so their ratio is rounding; at `settingsBakeOffset.step15`/`settingsCycle.step23` pivotWeight differs by 7.7e-6/1.6e-5 (within tolerance) and the feet are about 60 world units apart, giving 4e-4 on pivotPosition[0]. (3) arm twist: `HumanPoseHandler.GetHumanPose` removes the forearm's y rotation by turning the upper arm, stopping at the first iteration whose angle grows (under 1 degree). For these IK poses the angle does not converge: it stays at 0.6496 degrees, moving by float noise (about 1e-5 degrees) each iteration, while each iteration moves one 0.0036 step of twist from the forearm to the upper arm. Forcing 1 to 5 iterations reproduces Unity's value at every one of the 116 steps; which count Unity stops at depends on the last bits. In the Editor too, nearly the same looped pose (`ik.step3` and `step27`, `ikB.step0`/`26`, `3`/`27`, `6`/`28`, the other 93 muscles within 1.5e-5) reads back one to three steps apart. No visible effect in the probe (the transforms match; the two muscles' sum matches); a game that writes the read pose back with SetHumanPose gets the twist split between upper arm and forearm differently by up to that much, as the Editor's own spread does. No systematic cause: recorded, not fixed. Since Humanoid transform keys take the single-precision frame times (2026-11-11) the same three families sit on partly other keys: velocity also on `crossFade`, `exit020`, `mixer.clipB`, `mixer.weight1`, `settingsBakeOrig`, `settingsOrigRot` and `stateMirrorB` steps, pivotPosition[0] on `eventsMirrorCycle.step8`, `stateMirrorCycle` and `transitionMirror` in place of `settingsBakeOffset.step15`, the arm twist on other `ik*` steps; five leg-muscle values of the Foot IK family newly exceed the tolerance (`stream.ik.step10` and `stream.ikPass.step10` muscles[35] by 2.4e-3 and muscles[33] by 8.1e-4, `stream.bodyLocal.step24` muscles[31] by 3.4e-4: the sizes the family has on its other steps) while eight listed ones match; they count as differences, the list in `known.json` only shrinks. Since the root motion fix of `genericOnHuman.graph` (Q525: the Animator keeps the root rotation as the Transform stores it, normalized; its 22 values now match) the families sit on partly other keys again; on Linux 418 differences remain, each in these families or the `longClip` row: velocity on 88 values of the plays above and 31 of the `mixer.*` plays (HumanA's steps 5 and 17 on `mixer.clip`, `mixer.one`, `mixer.upper` and the others, as on the controller plays, besides `mixer.clipB`/`mixer.weight1`), the arm twist on 134 `ik*` values, the Foot IK leg muscles on 10 `stream.*` values (among them `stream.bodyLocal.step28` muscles[29], [32] to [35], up to 4.8e-4), pivotPosition[0] on four, `body.directHighFootIK.step4` muscles[35]; 241 known. |

| humanoid / 6000.0.71f1 | `stream.stance` `.pose` bodyPosition and leg, hand and arm muscles (61 values in `known.json`, `stance-drift`); `stream.ik`, `stream.ikPass`, `stream.bodyLocal` `.pose` leg muscles on single steps (listed: [31], [33], [35]; since Q525 also `stream.bodyLocal.step28` [29], [32] to [35]) (`foot-ik-legs`); `bone.rotateThenSolve`, `bone.solveTwice` arm roll muscles[41], [43], the upper arm's and hand's local rotations and left foot muscles[26], [27] (`readback-solve-straight-limbs`) | Stance: the read-back body up to 3.3e-3 (the hips' world position up to 3.6e-3 on a character 105 units tall, 3.4e-5 of its height), leg muscles up to 1.5e-2 (a straight leg's twist; a foot's up-down 2.5e-3), hand and arm muscles up to 9e-4, within the 29 updates recorded. Foot IK legs: up to 2.4e-3 on one step. Read-back solve: arm roll muscles up to 9e-3 (their sum within 9e-5), left foot muscles up to 1.4e-3; the class is bounded, not listed: each of these muscles and the two local rotations within 1e-2, the roll muscles' sum within 1e-4 on every step | Measured 2026-11-11, one rig and one clip, with every transform recorded in Editor captures outside the reference. (1) Stance (a job resets the stream to the stance pose each update, the state's Foot IK then pulls the straight legs to a walk's foot goals, root motion applied): the Editor's values depend on how far the Animator is from the origin (here root motion moves it; Q548 below: a static placement does the same); Unbound's muscles do not, and its stream body about seven times less. Kept at the origin (`stream.stanceInPlace`, in the reference) the two agree on every value. With root motion the muscles the Editor's job reads right after the reset already step as the root moves (the left upper leg's front-back 0.5996863 to 0.5996884 in updates 0 to 3, 0.5997035 in update 4, 0.5996372 in 5 to 7 and 0.5997034 from 8; 0.5996863 on every update in place, 0.5996861 in Unbound), its stream body after the reset wanders over 2.5e-4 where it is constant in place (Unbound's over 3.4e-5), and in the final pose the hips' local position is off Unbound's by about (0, 1.3e-6, -0.5e-6) of the root's local units in updates 5 to 7, about (0, 6e-6, 1.5e-6) in 13 to 20 and (0, -3.5e-5, -5.9e-6) from 21 on (times 100 in the world; under 5e-7 in the other updates), the arms' rotations by up to 0.038 degrees and the legs' by up to 0.047; the hips and the arms sit on runs of updates with steps between them, the legs change every update from 21 on. Every step starts at the update where the root's world z first passes a power of two: 2 at update 4, 4 at 5, 8 at 8, 16 at 13 and 32 at 21; each step so far is larger than the last, and the root is 46 world units from the origin by update 28. Unbound's hips follow a smooth, symmetric path over the cycle. That is the mark of single-precision loss in the reset away from the origin rather than of a missing Foot IK mechanism; what exactly produces the steps is not known (Q548 below locates it in the reset's body). Q548 (Linux GPU Editor captures outside the reference, root motion off, the stance job and HumanA's clip without it on an Animator placed at z 0, 3, 40, 1000, 1e5 and 2.8e6): the drift needs no root motion, only the distance: the stance job matches the Editor at 0 and 3 and differs from 40 on (muscles up to 5.4e-3 and the body up to 3.3e-3 at 40, 4.2e-3 and 2.7e-3 at 1000, the body's z by 23 at 2.8e6), while the clip without the job, Foot IK included, matches at every offset (pose within 1e-4). It starts in what the stream answers right after `ResetToStancePose`: of the five muscles the job reads there, the Editor's move with the offset from z 3 on (the left upper leg's front-back 0.5996863 at 0, 0.5997035 at 3 and 40, 0.59970367 at 1000; the right arm's down-up by up to 4e-5) while Unbound's stay as at the origin, and the body carries most of the difference: both engines' stance body comes out on the float grid of the world offset (steps of 6.1e-5 at 1000, 0.0078 at 1e5; y alike), its z 405 steps in the Editor and 393 in Unbound at 1000 (the exact stance 390.3), 4 and 5 steps at 1e5, and at 2.8e6 (steps of 0.25) -96 steps (-24) in the Editor against 2 (0.5) in Unbound (the exact stance 0.024, 0.1 step); the Editor's x moves by up to 6e-6 with the offset where Unbound's does not. Unbound's error stays within about three steps of the grid; the Editor's does not (15 steps at 1000, about 96 at 2.8e6): a coarser computation through world-sized values, whose operations these captures do not identify. Only a job that resets the stream to the stance away from the origin was measured to trigger it. Also ruled out: rounding of the foot goals (moving them by one single-precision step changes Unbound's result by at most 3e-7 on the body and 1e-4 on a muscle; measured once, runs not kept), the stabilize-feet path (off here), the pivot weight (equal within 6e-6, no event at those updates). (2) Foot IK legs after a job changed the pose: moving the foot goals by one single-precision step changes Unbound's own result by the same 2.4e-3 on the same value (`stream.ik.step10` muscles[35]) and by no more than 6e-6 when the job only reads; the difference is consistent with amplified rounding of a nearly straight leg (measured once, runs not kept). Since Humanoid transform keys take the single-precision frame times the family sits on other steps (five values counted as differences, one left in `known.json`; `stream.bodyLocal` step 17 muscles 23 and 25, up to 5.7e-4, joined it with the Mono float opt-in, where game code's quaternion products evaluate as Mono does). (3) A solve after the job read the pose back on the root scaled by 100 (`bone.rotateThenSolve`, `bone.solveTwice`, 140 keys in `known.json` from Unbound runs on macOS and Linux; with the Mono float opt-in, game code's quaternion products evaluated as Mono does move the split onto other steps, `readback-solve-straight-limbs`, recorded with the macOS Editor): the goal is out of reach of the re-based skeleton in both (the body displaced toward the root alike) and the left arm straight; the upper arm and forearm roll muscles split one roll between them, and Unbound's split is off the Editor's in steps of 1.8e-3 (up to 9e-3) that cancel in their sum within 9e-5, with the upper arm's and hand's local rotations following; moving the goal by one single-precision step moves Unbound's own split by 5.4e-3 (step 16, measured once, runs not kept): amplified rounding of a straight arm. The left foot's muscles 26 and 27 are off by up to 1.4e-3 on five steps of each control, not traced further. |

intensity and Gamma encoding. The light-probe image now matches all 57600 pixels
exactly. Its earlier 56 introduced pixels were fixed, not waived as insignificant.

## Failing probes by cause (triage of 2026-11-11)

Every probe that failed in the sheriff's full suite on `a6f0d8af2` (148 pass / 45 fail / 10 report
of 203) and had no open task, with what its failures are and where they are recorded. Counts are
that run's, except for the named follow-up replays in each row, including
Q369/Q296/Q363/Q248 and Q372/Q368/Q353. One probe can hold several causes; each is listed with its own count. Classes:

- **order**: an order or text Unity's documentation leaves open (see the notes under the table);
- **residual**: listed in [Known insignificant residuals](#known-insignificant-residuals);
- **gap**: a real difference, with the row of the map that carries it;
- **probe**: a defect of the probe, its recipe or the way its reference was recorded.

Corrected references are identified below. Comparators and tolerances are unchanged, and raw
engine differences still fail. The shared sheriff baseline has not been promoted by this work.

The 2026-11-12 lifecycle and scripting follow-ups close inherited Edit-mode execution and startup ownership
(`editinheritance`, 171/171 values), imported texture flags (`assetclones`, 14340/14340),
Editor/Play exit controls (`editorcallbacks`, 114/114 values across four quit profiles),
internal reflection/particle-light root ownership (`hiddenroots`, 15/15),
public flag transitions (`hiddenflags`, 557/557), and material reload identity
(`droppedparams`, 252/252; `materialalias`, 62/62), and captured exception output
(`stackcapture`, 15/15; `testrunner`, all 11 PlayMode nodes).
CRT scheduling now matches all 448 customrt values and 228 images, with 229
additional load-cycle controls in crtload. Created-clip memory matches 81 controls
and closes five appfacts values.
The retained callback order, queued-load timing, stack-utility text, input,
importer and numerical differences below remain visible in the strict comparisons.
Each gap row gives the measured context and the remaining follow-up scope.
The implementation follow-ups are shared serialized engine-property bindings,
host keyboard configuration delivery and layout refresh, general game float
evaluation, and the remaining memory and stack-utility coverage. Exact async
completion frames and cross-object callback order retain their scoped records.

| Probe | Failing | What fails | Class and record |

The Q364/Q360 follow-up closes the measured 6000.0.71f1 physics causes above.
The final targeted regression set has 18 passing probes and the retained twod
rendering failure. Physics2ddynamics matches 27676/27676 values, geometryframe2d
50863/50863 and query2dparity 58603/58603, including all 55251 queries. Added
Editor controls preserve every previous scenario dictionary; the query2dparity
and twod references are unchanged. Comparators and tolerances remain unchanged,
and numerical differences within those tolerances remain in the reports.

Open scopes remain explicit. The twod re-sort image needs a rendering follow-up;
its collider.runtime scenario passes. Other Unity releases retain the diagnosed
legacy contact-payload, generated-geometry and cast-witness policies described in
the map. Edge controls cover nonfinite segment metadata and queries, not arbitrary
nonfinite dynamic contacts or malformed adjacent endpoints. The measured results
do not establish universal parity outside the recorded contexts.

Q426 updates link activation without replacing occupied traversals or unrelated
corridors. Queries exclude a deactivated link immediately; affected walking routes
are repaired at the next AI update. Pending results retain staleness through their
publication update and are validated on the following update. The strict navlinks
count falls from 325 to 258 with no newly failing old value. All 2306 values in
eight added activation controls match two identical GPU Editor captures, preserving
every earlier reference row byte for byte. The managed navigation tests pass
308/308. The remaining query, arrival, height and stacked-link work is scoped in
the probe READMEs and queue rows below; no comparator or tolerance was changed.

Q462 reduces the original navlinks scope from 258 to 255 values through placement
and completed-destination state. All 64 added rows and 1555 values match two identical
GPU Editor captures, preserving the old reference byte prefix. The 329 managed
navigation tests and the six-probe regression run pass their existing gates. The
remaining link cases are cross-tile movement, the duplicate endpoint corner, area-mask
movement, and two frame-130 stale reports whose earlier placement history remains
unresolved. The optional navmesh corridor diagnostic retains all 103 differences in
422 values, with a separate reference. Default navmesh, avoidance and query counts
stay unchanged. Braking preparation
reduces the height-agent count from 14 to 1/816, fixing all 13 bump X positions;
the ramp final-arrival velocity remains open. Runtime height generation and
persistent stacked-link ownership
remain separate implementation work; no comparator, tolerance or ordering is relaxed.

The earlier Q401 navigation work reports `hasPath` and `steeringTarget` from the corners
found after each step (reviewed landing on main 05d1946874). The navlinks reference
falls from 374 to 325 differing values and avoidance from 34 configurations in four
groups to 3 in two, with no previously passing value or record lost. The navmesh,
navbaked, navcost and navpath outputs are unchanged. The engine runs were made on
macOS arm64 against the existing Linux Editor references; the Linux land gate gives
the same counts. No new Editor capture was recorded.

Earlier work (Q385) took runtime mesh regions first, then neighbour prediction, link
movement and reporting, query/height scope and closure, each in its own reviewed landing.
The original translated-build differences fell from 36 to zero, with all nine bounds
controls matching, and the restored-baked-mesh agent failures from 238 to 212 frame
scenarios. All 1453 added navlinks orientation, endpoint, stopping and transform-write
records and all 608 values in 44 added navmesh prediction, finite-input and
large-obstacle scenarios match.

The six-probe regression gate retains strict raw failures: navmesh 212 frame
scenarios, navlinks 255 values, 3 avoidance configurations, navpath placed routes
23/163, wide-link offsets 2/167 and post avoidance 17/578, and navbaked height agents
14/816 (navpath `carve.rules` 2/551 are recorded in the carving row). Imported height queries remain 103/103; runtime height generation remains
unsupported with its warning. `replan.topofar` is closed at 1687/1687. The separate
stacked-link diagnostic fails strictly in 523 values across 18 scenarios and remains a
topology follow-up. Its reference does not replace the main navpath reference or admit
any failures in that comparator.

Remaining work is split by observable cause. Two residuals turn on ties that rounding
decides: walker's desired ray through the vertex where the next edge starts (navmesh,
211 frames) and the `endatlink` link end one float step from the path's end (navlinks
corners of frames 1 to 81). `areamask` desired velocity differs from 102 after a
sub-millimetre trajectory difference from frame 6 (54; a flipped sample choice is a
hypothesis). The three avoidance configurations' second-frame steering target is
Unity's intermediate corner at x = 42.67, which the agent's line to its destination
passes 2e-5 to 7e-5 m away; the cause is open. The others are the `crosstile` approach direction
after the occupied link frees (118); five destination/Warp/disable reports;
baked-agent corners (blocked), arrival (right) and hasPath (left); bent and wide-link
query routes; persistent point-link layer ownership and selective reconnection; bump/ramp
arrival; runtime height generation; and post avoidance. Q401 rejected two candidate
rules that regressed measured cases: merging only exactly equal straight-path points
(no change) and an arrival test against the previous step's straight distance (navlinks
325 -> 501; avoidance arrival, arrival-start and runs each fail 2 configurations). The three climber rounding
records stay in the raw count and the numerical-residual table, without a corrective
constant. Other releases retain the previous unmeasured navigation mechanisms
through Compat. No capture establishes universal parity outside its recorded context.

The Q362/Q300 text work closes textcore (all 616859 values match; 8226 differed):
TextCore glyphs load and rasterize through FreeType per render mode, with the hinted
face lines and the Editor's SDFAA and SDF fields. Textmesh passes 1355/1355 (two
rotation residuals before), its dynamic font texture packed, grown and reset as in
the Editor. Measured on 6000.0.71f1 only. Q407 (tests/projects/textcolor, new: 15 of 19
values match, the other 4 are listed residuals; its panel image matches) adds colour
bitmap glyphs (TextCore colour atlases, UI Toolkit drawing, legacy fonts), the strike of a
bitmap-only face, importer character spacing, a scaled canvas's requests and a legacy
texture full at 4096. Q413 adds 82 generator bounds and packing controls without changing any
of those 19 reference rows. Updated overflow bounds now determine pivot translation and line
alignment; the base-size space is cached before the text. This resolves the direct-generator
placement and subsequent canvas UV differences. The earlier OS fallback warning is also resolved. Open
there as well, unmeasured: colour vector (COLR) faces, several strikes per face, and a full
legacy texture that still holds glyphs of older frames (Unbound evicts them with a reset).
A separately imported bitmap font at its exact 109-point strike is now recorded: its metrics,
UVs, Alpha8 texture and captured coverage pixels match. This adds one data observation while
keeping all 102 preceding reference rows and the panel image unchanged.

The Q349 UI Toolkit work adds uitkvector (new: 69 of 69 values; both panel images within
the limits of its `image_limits.json`): SVG files imported by the `com.unity.vectorgraphics`
2.0.0-preview.25 package's own ScriptedImporter and drawn as backgrounds, `Image` elements
and through `MeshGenerationContext.DrawVectorImage`, where Unbound warned once and drew
nothing before. Uitkpainter matches all 140 of its checks (118 meshes, 19 API and state
lines, 3 images; 133 before) with no open case: the three curve cases without length were
open before, and seven such meshes are added. Uxmlattributes matches 1099 of 1099 values
(1074 before): a list item whose file id names no object of a project ScriptedImporter's
asset is null without a message, as in the Editor. The raw image reports retain the remaining gradient and edge-coverage differences. Q505 adds
panel dynamic atlases; the vector images' mean errors fall from 0.167/0.292 to 0.010405/0.016556
at scales 1/1.25, with unchanged prior limits. Open, in the rows `UI Toolkit
VectorImage drawing and SVG import` and `UI Toolkit runtime stubs`: the 2D Sprite package's
sprite editor window registration (a warning stub; the importer's sprite and texture outputs are
done, see below) and, unmeasured, arc pieces of radius above 0
whose end tangents oppose and pieces cut at the subdivision depth limit. Measured on
6000.0.71f1 only. Q413 confirms UXML VectorImage references and retained vector masks in
73 values and eleven images. Q505 expands this to 83 values and 28 images: point/bilinear
pages, padded row allocation, growth, shared users, removal/reuse, dirty/reset transitions,
custom rejection, shared source resizing and raster-font glyph additions. The measured release
uses these through Compat; other releases keep direct sampling, with unmeasured seams and
texture lifetime. Captures and the remaining small coverage residuals were measured on NVIDIA only.

Incompatible serialized reference types into a single-GameObject scripted asset remain
queued. Their requested object-wrapper identity needs a broader compatibility rule.

The Q93, Q380, Q412 and Q427 sprite work: spriteimport passes 650 of 650 fields over 65 sprites
(519 of 520 before; a Polygon-mode sprite is always Tight and takes its mesh's boundary as its
default physics shape, with thirteen Polygon controls). Spriteassets matches 120,888 values
(46,318 before): the Sprite layouts the Editor saves load (clones of SpriteAtlas members, read
unpacked from their own render data; skinned clones; colour, normal and tangent channels,
colours drawn; secondary textures; vertex data version 2; Sprites saved without a texture, not
drawn). Svgsprites matches 108,891 values (four pixels at a radial gradient's focus listed as a
residual): the SVG importer's vector sprite, UI SVGImage, textured sprite and Texture2D outputs,
run by the package's own code, imported sprite IDs and the generated physics shape (row
`UI Toolkit VectorImage drawing and SVG import`). Spriteatlas is new (Sprite Atlas V1 1,306,495,
V2 444,109 and oversize 46,607 values, zero differences; the page textures' name hashes listed as
a residual): SpriteAtlas packing in Editor Play mode, with `unbound doctor` and a one-time warning
for an Edit-mode session (row `SpriteAtlas packing in Editor Play mode`). Spriteoutlines is new
(12,059 values, zero differences): runtime `Sprite.Create` Tight meshes and fallback physics
shapes, also from texels a script wrote, PolygonCollider2D and TilemapCollider2D outlines of
sprites without a physics shape, `SpriteUtility.GenerateOutline` and `Sprite.OverridePhysicsShape`,
`SpriteUtility.GenerateOutlineFromSprite`, colliders fitting a renderer's bounds when added (also
flipped) and `PolygonCollider2D.CreatePrimitive` (row `SpriteMeshType.Tight` outline). Saved
Sprites now also load scriptable objects, texture coordinates 1 to 7, their own texture
coordinates 0 and clones of variant and split-alpha atlas members; packed, rotated, split-alpha or
downscaled m_RD and legacy Sprite Packer atlas names stay rejected, with the reason (the Editor
never saves them; row YAML Sprite documents). Still open, with their rows: the SpriteAtlas cases
its row lists as not reproduced (with warnings); the SVGImage drawn on a canvas. Measured on
6000.0.71f1 with the NVIDIA driver only, except spriteoutlines (macOS Editor, Metal; equal to its
earlier Linux reference in every value it had).

Seen beside these, tracked elsewhere and not classified again: coreapis, humanfirstpose,
humanoid, indirectspec, modelimport, particles and shaderkeywords have open tasks, deferred
rows or residual entries. Q368 audits the two previously unclassified probes below.
Lightprobes retains 250 of 625 strict failures after Q381: 228 numeric-only keys and 22
functional keys covering combined-mesh indices/counts and occlusion at probe positions. The
numeric-only keys have mixed signs and a maximum absolute component delta of
1.1920928955078125e-7. The functional keys are not precision residuals. The light-probe gap row
and README record the remaining boundary-cell and mesh scope. Lines now matches 2200/2201:
Q374 resolved the 15 trail-time values, leaving one perceptual-gradient colour byte, reference
0.07058824 versus 0.07450981. Its geometry, images and all other keys match in this fixture;
that colour delta remains strict and belongs to the existing perceptual-gradient follow-up.

### Order and text Unity's documentation leaves open

- **Order between GameObjects.** Manual 6000.0, *Order of execution for event functions*,
  Limitations: "In general, you can't rely on the order in which the same event function is invoked
  for different GameObjects, except when the order is explicitly documented or settable", and the
  `Update` of one MonoBehaviour "might be called before or after the Update function for the same
  MonoBehaviour on another GameObject, including its own parent or child GameObjects". The
  activationorder keys, the 24 editorcallbacks events and 59 oroorder lines are such orders
  (`OnRenderObject`'s page says only that it "is called after camera has rendered the Scene", on
  "any object that has a script with the function"). In each of them the two sides hold the same
  events with the same payloads; each object's own Awake, OnEnable, Start sequence and the
  configurable script execution order are kept. What is not shown: that no game depends on the
  Editor's order. A game that does would behave differently, as it could between two Editor
  releases; the raw sequences stay in the reports.
- **Exception message text.** The exceptions are of the documented type. Game code reads an
  argument exception's parameter name on a second line (`Parameter name: name`) as in the Editor
  (row "Argument exception texts"); one `NullReferenceException` message the runtime raises ends
  with a full stop only in Unbound. No Unity page documents the text. A game that parses it would
  see the difference.

### Probe and reference repairs

- **Recording mode, Q363.** `destroycontract`, `activationorder` and `shaderpasscount`
  now use two identical windowed GPU Editor captures from the existing display. The
  [Unity 6000.0 EOF limitation](https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.0/Documentation/ScriptReference/WaitForEndOfFrame.html)
  excludes batch-mode execution. Their READMEs state every changed row scope.
  Destroycontract still fails 887 keys because first-render/EOF startup differs in
  this fixture; re-recording did not establish engine parity.
- **Numeric recorder, Q363.** Oroorder now names only explicitly formatted instance
  IDs. Two Editor repeats preserve every reference line. Its 23 corrupted numeric
  payload pairs are resolved; the other 91 missing/extra pairs remain raw.
- **TextCore recipe and counter, Q369.** The font recipe already copies and verifies
  both fonts, so all 372 cases run. The suite now reads `N of M values differ` and
  counts the existing 8226 failures. S19 stored zero counted failures because its
  parser missed that form, and its missing-font recipe compared no cases. A replay
  against that unchanged baseline therefore flags the visibility increase. It is
  not waived and no baseline is promoted here.
- **Wind clock, Q363.** A fixed capture interval replaces wall-time-dependent
  phases. All 18 records match, with the same raw comparator and tolerances.
- **Work-area sampling, Q296.** Inputplatform waits up to ten seconds for both
  changed and restored work areas. A 30-frame delayed control passes 11/11; the
  ten-frame sampler fails that control at `workArea.changed`. The normal control
  passes 43/43 and the undelayed work-area control 11/11. All five Editor reference
  files remain byte-identical.
- **Stopwatch units, Q248.** The new Linux Editor reference has 12 records from two
  identical windowed GPU captures, including an escaping readonly field reference.
  The original runtime matches 6/12, the corrected one 12/12. Engine-internal timer
  units are unchanged; the game-assembly members use the measured 100 ns unit.

## How gaps hide

Game runs keep hitting features that are missing *without a warning* (physical cameras,
`Camera.SetReplacementShader`). The generated stubs warn on first use; these do not:

| Pattern | Example | Why it is silent |
|---|---|---|
| Hand-written empty body | `Camera.SetReplacementShader { }`, `Animator.ApplyBuiltinRootMotion` | A hand-written member suppresses the generated stub, and with it the warning (516 empty public bodies in the hand-written sources, many legitimate) |
| Stored, never consumed | `Physics2D.simulationLayers` | Auto-properties and fields the frame/simulation never reads |
| Serialized field or import setting not read | `m_DisabledShaderPasses`, `m_RenderingPath`, terrain lightmap, ModelImporter clip masks/root motion, `m_ContactPairsMode` | `ReadSerialized` / the importers read a subset of keys and drop the rest |
| Message looked up, never sent | done: `Messages.cs` `s_NotSent` (empty since OnParticleUpdateJobScheduled is sent) warns once per script class for a listed message | Messages are not API calls: `api-gaps` cannot see them |
| Overload drops a parameter | `Graphics.ConvertTexture(src, srcElement, dst, dstElement)`, `Mesh.Set*(..., flags)`, stereo-eye overloads (Blit slices, `LogOption`, `SetPropertyBlock` index done) | Forwarded to the simpler overload |
| Asset class with a ClassId but no reader | done (`classids`): a class without a reader warns once at load, naming the class and the file, and `unbound doctor` lists the project's files holding one | Ignored classes (`ClassCoverage.s_Ignored`, with a reason) stay silent |
| Stub default not neutral | done (`stubdefaults`): value-returning stubs take the Editor's answer from `StubDefaults.cs` where it is a constant; `ScalableBufferManager` and `CalculateObliqueMatrix` are hand-written, `Microphone.devices` takes the generator's empty-array rule | Members whose answer is not a constant still return the default after their warning (see the tooling item) |
| Generated stub events | done: subscribing warns that the event is never raised | |
| Claimed capability | done for the measured NVIDIA OpenGL Core and Vulkan `SystemInfo.supportsTextureWrapMirrorOnce` cases: native mirror-clamp is used where supported | Other driver capability profiles remain unmeasured |
| Warn once, then silent | SpriteMask, reflection probes | A second scene shows nothing in the log |

## Tooling first: make every gap loud

Each of these turns a class of silent gaps into a list that keeps itself up to date:

- [x] Q403 declaration checking: `unbound api-check` compares release metadata, including custom
      attribute arguments, AttributeUsage, optional defaults, constants, modifiers, constraints,
      interfaces, accessors and storage metadata. See [declaration policy](API_DECLARATIONS.md).
      The current 6000.0.71f1 audit has 407 exact migration exceptions in
      `managed/UnityEngine/ApiCheckAllowList.json`: attributes 246, modifiers 13, accessors 4,
      layout 68, interfaces 64, constraints 1 and eleven lookup policies. These remain open
      declaration repairs, with raw counts and individual reasons retained by the checker.
      Q403 lookup checks are done (`attributeinheritance`, 215 exact Editor records): thirty
      attribute-type declarations now match the release metadata; eleven direct-discovery lookup
      policies carry individual measured reasons. TypeCache, editor registration and JSON rename/
      envelope controls match the Editor; serialized prefab renames remain supported.

ParticleSystem resolves 337 more, Editor resolves 132 and UIElements resolves 61 more across its values and shared JSON string transfer.       Global warm-up still prepares every source SubShader of a known shader, and a fallback without
      source ends its chain as in drawing; a collection variant of a known shader that draws no
      SubShader under the current pipeline and maximum LOD is skipped with a warning (unmeasured).

## The map

**S** = silent (no warning), **W** = warns (stub or "skipped"), impact **H**/M/L on real games.

### Core runtime and messages

Done: lifecycle and execution order, all `RuntimeInitializeLoadType`s, `[InitializeOnLoad*]`,
coroutines (measured timing), SendMessage family, parent/children/canvas messages, Transform API,
Instantiate, scenes (single/additive/async, events, merge/move), serialization (SerializeReference,
callbacks, FormerlySerializedAs), prefab variants/nesting/overrides, Time, PlayerPrefs.

| Gap | Where | | Impact |
|---|---|---|---|
| LinkedPool used ObjectPool storage and derived its count, omitting the selected private counter | `Platform/Pool.cs` | S | done for Unity 6000.0.71f1 (`tests/projects/linkedpool`, 161 exact controls): independent linked storage/counter, capacity, null/reference duplicate checks, reused nodes and callback partial state; ObjectPool behavior remains independently controlled |
| `OnMouseDown/Up/UpAsButton/Enter/Over/Exit/Drag` never sent | `Core/PlayerLoop.cs:110` (`SendMouseEvents` is an empty marker) | S | done (Unity's SendMouseEvents algorithm: tests/projects/messages) |
| `OnApplicationFocus` / `OnApplicationPause` never sent (Editor sends focus(true) at Play start) | `Runtime/Messages.cs:79,90`, `Core/Application.cs:65` | S | done (tests/projects/messages) |
| `OnBeforeTransformParentChanged` never sent | `Runtime/Components.cs:203-240` | S | done (tests/projects/messages) |
| FixedUpdate capped at 8 steps, leftover time dropped | `Runtime/PlayerLoopDriver.cs:316-330` | S | done (and the first step at fixed time 0: tests/projects/messages) |
| `Time.renderedFrameCount` phase during camera rendering, GUI and EOF | `Runtime/PlayerLoopDriver.cs` | S | measured scope admitted (tests/projects/coreapis w4: all 313 window reads now match the counter value; it advances once on rendered and skipped frames, after coroutine EOF at GUIClearEvents. Matching validation resolves 238 lines with no lost matches; ORO order remains separate. Unity's exact node, custom loops without GUIClearEvents, paused/stepped/Edit frames, other intervening readers and absolute startup values remain unmeasured.) |
| `OnRenderObject` initial handler order | `Runtime/Loading.cs`, scene activation; `Runtime/Lifecycle.cs` | S | Deferred order-only differences for Unity 6000.0.71f1: the strict `oroorder` pair establishes stable removal and append on re-enable for one handler class; `editorcallbacks/SceneLoop` adds prefab roots, hierarchy reordering, re-enable and runtime AddComponent controls. Initial registration differs from public hierarchy order in the Editor. Both APIs leave receiver order unspecified; raw sequences and all existing comparator failures remain visible. No global wake or rendering sort is introduced. Configurable script execution order is preserved. Cross-type render registration, hot reload and SRP remain unmeasured. |
| `Quaternion.Dot` and `Normalize` sum at double precision | `Math/Quaternion.cs`, `Math/QuaternionMath.cs` | S | done for 6000.0.71f1 (tests/projects/quaternioneuler: 7299/7300 exact; only the original vector-rotation residual). Public Dot sums double products once to float; Normalize rounds the length before dividing. New controls distinguish the rounded equality threshold, Angle near 0/180 and NaN, zero/subnormal/nonfinite normalization, and normalized-result APIs. Axis conversion uses measured float intermediates. One shared internal helper retains engine arithmetic; animculling retains its four original residual keys/values. Per-caller evidence and unmeasured branches are in the probe README: navigation/carving thresholds, animation blend signs/raw stream inputs/target matching, transform and rigidbody raw rotations, query orientations/pose sync, random rotations, rotated text, canvas parenting and UI Toolkit style conversion. These remain unmeasured and unchanged, rather than being assigned the script arithmetic without evidence. Other releases retain the previous unmeasured arithmetic through Compat |
| Quaternion factory float bits | `Math/Quaternion.cs` | S | open: exploratory 6000.0.71f1 quaternioneuler captures found signed-zero differences in Euler controls 16, 17, 19, 20, 21, 24, 35 and 38, and AngleAxis component rounding differences: one float step in a small-angle sine, and about 1.19e-7 absolute in the near-180-degree cosine, which moves the reported Angle by one float step. The existing decimal factory controls do not distinguish signed zero. Fixed-input Dot/Normalize/Angle bit controls deliberately isolate their API inputs; factory arithmetic, its engine callers and a canonical factory-bit extension are a separate follow-up |
| Queued `LoadSceneAsync` staging | `Core/SceneManagement.cs`, `Runtime/Loading.cs` | S | M, open (tests/projects/editorcallbacks, unchanged scene-queue profile: 0 of 9 streams match; 64 lines only in the Editor, 44 only in Unbound). The canonical two-load capture completes the second load at step 4, Unbound at step 3. A later pair of windowed Linux Editor captures (2026-11-17, byte-identical to each other) completes it at step 3 too; the reference is kept as recorded. Fresh three-load Editor repeats complete at steps 2/4/6 and 2/3/4, so a fixed extra frame would contradict a measured control. Completion timing is wall-clock dependent, not a fixed frame contract. The existing implementation preserves the measured equal-priority FIFO completion, progress and activation hold; exact timing remains open, with all strict failures visible. Unity 6000.0 documents a shared main-thread integration budget for background loading, but Application.backgroundLoadingPriority has no effect in the Editor. AsyncOperation.priority can reorder work that has not started, so FIFO is only the measured equal-priority scope; non-default priorities remain unmeasured and unsupported by this queue. The added queue-three profile checks FIFO completion, completed progress, loaded scenes and roots, with raw times retained separately. Existing strict queue failures remain visible. Single, additive, forced and starts profiles match |
| `sceneUnloaded` when the Editor quits from Play mode | `Core/SceneManagement.cs`, `Editor/EditorSession.cs`, `Runtime/PlayerLoopDriver.cs` | S | done for Unity 6000.0.71f1 (tests/projects/editorcallbacks main record and quit controls). Direct EditorApplication.Exit in Play mode destroys loaded-scene and persistent roots, then emits sceneUnloaded with valid, unloaded scenes while isPlaying remains true; it omits script OnApplicationQuit. Stopping Play sends OnApplicationQuit, including to disabled scripts on active objects, before disabling/destroying the objects; it emits no sceneUnloaded. The default and disabled-domain-reload controls match. The direct-exit throwing-unload control still closes both scenes and exits normally, skipping later subscribers in each throwing invocation. Two loaded scenes, a persistent root and inactive scripts discriminate the rules without imposing cross-object order. Initial untitled-scene ownership was closed by Q372. Other quit entry points and scene changes requested during teardown remain unmeasured |

| A coroutine waiting for an AsyncOperation resumes at the next Update's coroutine point | `Runtime/Coroutines.cs` (Wait, Resume), `Core/ScriptableObject.cs` (AsyncOperation.Complete) | S | done for measured Unity 6000.0.71f1 cases (tests/projects/navbaked `resume.*`: LoadSceneAsync additive, held by allowSceneActivation, UnloadSceneAsync, Resources.LoadAsync, InstantiateAsync; managed CoroutineTests). Documented (Scripting API 6000.0, AsyncOperation): it can be yielded from a coroutine; when the coroutine resumes is not documented. Measured: the first coroutine that yielded a running operation resumes as the operation completes (in EarlyUpdate.UpdatePreloading for scene loads and unloads and Resources loads, UpdateAsyncInstantiate for InstantiateAsync), after sceneLoaded and before the completed handlers, so before the frame's early AI update; another coroutine yielding the same operation, and one yielding an operation already done, resumes at the next Update's coroutine point. Applies to every release; only 6000.0.71f1 is measured. Scene-load staging is now measured by `editorcallbacks/SceneLoop`: `Compat.SceneAsyncPreparationMeasured` separates document preparation from activation, serializes queued integrations, holds the FIFO at 0.9, and preserves preloading completion and coroutine resumption. The measured small first/later single and additive loads complete in frame 2; held loads activate after release, and synchronous loads force their pending prefix in the next frame. This is not a duration guarantee for arbitrary content. Other releases retain the previous one-pass timing, unmeasured there. |

| `WaitForSeconds`, `Awaitable.WaitForSecondsAsync` and `Invoke` come due at the wrong point (a zero wait started during Update resumed in that frame, before the next frame's fixed steps; an Invoke ran only after Update) | `Runtime/Coroutines.cs` (FrameTimedWait, Invokes), `Runtime/Awaitable.cs`, `Runtime/PlayerLoopDriver.cs` | S | done for measured Unity 6000.0.71f1 cases (tests/projects/waitforseconds: 75 values, two identical windowed GPU Editor captures; managed TimedWaitTests). Documented (Scripting API 6000.0, WaitForSeconds): the coroutine continues "on the first frame after 't' seconds has passed". Measured: both timed waits count from the time of the frame that starts them (also from a fixed step, where Time.time reads the fixed clock) and end at the first Update coroutine point of a later frame whose time has reached the end, so a zero or negative wait never ends in its own frame; a long frame does not delay the start (the page's "start waiting at the end of the current frame" is not what a 250 ms frame shows). Invoke timing is additionally measured by tests/projects/invokesteps: the first deadline uses the earlier of the fixed and frame clocks, the fixed-step check reads that step's clock, and checks also run after Update and LateUpdate. A single late repeat runs once per check and keeps its original cadence; deadline-ordered traversal can revisit interleaved repeats. A one-shot is removed before its callback; cancelled neighbors are excluded from subsequent traversal, while cancellation inside a callback can expose an already-reinserted repeat. NaN deadlines remain pending without callbacks (22 values in invokesteps, two identical captures). These additional rules use Compat for 6000.0.71f1; other releases retain the previous, unmeasured clock and delayed points. Deferred: a delay that ends exactly on a frame boundary (the Editor resumed 1 s at 0.125 s per frame after 8 frames in one capture and 9 in the next). Unmeasured: timeScale changes during a wait, custom player loops without these points. The timed-wait frame policy applies to every release; only 6000.0.71f1 is measured. |

Unbound copies the particles into per-system arrays reused from frame to frame when the system's first job starts (as Unity's job sees the system as it is then: a Clear, Clear(true) or Stop with clearing of a parent, or an Emit in the messages before it runs, reach the job; none of them runs or waits for it), runs the jobs right after the messages and copies back: a read in the message sees the particles before the job, scripts after the messages see its writes (in Unity a LateUpdate read without waiting saw the particles before the job in some probe versions and after it in another: unmeasured whether from timing or from a read waiting for the job batch); a script's read or write in a message takes in a job of that system that has run, and a write first finishes one that has started; custom data holds a job's writes until the module's next run. ### Built-in render pipeline

Done: forward base/add lighting, vertex/SH lights, shadows (cascades, spot, point; casters from
fallback shaders), camera matrices and physical cameras, clear flags, MSAA, command buffers at
every forward event, OnPre*/OnPost*/OnRenderImage and [ImageEffectOpaque], depth, depth-normals
and motion-vector textures, sorting layers, SortingGroup and transparency sort axes, fog, ambient
modes, lightmaps and light probes, custom, baked and realtime reflection probes with blending/box
projection, keywords, GrabPass, LODGroup, SubShader LOD, disabled passes, `.renderTexture`
assets, Display 1 only, projectors, replacement shaders, oblique mirrors with inverted culling,
cubemap renders, camera skyboxes, deferred and vertex-lit paths, material variants, raster
states (tests/projects/builtinrp, builtinfx, display, baked, lighting).

| Lens flare element edge line | `Graphics/Halos.cs` | S | done for Unity 6000.0.71f1 (tests/projects/flares, OpenGL Core, NVIDIA: every image without a halo is exact, the `f.*`, `q.*`, `o.*` and the new `fl.*` cases; the `h.*` and `ho.*` images differ only in their halo pixels, by the open halo-image entry in the cause table above; managed FlareGeometryTests). The cause was the construction, not a rasterization rule: Unbound built the elements in viewport pixels with fitted constants (a scale of 0.0100354, image insets by the part of the texture outside an image). They are now quads in view space at the flare's depth, half `size * 0.01` of that depth, a zooming element also times the brightness and visibility, on the line from the flare's point to the view axis, a rotating element 1.4 half sides out with its first corner away from the axis, a directional flare at twice the near plane's distance, each image its cell less half a texel (the large and medium images of the second layout keep their left and top sides whole), with the sums in the one order that reproduces the reference images to the last float bit; the camera's projection alone places them. Documented (Manual 6000.0, Flare asset reference, https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.0/Documentation/Manual/class-Flare.html): an element's Position runs "along a line running from the containing GameObject's position through the screen center", and Rotate turns it towards the centre of the screen. Measured with the `fl.*` cases, and changed by this from what Unbound drew before: under an off-centre projection (a lens shift of a quarter of the width) the Editor's elements follow the view axis, not the screen's centre, so there the documented wording does not hold (one horizontal shift is measured; a vertical one and a script-set off-centre or oblique projection are not); a zooming element is half and twice as large at brightness (or a light's flareStrength) 0.5 and 2; a flare beyond the far plane or nearer than the near plane is not drawn; a directional flare 50 degrees off the view axis is drawn and one 70 degrees off is not. Not measured: the angle at which a directional flare leaves the view (60 degrees in Unbound, from its placement) and its depth under a script-set projection (the camera's near plane is used); whether a flare that is not drawn for these reasons keeps fading in (it does in Unbound); an orthographic camera with a directional flare (not drawn, as before); an element of negative size or behind an orthographic camera (not drawn); the distance from the view axis under which a rotating element stops turning (1e-5 in Unbound; on the axis it points along +x as measured); flares under a render pipeline; drivers other than NVIDIA's |

Not measured, Unbound's reading: every constructor and a descriptor given to a texture mark it, a texture loaded from an asset is not marked, and a clone is marked as its source is. The descriptor's `colorFormat` setter takes the format in the descriptor's own encoding (a linear descriptor stays linear in a Linear project), as the device renders it, and sets `shadowSamplingMode` to compare for a `Shadowmap` and to none for any other format, as Unity's own managed descriptor does; `RenderTexture.format` keeps its own rule (the format as it is, without the device fallback, a `Shadowmap` turns comparing on and nothing turns it off, a depth-only format without a depth buffer gets 16 bits with Unity's warning). Not modelled: the descriptor setter's reset of the depth-stencil format to the one its depth bits and sampling mode give (managed RenderTextureDescriptorColorFormatTests; not measured in an Editor capture) |

Unbound's choices, not measured: the kept views end when the texture is reinitialized or destroyed (the probe reads views after `Reinitialize` and destruction of readable textures only), and they read the bytes they had, which are the unreadable texture's raw data, so a write through one shows in `GetRawTextureData()` until an engine copy into the texture replaces that data (one array for an uncompressed texture; a block-compressed texture's views are its blocks). | `LODGroup` transition distance | `Graphics/LODGroup.cs` | S | done for Unity 6000.0.71f1 for the level rule (tests/projects/lod, all 17 records; managed LodGroupLevelTests). Fixed (Q510): on the OpenGL Core device, in most runs on a loaded machine, one to three of the probe's 15,600 `Camera.Render` + `ReadPixels` samples read wrong, never the same sample twice. In those samples a quadrant of the 9x9 target, with a corner at its centre, held transparent zeros or another image. The window present on Bevy's render thread now waits for its copy before it releases the OpenGL context that the game thread also renders with (vendor/wgpu-sampler/PATCHES.md `gl-present-wait.patch`). With this wait the wrong samples are gone (measured: 0 in 6 runs). Inferred, not measured: the present overlaps the game thread's rendering on the shared context. This is measured on the NVIDIA 580 driver only. Which driver operation overlaps is not known. Other drivers are unmeasured. No wrong sample was seen in 4 runs on the Vulkan device. `LOD_PROBE_REPEAT` repeats each sample's render to look for this under load (README). A camera draws the first level from `QualitySettings.maximumLODLevel` on whose reach, the group's world size over the level's transition height, is more than the camera's distance to the reference point times 2 tan(fov / 2) / `lodBias` (orthographic: twice the size over the bias, at any distance); at equality the next level is drawn. Documented: the transition height is screen-relative in [0, 1] and `lodBias` multiplies the switching distance (Scripting API 6000.0 [LOD.screenRelativeTransitionHeight](https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.0/Documentation/ScriptReference/LOD-screenRelativeTransitionHeight.html), [QualitySettings.lodBias](https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.0/Documentation/ScriptReference/QualitySettings-lodBias.html)); the tie rule is measured. Unbound's choices, not measured: a group of size 0 and a level of height 0, or `lodBias` 0 with the camera at the reference point, draw nothing. Not modelled (measured on one machine only, probe `run.py editor --hold`, not in the reference): in the Editor a camera rendered again within about 1 ms of real time can draw the levels chosen for its previous place (a 0.5 ms wait drew the old level, 0.75 ms the new one); Unbound chooses at every render. The previous reference was a batch-mode capture with renders that close; windowed captures give the same records with or without the wait. Cross-fades are not drawn (deferred, see below) |

| Stacked screen cameras that clear Depth only or nothing, through HDR/MSAA intermediates | `Graphics/FrameBuilder.cs`, `crates/unbound-app/src/render/executor.rs` | S | done for measured screen paths (tests/projects/camerastack, Linear and Gamma, 64 cases x 2 frames each pass pixel for pixel): the screen keeps its color and depth from frame to frame; consecutive full-screen cameras that keep the color share one intermediate (HDR when any of them is, the most samples any asks for) and its depth; a Don't Clear full-screen intermediate starts from the screen's depth (`CopyDepth`, render protocol 9); a full-screen resolve leaves the screen without depth; partial cameras take a pixel-size MSAA intermediate and start Don't Clear with cleared depth. Unmeasured, chosen: a script's Camera.Render() and any drawing on the screen after a stack's resolve (late command buffers, blits, GL) end the stack; deferred cameras after an HDR stack and on explicit targets (whole target) seed their light buffer by the same rule; a full-screen camera keeping the color after a deferred one starts a stack of its own with its own samples; a deferred camera with image effects drops the stack's MSAA as one without. Explicit-target Don't Clear depth is done for initialized retained targets in sequential disabled Camera.Render calls, built-in forward, full rect without effects (`cameraexplicitdepth`, Unity 6000.0.71f1 Linux OpenGL/NVIDIA, Linear/Gamma, 254/254 values and 210/210 images per space): direct byte/half-float targets retain their own depth; HDR-to-byte renders start with fresh intermediate depth independently of unrelated near/far history, and color resolve preserves the owned target's prior depth, with quality/target samples 1/4. Native's clear-versus-replace mechanism is unresolved. Also measured (camerastack, Linear and Gamma, 96 cases x 2 frames each pass pixel for pixel): a color-clearing camera inside a screen stack (full and partial, LDR and HDR, quality AA 0 and 4), a partial camera between full-screen cameras (A LDR and HDR), a perspective deferred stacked camera at quality AA 0 and 4 (`.persp`; the orthographic `.def` cases render Forward): a deferred camera that clears only depth or nothing starts its light buffer from the color under it, a partial screen one renders at its pixel size, and a full-screen one continuing a screen stack makes that stack render without MSAA. Not a parity target: the contents a Don't Clear camera finds in a new, discarded or pooled target, which Unity 6000.0 documents as undefined ([RenderTexture.Create](https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.0/Documentation/ScriptReference/RenderTexture.Create.html), [DiscardContents](https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.0/Documentation/ScriptReference/RenderTexture.DiscardContents.html), [GetTemporary](https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.0/Documentation/ScriptReference/RenderTexture.GetTemporary.html)). Unmeasured, chosen: a screen read between a frame's start and its first screen pass (an offscreen camera's OnPostRender) reads the last frame, as that pass carries it forward. Unmeasured: explicit-target deferred/partial/effect depth history. On Metal (Q564, tests/projects/camerastack `metal/<space>`, the macOS Editor's Game view on an Apple M2 Pro): in Linear color space a partial camera's HDR intermediate that keeps the color under it starts from the screen's stored bytes read as linear values (`GraphicsDeviceProfile.PartialScreenSeedUndecoded`; unmeasured for explicit targets), and a Don't Clear deferred camera over the screen takes the screen's depth rows the right way up (CopyDepth between a top-down screen and a texture mirrors them; Vulkan's screen is top-down too and takes the same rule, unmeasured there); Unbound 192/192 images in both color spaces. Unmeasured: a partial deferred camera without HDR (Unbound's light buffer is half float, so it takes the undecoded seed too) |

Its C++ interface and the plugins' handlers are laid out as the Itanium C++ ABI does; the callbacks run without the plugin context's lock, so they may call back into the engine; a custom player loop without those two nodes gets neither (unmeasured). Residuals (`residuals.json`): the Editor's own markers, worker threads and job flows are not modelled (`profiler.editor`). All 15 earlier Vulkan stencil content differences close; 209 added publication controls and 174 readback controls per Vulkan profile match. Two DXT sRGB mip rows retain strict nine-digit decimal midpoint formatting differences that round to the same float bits. The 42 original OpenGL residuals are unchanged; 63 of the 64 original Vulkan residuals are closed by actual attachment allocation capabilities and sampled-usage selection, with no reference or tolerance changes; six new OpenGL multisample compute rows retain preceding graphics bindings instead of the requested resources, confirmed with alternating graphics color/depth reads and isolated kernels. This OpenGL compute history remains a queue item. Suppression of retained single-sample color publication for bindTextureMS remains unverified and queued; the new direct-sample reads bypass that retained image and therefore do not establish this guarantee. The OpenGL stencil profile retains its raw callback-error rows under the existing measured error category. Vulkan resolve-image access returns the actual multisample image with bindTextureMS and the separate single-sample image without it; this does not expose a hidden retained image in bound mode. On Unity 6000.0.71f1, Compat.RenderingMeasured also selects bindTextureMS setter refusal on created storage, quiet same-value assignments, changes after release or failed creation, and 2D/2D-array dimension validation. The setter diagnostics are measured behavior rather than a documented guarantee; other releases retain the earlier unverified setter policy. Open: the Game view of a windowed Editor as a plugin target is not measured, and Unbound's screen is not shared with plugins (logged); texture updates of other formats fail when recorded (compressed storage is not written, logged); foreign wrappers with incompatible storage metadata remain unsupported; measured known-owner smaller-size aliases retain physical dimensions and mip levels as described above; a callback while a slice of a 3D render texture is the target finds that slice attached (unmeasured); a callback while a target whose mip or slice is out of range is set finds no target (logged; unmeasured); an external texture declared with more levels than the plugin's texture has samples the levels there are (unmeasured); everything in this row is measured on the NVIDIA driver only, in the Editor and here (other drivers: unmeasured, nothing is assumed of them); on macOS the device is Metal (Q423) and plugins get `IUnityGraphicsMetal`, `V1` and `V2` (`crates/unbound-app/src/render/executor/plugins_metal.rs`; tests/projects/nativegfx `--device metal`, `Reference/unity-6000.0.71f1/metal.jsonl`, two identical macOS Editor captures, arm64; plugins load from `.dylib` files with the importer settings OS OSX and CPU ARM64): `GetRenderer` 16, the renderer's own Metal device and queue, Metal's bundle; a callback gets a command buffer of that queue, committed after it between the work before and after, a render encoder begun on request over the current target (a render texture's attachments, their contents loaded, a multisampled color resolved again; else the screen's color texture and depth buffer, loaded; store actions left unknown in the descriptor and set when the encoder ends, as the Editor's: Q475) with its descriptor (a new one for each encoder, so a plugin's change to one it was given reaches no encoder of Unbound's; as the Editor answers, the descriptor is the current encoder's: there is one where something was drawn into the target since it was set or a plugin's encoder was left begun, in a later event too, none after only a clear or once the plugin ended the encoder; over the screen there is one until the plugin ends it), `EndCurrentCommandEncoder` (whatever the target, in a callback and from scripts, the next encoder the plugin asks for is over the screen, its contents not loaded, and lasts there into later events until Unbound draws or sets a target: unmeasured beyond the probe's events), `CommitCurrentCommandBuffer` (a new current buffer, the encoder ended with it, so no descriptor until one is asked for again: unmeasured; the next encoder is over the same target; from scripts too, where the current buffer is one of the queue's, committed by it or before the next callback or at the end of the frame, so before the rendering the frame has not submitted yet: unmeasured, and the current encoder is over the screen of the latest frame or callback: which frame's image a script's draw reaches is unmeasured); native pointers are `MTLTexture`s as Unity's device keeps them (sRGB in Linear projects, cubes as cubes, a multisampled render texture's samples, a depth-only target's depth), render buffer pointers give their texture, a multisampled color's resolved texture and no stencil texture, and `CreateExternalTexture` shows a plugin's own `MTLTexture` (2D, cube, 3D; 8-bit, half and float color formats; others fail with a message) or another texture's storage: 339/386 records match with 47 declared residuals (the dynamic resolution controls in the Graphics devices row; the four `outside.*` rows since Q515); a screen camera's stand-in is stored as the screen is (BGRA8Unorm), so a callback in its command buffer finds the format the batch-mode Editor's screen has (80; the windowed Editor's Game view is BGRA8Unorm_sRGB at the window's size, unmodelled), and ReadPixels inside such a camera reads the stand-in as the screen (residuals: its bytes are sRGB-encoded where the batch-mode Editor's screen is not, and a blit of its target onto itself samples white in the Editor, the camera's image in Unbound); a texture updated with `Apply` just before an event is copied with its earlier texels, the update running after the plugin's buffer, as in the Editor; the screen's color texture is BGRA8Unorm as the Editor's (Q491: grab textures of it share its storage, readbacks swizzle, copies between it (or its grabs) and RGBA8 textures keep each channel) (`residuals-metal.json`: texture memory modes, the descriptor's load action over a render texture (the Editor's is its clearing pass's), and a Blit from a screen camera's target 1/255 apart in green (the batch-mode screen is not sRGB-encoded); `CommandBuffer.CopyTexture` naming `CameraTarget`, outside cameras and of a screen camera, copies nothing and logs the null source or destination error, as the macOS Editor does in batch mode and windowed in Play mode's Game view (Q506, `screencopy.*`, `screencamera.*`; applied on every device, OpenGL Core and Vulkan unmeasured, queued with Q515; `metal-windowed.jsonl`: the windowed Editor's rows other than the plugin's, two identical captures, whose screen is the Game view's BGRA8Unorm_sRGB texture); a macOS-only check, not in the Linux gate, run at the Editor's batchmode screen size, 64x64; an HDR (EDR) screen, foveation and Intel Macs are unmeasured). The OpenGL plugin gets no context on macOS. Closed without work: handing image layouts between Vulkan and OpenGL with semaphores (the graphics devices row: no image crosses between the two since each device has its own backend) |

A fractional render scale sizes the target as URP's C# does in Mono's double precision (row below); STP (Q172, https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.0/Documentation/Manual/urp/stp/stp-upscaler.html): its compute kernels run (`upscale_stp` from every pixel wrong to a mean of 0.53, 357 pixels over 8 along silhouette edges, open: Q227 found the horizontal edges biased, Unbound's leaning toward the lower side; negating STP's x or y jitter makes it worse, 689 and 1037 pixels). Q227's time-boxed follow-up (2026-11-15) recorded the residual and queued it. 263 of the 357 pixels lie on horizontal edges. `Time.frameCount` is 27 at the capture in the Editor and in Unbound. With the default offset, URP takes STP's jitter from it, so the jitter's frame index is the same (inferred). A uniform vertical shift of the Editor's image, by a whole pixel or by 0.1 to 0.3 of one, fits worse. Unmeasured: STP's other inputs (`deltaTime`, the history and the previous frames' matrices) and the frames before the capture. Queued: compare STP's passes one by one against an Editor capture of its intermediate targets. The residual is small (mean 0.53) and stays at anti-aliased silhouette edges. STP's other parts: the HLSL front end now reads namespaces, member functions declared in their struct and defined outside it, and member calls needing an argument conversion (core's Threading library); infinite float constants (STP's sign tests) become the largest finite floats, which WGSL can hold (`isinf` of such a constant differs); kernel samplers that gather integer textures are bound unfiltered. Skinned meshes (Q174, https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.0/Documentation/Manual/urp/features/motion-vectors.html): on the Editor's OpenGL Core device a bone-skinned mesh moves as its object matrix does, its actual root bone's (rootBone, else the renderer's transform) position and rotation without scale, and its bones' deformation adds no motion, with skinnedMotionVectors on or off and with CPU or GPU (Batched) mesh deformation (tests/projects/urp `motion_blur_skinned_moved`, `motion_blur_skinned`, `motion_blur_skinned_off`; Q226: `motion_blur_skinned_root`, the root bone moved alone, now 0 pixels over 8 (worst 1; mean 0.26 before the motion blur gathers matched), `motion_blur_skinned_renderer`, the renderer moved apart from its bones, no motion, `motion_blur_skinned_root_scale`, only the root bone's scale changed, no motion; the Manual's double-buffered skinned motion vectors do not show on this device); Unbound draws skins in world space, so a skin's previous matrix is its kept object matrix times the current inverse (the built-in renderer's motion pass shares the rule, unmeasured there); unmeasured: blend-shape-only skins (no bone weights: their drawing matrix is taken), a skin with active Cloth (no object motion); a renderer list with `FilteringSettings.forceAllMotionVectorObjects` (URP's XR depth and motion pass) is unmeasured: it draws without per-object motion vectors and warns |
