one limit stays, recorded in the macOS Editor (two identical captures) and declared in `residuals-metal.json`: overlapping triangles of one draw (batched particle quads, for example) read the color from before the draw where the Editor's read what the earlier ones wrote (`fetch.Overlap`, warned once per file; reading the target itself needs Metal fragment inputs `[[color(n)]]`, which naga 30.0.1's MSL output lacks (it writes `color(n)` for outputs only), so it would take hand-made MSL through wgpu's passthrough shaders with their own bindings beside the WGSL pipeline: not a contained change, recorded); on a multisampled target each fragment reads its own sample, as the Editor's do (`fetch.Msaa.*`, color target 0; further multisampled targets read alike, unmeasured); unmeasured: Intel Macs without fetch), Vulkan's conventions, `IUnityGraphicsMetal` (the native rendering plugins row). A Shadowmap descriptor's 24 bits are depth without stencil on every device (D24_UNorm, Metal D32_SFloat). `HasShaderDefine` answers framebuffer fetch's fallback with the standalone target only (`caps-metal-android.jsonl`: not with Android active; iOS and WebGL unmeasured, taken as Android), and programs compile with it (their framebuffer inputs read `_UnityFBInput<i>`). Not measured: Intel Macs and GPUs other than an Apple M2 Pro, other releases than 6000.0.71f1 (the macOS Editor ignoring `-force-*` included); SystemInfo answers and format tables per device (tests/projects/urp CapsProbe `caps-6000.0.71f1.jsonl`, `unity-6000.0.71f1/caps-vulkan.jsonl`), `SHADER_API_*`, `GL.GetGPUProjectionMatrix` (bit-exact), reversed depth, flipped render-texture projection, top-down screen and the multisampled depth resolve's sample on Vulkan (tests/projects/rtdepth `Reference/unity-6000.0.71f1/vulkan`: 1071/1071 values, 368/368 images), `IUnityGraphics::GetRenderer`. A built-in pipeline screen camera with command buffers draws into a stand-in render texture stored as the screen is (top-down on Vulkan): a Blit from `CameraTarget`/`CurrentActive` holds the screen's rows as stored, upside down on Vulkan, and wireframe edges rasterize into it as onto the screen (rtdepth `vulkan` `target.screen.*.blit{Active,Cam}.out`, `vulkan/controls` `ctl.wireframe{,Kept}.buffer.final`; a Blit with a material, `CopyTexture` and `ConvertTexture` of it follow the same storage, unmeasured, and `_MainTex_TexelSize.y` keeps its sign: the negative texel height top-down sources have where UV starts at the top is not modelled; a target pairing the stand-in with another texture draws both as the stand-in is stored, unmeasured and warned); Shadow casters' clip-space bias takes the device's clip depth (directional and spot: half, negated; point: half, measured), so the built-in pipeline's shadows match the Vulkan Editor as closely as on OpenGL Core (tests/projects/lighting `Reference/unity-6000.0.71f1/vulkan`); copies of the top-down screen are raw with their regions counted from the bottom (unmeasured on the Vulkan Editor); on Linux each device is drawn by its own wgpu backend: OpenGL Core by the OpenGL backend (an OpenGL 4.5 core EGL context drawing into an X11 window, under Wayland through Xwayland; without an X11 display the device does not start and the next one of the list is tried; the vendored backend changes are in `vendor/wgpu-sampler/PATCHES.md`), Vulkan by the Vulkan backend, and native plugins on OpenGL Core get a context in the renderer's share group, so a texture's native pointer is the renderer's own OpenGL texture, or a view of it where Unity's device has it with another target or in another format (the native rendering plugins row), of every shape (tests/projects/nativegfx `shape.*`: a cube map, a 2D array, a 3D texture, a cube map array, a multisampled render texture's resolved image; the native rendering plugins row). Nothing crosses between Vulkan and OpenGL any more: when Vulkan drew the OpenGL Core device, the renderer's images were imported into the plugins' OpenGL context, and handing their layouts over with semaphores was a follow-up; with a backend of its own for each device there is no such import, no code of it is left (no memory objects, no semaphores), and the follow-up is closed, not open. On OpenGL Core a graphics program samples a texture through the texture's own sampler state whatever sampler it names, an inline one or another texture's, in either stage, its comparisons too (tests/projects/samplerstates, 558/564 with six declared residuals; the Vulkan device samples through the sampler a program names, as the Vulkan Editor does, 564/564); compute kernels keep the states they name (tests/projects/renderapi computeSamplers). OpenGL samples through combined texture-and-sampler units, so where a program still samples one texture through several samplers (kernels, the other devices) it gets a texture variable for each, bound the same texture. A texture made with `CreateExternalTexture` from a plugin's own OpenGL texture is sampled in that texture's own format whatever format and color space the external texture declares, through its mip levels when it declares a mip chain (nativegfx `ext.*`). The anisotropic mip transition is each backend's own, as each Editor has it (tests/projects/samplerbias on both devices; the sampler states row). Measured on the NVIDIA driver only (the suite's OpenGL Core references run on it; other drivers may lack signed normalized render targets or sRGB framebuffers, which are asked of the driver or checked by `crates/unbound-app/src/render/gl_backend_tests.rs`); macOS's Metal device is drawn by wgpu's Metal backend (on macOS, rtdepth on Metal against the Vulkan reference: since the window has the asked size in pixels on a HiDPI display (Q475; it had twice the width and height before) 318/368 images and 1053/1071 values (Q491: texel loads outside a texture read zero on Metal as on the Editors' devices, `vendor/wgpu-sampler/PATCHES.md`, which closed `fmt.noDepth.depth.load` and the 24 `*.stencil.uint`/`*.globalStencil.uint` images): 44 `target.screen.*.final` and 6 `target.screen.*.blit{Active,Cam}.out` images differ by the GPU's sRGB conversions, which Unbound's Metal device shares with the macOS Editor's (tests/projects/nativegfx `metal.jsonl` `srgb.*`, two identical captures, matched exactly): a draw whose encoded value would be 127.5 stores 127 (presumably a tie) where the NVIDIA reference stores 128, and an 8-bit sRGB texel decodes to 32-bit floats (191 to 0.52088) where the NVIDIA reference's are half-precision steps (0.51953); the windowed macOS Editor's rtdepth itself is not recorded, and 18 values: 17 format answers following Metal's measured format table (10 of them measured directly) and the device name). Q506/Q515 (batch-mode and windowed macOS Editors, nativegfx `metal.jsonl`, `metal-windowed.jsonl`; windowed and batchmode Linux Editors on OpenGL Core and Vulkan, tests/projects/framestate, NVIDIA only): the next frame starts with no active render texture, whichever of Update, LateUpdate or an end-of-frame callback left one and whether a camera rendered (`RenderTexture.active` is reset after the end-of-frame callbacks, before EarlyUpdate), and `Graphics.ExecuteCommandBuffer` outside a camera's rendering (built-in cameras' callbacks, a pipeline's render loop and render requests count as inside) starts from GL's matrices, as immediate drawing does (`GL.LoadOrtho`'s inside it), its own matrices not lasting past it (`outside.*`, `matrices.*`). `Camera.Render` gives GL's matrices back; the frame's own built-in camera rendering leaves its last camera's for the end-of-frame callbacks (`matrices.endofframe`, windowed Editors; batchmode Editors run none); between frames a windowed Editor's GL matrices are its last window drawing's pixel matrix, so clip-space draws outside cameras draw nothing there (`outside.fresh`, `aftercamera`, `corner`, `after` on Linux and macOS): Unbound follows the windowed Editor and loads the Game view's pixel matrix, its one window, at the frame's end (the Editors' view is one of the window's layout, 1280x217 in the Linux captures: 6 declared framestate residuals per device; 4 nativegfx Metal residuals against the batch-mode `metal.jsonl`). Batchmode Editors start a frame from identity instead (`matrices.early`); in the batchmode OpenGL Core Editor every dump after EarlyUpdate's has a zero projection and `GL.modelview` reads zeros (`opengl-batch.jsonl`; cause not established). Unmeasured: a last camera with image effects, a scriptable pipeline's frame (Unbound leaves the screen's pixel matrix there), overlay canvases, and GL's matrices before the first frame ends (Unbound: identity). Each OnGUI Repaint loads the screen's pixel matrix from its top left (near -1, far 100), which stays for the end-of-frame callbacks after the GUI (`matrices.ongui`, `matrices.endofframe.gui`), so `Graphics.DrawTexture` there draws in the screen coordinates the Scripting API documents; residual: `DrawMeshNow` inside OnGUI keeps the last camera's view in the Editor (Unbound: identity); several OnGUI scripts and `DrawMeshNow` after a script's own `GL.LoadPixelMatrix` are unmeasured. The windowed Editor also runs plugin events later, on its render thread (`camera.pixels` and `executed.pixels` show colors the scripts set after the events; the plugin rows' order varies between runs), where Unbound runs them in the calls that issue them. Open: the Vulkan Editor's GPU recorder and the Vulkan and Metal Editors' foveation answers (Unbound has neither). |
| URP light layers: a draw's rendering layer mask reaches its shader (`unity_RenderingLayer`) | `Graphics/FrameBuilder.cs` (`WithRenderingLayer`, `QueuedItem`), `CommandBuffer.cs` (`DrawRenderer`) | S | done (Q128) for renderers and Graphics.RenderMesh draws (`RenderParams.renderingLayerMask`): a draw off the default layer carries its mask, so a light on another rendering layer lights it (tests/projects/urp `paths-opengl/light_layers_*`: the point light on Layer 1 lights the spheres on Default and Layer 1, not the cube or the ground on Default; before, every draw was Default). `Terrain.renderingLayerMask` is the terrain renderer's mask (Q283: set at enable and on change, so the terrain carries it to renderer lists and to light layers; tests/projects/urp `terrain_light_layers`, the Layer 1 point light lights only the Layer 1 terrain; the Scripting API: "Determines which rendering layers the Terrain renderer lives on"). Its trees and details, collected with the terrain renderer, take the same mask (Unbound's choice, unmeasured; a renderer list filtering the terrain out is not measured either). URP's Terrain Lit compiles since Q283 (`1.0h`-style half literals are read as floats, as FXC does on desktop; before, every URP terrain drew with the error shader). The URP Terrain Lit shading residual `terrain_light_layers` had (mean 0.57, the Layer 1 terrain darker away from the light) went with the terrain layers' metallic and smoothness (Q442): mean 0.00, worst 1 on the S21 suite. |
| URP forward visible light order and per-object light choice beyond the per-object limit | `Graphics/Srp/SrpContext.cs` (`Culling.CollectVisibleLights`), `Graphics/LightImportance.cs`, `Graphics/FrameBuilder.cs` (`PerObjectLights`), `Graphics/ForwardLights.cs` | M | done (Q282; tests/projects/urp light-order.jsonl 40/40, `paths-opengl/many_lights_*` exact). The Manual documents only that Forward has a per-object limit (the URP asset's Per Object Limit, https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.0/Documentation/Manual/urp/rendering/forward-rendering-paths.html); the order is measured: an object's lights rank by the luminance of the colour as set times the intensity, local ones divided by 1 + 25 d² / range² from the bounds' centre, a shadowed directional light times 16, Important +1000, Not Important −1000, ties in visible order; `unity_LightData.y` counts all of them, `.z` is 0 without a main light or when its culling mask leaves out the layer; spot lights reach what their pyramid touches. Visible lights: intensity ≥ 0.001, culling mask sharing a layer with the camera's; local ones spot lights first, then point lights, each nearest the culling origin first; with maximumVisibleLights ≥ 0 the nearest stay (0: none). The built-in forward path ranks its pixel/vertex/SH lights with the same rule, and the Auto light that fills the pixel lights keeps (its importance − the next one's) / (the previous one's − the next one's) of its pixel pass, the rest going to a first vertex light (point, spot) or the harmonics (directional); the first vertex light gives up that share whichever light it is, a fourth vertex light is dropped, and the last vertex light fades the same way (tests/projects/builtinrp `light_rank_directional`, `light_rank_color` in a linear project, `light_rank_shadowed_sun`, `light_rank_directional_fill`, `light_rank_vertex`: means 0.04, 0.01, 0.02, 0.00, 1.06; tests/projects/lighting `five_points` 4.42 → 0.29). The Manual (built-in forward rendering) documents Important lights as always per-pixel and Auto ones per-pixel up to the Pixel Light Count; the ranking and fades are measured. The colour is taken as set (urp `color_temperature`, builtinrp `light_rank_color`). Not measured: a rotated renderer's oriented bounds against a point light's range (world bounds here), the spot pyramid's corners against a round cone, the order without a maximum (maximumVisibleLights −1: enabling order here), the directional lights' order among themselves (not sorted by Unity; brightest first here), ties at equal distance. |
| Sampler states of graphics programs: inline samplers (`sampler_PointClamp`, ...), other textures' samplers, comparisons, by device | `crates/unbound-shaders/src/samplers.rs`, `crates/unbound-app/src/render/executor.rs` | S | done (Q171, Q307, Q352) for Unity 6000.0.71f1 on the Editor's OpenGL Core and Vulkan devices, measured on the NVIDIA driver only (both Editors and Unbound ran on it; other drivers are not measured). The 6000.0 Manual (Using sampler states, https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.0/Documentation/Manual/SL-SamplerStates.html): "Inline samplers don't work if you target the OpenGL ES or OpenGL Core graphics APIs"; measured: each texture samples through its own state (tests/projects/urp `inline_sampler`: a Point/Repeat texture through `probe_linear_clamp_sampler` and a Bilinear/Clamp one through `probe_point_mirror_sampler`, outside 0..1; `upscale_point` equals `upscale_linear`). Graphics programs' samples through an inline sampler (named like no texture's, spelling a state) are rewritten in its SPIR-V to the texture's own sampler (`sampler<texture>`, declared when missing), in vertex and fragment stages. Compute kernels preserve predefined sampler states (Q274; `tests/projects/renderapi` `computeSamplers.*`). Kept: integer textures (read unfiltered); comparisons on the other devices keep the comparison sampler they name. Results: `upscale_point`, `postfx_film_grain`, `ssao_downsample` and `ssao_samples_high` became exact; the motion blur gathers got closer (`motion_blur_camera` 2926 to 1767 pixels over 8, `_high` 1093 to 249, `_objects` 268 to 110); they have matched since, 0 pixels over 8 (URP row). A texture sampled through another texture's sampler is sampled through its own state on OpenGL Core too (Q307, tests/projects/samplerstates; graphics devices row), and so are its comparisons, whatever comparison sampler they name (Q352, samplerstates `CmpTwo.map`). A texture sampled with and without comparisons is two texture slots of one property, the depth texture with a comparison sampler and the texture as its other samples read it, each with the texture's own sampler of its kind (Q352, samplerstates `CmpOwn`, `CmpInline`, `CmpOther` and their `.map`/`.mapLinear` rows; 547/557 (10 declared residuals) records on OpenGL Core, NVIDIA). Fragment outputs go where their semantics say, by the DX9 names HLSLSupport.cginc gives them too: a CG program's `SV_Depth` is the depth it stores (it was a second color before; samplerstates `Fill`, `DepthOnly.depth`); into a target without a depth buffer such a program draws its colors alone, and one that writes only the depth leaves the colors as they were (`Fill.color`, `DepthOnly.color`). Non-comparing depth reads are done (Q386): native samplers retain texture-owned depth state independently of the shader's declared comparison. `DepthState.*` covers D16/D24/D32, point/bilinear filters, None/CompareDepths/RawDepth, foreign and inline samplers, and repeated binding changes. All 24 new controls match on both devices and every earlier 525 reference row remains unchanged. Eight additional temporary-pool controls preserve sampling mode and bindMS between leases, with all earlier 549 rows unchanged; pool identity includes both allocation settings. The native opt-in is restricted to NVIDIA OpenGL/Vulkan and is rejected elsewhere. The measured release profile carries explicit sampler state; other releases retain legacy comparison behavior until measured; a 16-bit depth buffer is stored as 16 bits without stencil, as the Editor's (`DepthChannels.split.raw`, `.map.raw` exact; Q370), and its linear filtering now matches through a normalized 16-bit copy where the device supports it (Q386, `DepthChannels.splitLinear.raw`, `.mapLinear.raw`, `D16Filter.*`; NVIDIA only). The D16 and R16 UNorm controls agree at nine sampling coordinates, while float copies of the same endpoints differ between texel centers. Other devices retain a float fallback with a warning. A stencil test drawn into a 16-bit depth buffer passes everywhere (no stencil buffer, as documented for `RenderTexture.depth`), and a D16_UNorm_S8_UInt target keeps a stencil buffer and reads back as D24_UNorm_S8_UInt on both devices (samplerstates `Stencil.*`, recorded in the Editor; Unbound converts a format the device cannot render when it is set, the Editor measured after Create; a `.renderTexture` asset converts unless `m_EnableCompatibleFormat` is 0, and one with the fallback off and a 16-bit format with stencil is stored with 24 bits and stencil, the Editor's result for it not measured). On OpenGL Core on Linux, graphics programs combining comparison sampling and `Load` remain explicitly unsupported and draw with the error shader; a separate raw fetch slot for graphics remains queued. Compute kernels read the target's depth buffer, or a far-plane default without one. A 2D-array or cube-array slot without a depth texture of its shape skips the dispatch with a warning, unmeasured in the Editor. Kernel comparison samplers compare less-or-equal through the texture's state, mirrored on Vulkan's reversed depth. OpenGL compute kernels now support comparison plus `Load` for non-array, single-sampled 2D inputs at constant mip zero through separate comparison and raw bindings; other shapes and mip accesses remain explicitly unsupported. The raw fetches match all measured cases, including source rebinding; six comparison-lane differences retain NVIDIA Editor resource history that is not explained by a single polarity, filter or Depth/Shadowmap distinction. On the Vulkan device a graphics program samples through the sampler it names (Q352; samplerstates `--device vulkan`, 557/557 records against the Vulkan Editor, NVIDIA): an inline sampler is the state its name spells (`Inline`, `CmpInline.*`, `DepthPlain.*`): one filter word, wrap words for the whole sampler or for the axes their `U`/`V`/`W` letters name (also along a volume's W), an anisotropic level of 2, 4, 8 or 16 (`InlineAxes`, `InlineV`, `InlineWords`, `InlineVolume`, `InlineAniso`: `ClampUV` names U alone, a whole-sampler `Repeat` wins over `MirrorOnce`, the axes a `MirrorOnceU` name leaves out mirror), and a wrongly spelt one (two filters, `Clamp` with `Repeat` or `Mirror`, another anisotropic level) is the Editor's error, word for word (`Bad*`; OpenGL Core, which does not read the names, draws those programs through the textures' own states); in a compute kernel a wrongly spelt name is logged and samples as the default (unmeasured there); another texture's sampler that texture's state, an inline `...Compare` sampler compares greater-or-equal on reversed depth, and a program with a sampler of a texture it does not use is refused in the Editor's words and draws with the error shader (`Shapes`: "Unrecognized sampler ... does not match any texture and is not a recognized inline name"; a `Load` of the texture or its use in the other stage counts: `UsedLoad`, `UsedVertex`); the rewrite to a texture's own sampler is OpenGL Core's alone (`GraphicsDevice::samples_through_texture_state`). On Metal (Q564; samplerstates `--device metal`, `Reference/unity-6000.0.71f1/metal.jsonl`, the macOS Editor on an Apple M2 Pro, two identical captures) a program samples through the sampler it names, as on Vulkan, but the compiler resolves each stage's samplers against that stage's textures alone (`Vertex`, `UsedVertex` are refused in the Editor's words and draw with the error shader) and refuses no wrongly spelt inline name, reading it with the same words and precedence and nothing refused (`Bad*` draw; of two filters the linear or trilinear one, `Clamp` over `Repeat` and `Mirror`, whatever the texture's own state: a separate capture of a two-texel texture of three own states, `InlineSampler::read_leniently`; an invalid anisotropic level is taken as none, unmeasured; probe rows of it wait for the Linux references to be recorded again): `GraphicsDevice::named_samplers`, Unbound 578/588. On Metal a depth texture's sample reads the depth in red, green and blue and 1 in alpha (OpenGL Core and Vulkan: red only), which Unbound's depth copy holds in four channels there (`GraphicsDevice::depth_sample_fills_color`; kernels and integer slots reading a depth-only target are given the same copy, unmeasured on Metal), and a program writing the depth into a target without a depth buffer draws nothing (the Editor fails to make its pipeline; `GraphicsDevice::draws_depth_output_without_depth_buffer`): Unbound 588/588 on Metal (was 578/588). The non-comparing depth graphics and Vulkan compute residuals are closed. Current scalar results are OpenGL Core 558/564 with six declared comparison-lane residuals, Vulkan 564/564. OpenGL mixed comparison-plus-Load compute kernels use separate comparison and raw-value bindings; all four raw-load pairs match, and the bilinear Shadowmap mixed case matches completely. Seven new strict raw-lane/source-switch rows per device preserve all 557 older reference rows byte-for-byte. Independently cleared sources make SetTexture rebinding observable. Shader cache format 46 invalidates prior compiled kernels; graphics comparison-plus-Load remains explicitly unsupported. A single comparison polarity, filtering or Depth/Shadowmap rule does not explain the remaining measured NVIDIA compute source histories, which remain queued. OpenGL Core and Vulkan MirrorOnce use native mirror-clamp-to-edge when supported (tests/projects/samplerstates `InlineMirrorOnce`, `MirrorWrap.*`; NVIDIA only). OpenGL Core checks version 4.4 or ARB/EXT support once at device creation, and all three texture-owned `MirrorWrap.*` axis controls now match. tests/projects/samplerbias is recorded on the Vulkan Editor too (`Reference/unity-6000.0.71f1/vulkan`, every profile, two identical captures each): with anisotropic filtering the Vulkan Editor's mip transition starts at a whole level where OpenGL Core's is centred on a half, as each backend has it natively, so the correction that centred it when Vulkan drew the OpenGL Core device is removed (all 8 profiles match on both devices: 94834/94834 observations in the default one) |

| Script float arithmetic in single precision (wider intermediates in measured Editor expressions) | `managed/Unbound.Host/WideFloats.cs`, `WideFloats.Expressions.cs`, `HotReload.cs`, `Runtime/Compat.cs` (`ScriptFloatsWide`, `MonoFloats`) | S | off by default, user decision 2026-11-13: the wider intermediates are an artefact of the Editor's Mono runtime that IL2CPP builds do not share, so by default game code keeps CoreCLR's float32 evaluation, as a built player does. The rule below stays in the code as an opt-in: the environment variable `UNBOUND_MONO_FLOATS=1` (or `true`, `on`) when the engine starts, still only for the measured release; it covers the load-time conversion, the generated converted math (row below), the hot-reload restart and the cache key part. tests/projects/floatrounding's gate opts in. With it off, measured on Linux against the opt-in: texturepixels' three VolumeRGFloat rows per color space differ again (3 differences over tolerance 0, as before Q429), humanoid's bone.rotateThenSolve step 17 roll split differs again (muscles 41 and 43, within its bounded residual) while stream.bodyLocal step 17 matches, twod's spriteshape.render matches again (1 scenario differs instead of 2), and geometryframe2d, physics2ddynamics, physics2dmotion and query2dparity move by rounding with no failure; floatrounding, urp, particletriggerboundary and the other 50 of the 60 probes compared are unchanged; the other suite probes were not compared both ways (the sheriff baseline predates the rule, so a probe returning to its earlier values does not show as worse). Opted in: done for Unity 6000.0.71f1 script, package and precompiled plugin assemblies (tests/projects/floatrounding: Release 206/268 and Debug 215/268 values, 0 failures, the listed residuals below; the Linux x86_64 and macOS arm64 Editors record identical values in both modes, two captures each). In the Editor a float on the evaluation stack is a double: chains, comparisons, conversions (`(int)`, `(double)`, `Math.Sqrt`) and values left on the stack across calls and joins keep it, and it is rounded to float where the IL stores it (locals, arguments, fields, array elements, indirect stores, kept boxes, closure/iterator/async fields, every call argument and return value, also of inlined, generic, interface and delegate calls, and `conv.r4`); `box` straight into `unbox.any` keeps it. Debug and Release differ only where the compiled IL keeps a local. Decision: a load-time IL rewrite, because it covers source-compiled and precompiled assemblies alike and no Roslyn option or CoreCLR knob reproduces the rule (Q402 controls). Unbound converts to double the float loads of each expression whose result double evaluation can change (an operation on another operation's result, or a result that is compared, converted or used other than by rounding it to float); one operation rounded straight away gives the same float and stays as it is; the runtime rounds stores and arguments ([ECMA-335](https://ecma-international.org/publications-and-standards/standards/ecma-335/) III.1.1.1, III.1.6). Cost, macOS arm64 benchmark: a particle-style struct loop 1.5x, a scalar polynomial loop 1.2x (converting every float load would cost 3.3x by defeating inlining). Scope: `[BurstCompile]` methods and the `Execute` of `[BurstCompile]` job structs keep float arithmetic (the other methods of such types, and the methods Burst-compiled code calls, are converted; Burst in the Editor is unmeasured); a hot-reload edit is patched in place unless an edited method needs the conversions, which restarts the session (deltas are not converted); reflection on a converted method body shows the conversions and the calls to converted math (89 `il/` residuals). Unity's C# math in game code: row below. Open: other releases keep CoreCLR's float evaluation, unmeasured. The [C# specification 8.3.7](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/csharp/language-reference/language-specification/types#837-floating-point-types) permits these wider intermediates. URP's `(int)(pixelWidth * renderScale)` (tests/projects/urp upscale_auto_fractional) is package code under the same rule and is re-checked by the Linux GPU suite; tests/projects/particletriggerboundary keeps its explicit double arithmetic, which equals the rule |
| Unity's C# math in game code evaluated in float32 (Q435) | `managed/Unbound.Host/WideFloats.EngineMath.cs`, `managed/Unbound.ScriptMath/`, `managed/UnityEngine/Math/` | S | part of the Mono float opt-in (row above; off by default): opted in, done for Unity 6000.0.71f1 (tests/projects/floatrounding: every `fuzz/` math row and every `api/` row of a C# method that computes on its own now matches in both modes; Release 206/268, Debug 215/268 on both hosts with Q434, 0 failures; the Linux x86_64 and macOS arm64 Editors record identical values, two captures each per mode; the `native/` rows are the Linux Editor's on every host since Q488, other operands having given a few host differences in Quaternion.Euler, eulerAngles and Vector3.RotateTowards). Unity implements Mathf, Vector2/3/4, Quaternion, Matrix4x4, Color, Color32, Plane, Rect, Bounds, Pose and GeometryUtility mostly in C#: in the Editor their arithmetic follows the script rule (row above) and every local they declare rounds (`Vector3.Distance` rounds each coordinate difference). A game assembly's calls (also constructors and delegates) to such a method go to a converted copy of it in one generated assembly, `Unbound.EngineMath` (built once per engine, cached with the adapted assemblies, and optimized by the runtime in either code optimization mode); the engine's own calls keep float32. The copies come from a second build of the engine's math sources without IL optimization (`managed/Unbound.ScriptMath`, embedded in Unbound.Host and never loaded), where every declared local is a store; the optimized engine build keeps some of them on the evaluation stack. Methods Unity binds to native code (Quaternion Slerp, Lerp, Euler, eulerAngles, LookRotation, AngleAxis, FromToRotation, Inverse; Matrix4x4 TRS, inverse, transpose, determinant, rotation, lossyScale, Ortho, Perspective, LookAt, Frustum; Vector3 Slerp, RotateTowards, OrthoNormalize; Bounds Contains, SqrDistance, ClosestPoint, IntersectRay; Mathf Perlin noise, gamma and half conversions) keep the engine's implementation; `ScriptMathTests.NativeMathFollowsTheReleaseBindings` checks that list against the release's assemblies when its Editor is installed. A hot-reload edit calling converted math restarts the session. Fixed on the way: Matrix4x4.Rotate uses the rotation part of TRS rounded per element (in float32 the same as TRS with a unit scale, which engine callers keep); GeometryUtility.TryCreatePlaneFromPolygon gives the plane (up, 0) and false for fewer than three vertices, the triangle's plane for three, and for more Newell's normal at the vertices' mean distance, true when the normal is not zero (`api/polygonPlane` triangles, `api/polygonPlaneQuad` five vertices, `api/polygonPlaneDegenerate` two vertices and four on a line); Quaternion.RotateTowards takes Mathf.Min. Cost, macOS arm64: a per-object update loop over the math API 1.3x in Release game code (25.4 to 33.6 ms) and 1.03x in Debug game code (70.4 to 72.2 ms); building the generated assembly about 90 ms once per engine build. JIT check on two test games' 576 assemblies: 0 regressions, the 141 generated methods compile. The native math and the C# methods that take a native result (`Matrix4x4.TransformPlane`, `Quaternion.RotateTowards`) now match too: row "Unity's natively bound math in single precision (Q443)". Open: `GeometryUtility.CalculateFrustumPlanes(Camera)`, `Pose.GetTransformedBy(Transform)` and `Transform.RotateAround`'s position turn are C# in Unity too but keep the engine's float32 (they take engine classes, unmeasured); Unity's managed code outside the math structs (RectTransformUtility, UI Toolkit, ...) stays engine code in float32, unmeasured |
| Unity's natively bound math in single precision (Q443) | `managed/UnityEngine/Math/RotationMatrix.cs`, `PolarRotation.cs`, `Quaternion.cs`, `Vector3.cs`, `Matrix4x4.cs`, `Bounds.cs` | S | done for Unity 6000.0.71f1 (tests/projects/floatrounding: all 23 `native/` rows matched the macOS Editor's in both modes on macOS arm64; since Q488 macOS compares against the Linux Editor's rows, five per mode differ there, recorded; the 11 `native/` residuals and the `api/matrixVector` and `api/quaternionRotateTowards` residuals are gone; two Editor captures per host and mode, byte-identical). The engine's implementations of the methods Unity binds to native code compute in single precision with the Editor's algorithms and order: Quaternion.Slerp interpolates along the shorter arc with sines of the angle when the dot product is below 0.95 (unnormalized) and normalized linear interpolation above; Lerp is that normalized interpolation; AngleAxis converts degrees as angle / 360 doubled times PI and gives identity for an axis not longer than 1e-6; LookRotation builds an orthonormal frame and falls back to the shortest rotation from Z (identity for a forward not longer than 1e-5); FromToRotation uses Möller and Hughes' matrix (a half turn about an orthogonal axis for opposite directions); a rotation matrix becomes a quaternion by Shoemake's method, divided by its length; eulerAngles extracts Z, X, Y with gimbal lock beyond 0.499999; Vector3.Slerp and RotateTowards rotate the direction by an axis-angle matrix (straight interpolation within a dot product of 0.99999); Matrix4x4.inverse is Gauss-Jordan elimination with partial pivoting (zero for a zero pivot); rotation takes the matrix, its normalized columns or, for a mirroring or degenerate one, the rotation of its singular value decomposition (McAdams et al., five Jacobi sweeps), so a mirroring matrix keeps the reflection on its smallest-scale axis; lossyScale negates X by the 3x3 determinant's sign; Perspective takes the cotangent from a single-precision sine and cosine; Bounds Contains, SqrDistance and ClosestPoint compare against centre plus or minus extents (a point inside comes back unchanged). The hosts differ through the platform's sine, cosine and inverse trigonometric functions, which .NET takes from the same C library as the Editor (glibc, Apple libm), and in one rule of the arm64 Editor: Bounds.SqrDistance's larger-of-two gives NaN for a NaN operand there and the extent on x86_64, which Unbound gives on every host (Q488); the remaining arithmetic gave the same values on x86_64 and arm64. The public methods log the Editor's messages for degenerate operands (`native/log`: "Look rotation viewing vector is zero" for a forward not longer than 1e-5; assertions for a zero-length normalization, a non-affine matrix's rotation or lossyScale, a rotation matrix that is not orthonormal, a rotation that is not normalized or gives angles that are not finite, and a box with a NaN centre or extent); the engine's own callers use the same arithmetic through variants without these messages, except where they stand for Unity's internals rather than its scripting API: rendering, physics, particle, cloth and navigation code keeps the previous double-precision matrix inverse and the cameras', lights' and projectors' perspective a double-precision cotangent (`Matrix4x4.InverseDoublePrecision`, `PerspectiveDoublePrecision`; with the scripting single-precision forms the Linux lighting probe moved: shadow_directional_hard pixels over 8 from 24 to 26), root motion, layer turns, body interpolation, agent turning and constraint weighting keep their previous slerp and lerp, and the engine's turning of bodies, transforms, vehicles, terrain trees and particles its previous axis-angle rotation (`QuaternionMath`; the scripting one moved physics2ddynamics and physics2dmotion values by rounding on Linux), and the engine's look rotations (lights' shadow views, particles, physics frames, constraints, navigation, environment probes, billboards) their previous double-precision one (`QuaternionMath.LookRotation`; a forward along the up vector takes the single-precision FromToRotation from +Z, unmeasured; the scripting one turned four of a spot light's culling planes across the fourth decimal in tests/projects/brg local_shadows). Measured against the Scripting API's text: with a negative maxRadiansDelta, Vector3.RotateTowards turns away from the target and, once nearly opposite, keeps turning by the angle about an orthogonal axis instead of stopping (`native/degenerate`); Bounds.Contains is true for a point with a NaN coordinate; Quaternion.Lerp of a zero-length result gives NaN components with its assertion. Open: the precision of Unity's internal matrix inverse, perspective, slerp and axis-angle in the engine-internal paths above is unmeasured (they keep what their probes were measured with), and so is RotateAround about an axis between 1e-6 and 1e-5 long, whose position turns (scripting cutoff 1e-6) while its rotation does not (engine cutoff 1e-5); NaN payloads are unmeasured; Matrix4x4.rotation of a matrix whose determinant is within 1e-6 of 1 without being a rotation (e.g. scale (2, 0.5, 1)) takes the matrix as it is, by the same rule but unmeasured; other releases use the same algorithms, unmeasured |

For Unity 6000.0.71f1, fixed rendering resolutions now use a centered image rectangle with uniform scaling and integer default zoom when enlargement fits. The same default-zoom rule maps mouse/Input System positions and cursor warp requests between window and rendering pixels. Presentation recomputes its rectangle from the current host dimensions even when an older screen image is retained. tests/projects/gameview records six floating-window controls and a retained-frame resize, including landscape/portrait targets, downscaling and odd margins, in two identical NVIDIA OpenGL Core captures. All 43 original rows remain byte-identical after the seven-record extension. The retained-frame camera is disabled after drawing, with no further render callback while the host resizes under OnDemandRendering; an engine control also preserves ordinary unfixed-window presentation across that resize. The replay retains one pointer-coordinate residual of two float32 steps, 49/50 exact records, and enforces all host-image bounds; Screen and Camera.pixelRect retain rendering dimensions. Other releases keep the prior independent x/y stretch until measured. Unmeasured: sizes below 1, `GetRenderingResolution` between the call and the next rendering, what a frame after one in which no camera drew starts with (Unbound: opaque black). Reads in Update return the Editor window's framebuffer in the Editor (screencapture `read.update.*`, not reproduced). Q469 presentation scope: Linux at one display pixel per point and the default Game view zoom. User-selected zoom/panning, high-DPI backing scales, native Input System warp delivery, multiple Game views and other GPU drivers remain unmeasured. The host blit's filtering is unchanged; solid-color border controls do not measure it. The [6000.0 Game view Manual](https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.0/Documentation/Manual/GameView.html) describes zoom; [Input.mousePosition](https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.0/Documentation/ScriptReference/Input-mousePosition.html) permits coordinates outside the Game view, exercised by the bar controls; exact centering/default scale are release-specific observations. Existing screen-read references are unchanged. |

| BatchRendererGroup / GPU Resident Drawer | `Graphics/Srp/BatchRendererGroup.cs`, `Graphics/Srp/BatchCulling.cs`, `crates/unbound-app/src/render/dots.rs` | W | partial (Q132): BatchRendererGroup draws for camera and shadow cullings on the Editor's OpenGL Core and Vulkan devices, measured on the NVIDIA driver, and on Metal (Q551: the macOS Editor on an Apple M2 Pro, tests/projects/brg `Reference/unity-6000.0.71f1/metal`, every value and image as Unbound's; Metal's values match Vulkan's apart from `device` and a one-step rounding of read-back colours (24 rows), its images differ from the Linux ones by Metal's rounding; the GPU Resident Drawer likewise, Q564: tests/projects/residentdrawer `Reference/unity-6000.0.71f1/metal/<profile>`, all seven profiles, Unbound matches every value and image) (tests/projects/brg `basic`, `cameras`, `shadows`: no pixel differs on either device; the Editor draws on both, though the Manual's platform list names only Vulkan for Linux, https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.0/Documentation/Manual/batch-renderer-group-how.html). Registration (1-based IDs, owners counted, IDs not reused after removal or the last owner leaving: brg `lifetime`), batches (metadata immutable, missing metadata zero, buffer swappable: the Manual's "Create batches" page; brg `swap`, and data changed between renders draws at the next: `lifetime`), AddBatch refusing a window the device does not take with the Editor's errors and BatchID.Null (none for a constant buffer, one off the alignment or over the device's constant buffer size of 65536 (65792 refused, 32768 taken; a window of exactly 65536 taken, as the message's "over" says: unmeasured); any window or offset for a raw buffer: brg `errors.add`), every scriptable camera culling invoking the enabled groups' callbacks (the Scripting API: "Whenever Unity renders a BatchRendererGroup, it invokes the OnPerformCulling callback") with the Editor's context (6 planes as one split, the Game view's scene culling mask, the camera's layers and view ID; a second camera its own), every ScriptableRenderContext.CullShadowCasters invoking the groups that take light views once per light with splits not all excluded (the splits' planes in order, then the camera's frustum as receiver planes with the far one at the shadow distance when the light culls by the view frustum, the light's matrix and view ID, the camera's LOD parameters and its layers the light lights, CullLightmappedShadowCasters for a light whose direct light is baked or a mixed Shadowmask light that leaves lightmapped casters out: the brg probe's light is realtime; the rule runs in residentdrawer `lightmaps` and `shadowmask`, not isolated by a control) and the light's shadow caster lists drawing the casting ranges of its culling's commands, a camera culling's ranges on a layer its layer mask leaves out discarded (the Scripting API's BatchCullingContext.cullingLayerMask; brg's pillar on a layer the second camera leaves out), while a light culling's casters are drawn whatever their layer though its mask leaves the layer out (measured: brg's pillars on layers the sun's and the camera's masks leave out still cast shadows), SetEnabledViewTypes (a group taking light views alone gets no camera callbacks), the output arrays freed after the frame (the Manual's "Create draw commands"), direct draw commands drawn by the culling's renderer lists through their layer, rendering layer and motion filters with the material's `DOTS_INSTANCING_ON` variant, at most 256 instances per draw (the shader library's visible-instance array), with the per-draw header the shader library reads (rendering layer, flip winding, motion, main light, cross-fade, mesh bounds) and `unity_DOTSInstanceGlobalValues` (ambient probe, unoccluded, default reflection decode). Constant buffer windows (OpenGL) are copied on the GPU from the batch's buffer into each draw's uniform block before its pass, so a raw buffer given to AddBatch with a window (as Entities Graphics does in constant buffer mode) and instance data a compute kernel or a copy wrote are drawn (Q535, brg `gpu_written`: a raw buffer whose colours a kernel writes after SetData; every value on both devices; Unbound read a CPU copy of constant buffers before, so such a batch drew zeros). Needed on the way: FXC's re-split of a short macro call's expanded arguments (URP's DOTS lightmap sampling), `static` declarations moved out of cbuffers (glslang read them as zero members), exact int draw properties, a camera's exact near and far culling planes. Directional cascades (CullingResults.ComputeDirectionalShadowMatricesAndCullingPrimitives, Q393) carry the Editor's caster culling planes (the slice's planes facing against the light, then a plane along the light through each silhouette edge; the slice reaches the culling sphere's far side or the shadow distance, its near plane moved back by the cascade's near plane), culling matrix (projection by view, the projection's near plane at 0.1 radii minus ten times the near plane offset, URP's Light.shadowNearPlane; Unbound took 30 before) and conservative culling spheres (the iterative sphere around the slice's corners, from the projection inverted in single precision): brg `shadows` matches every value and pixel on both devices, the light contexts' planes first, then the receivers. Without conservative spheres the shadow distance's slice covers the Editor's share of the frustum (from the frustum's far corner ray and an offset by the field of view, counted from the camera's near plane less the culling parameters' shadowNearPlaneOffset, whose default is 0), every cascade's culling sphere bounds its own part of the slice, from the previous split, and its culling near plane lies where that part starts (Q450: tests/projects/urp ShadowMatrixProbe's recorded slices and cascades within 1e-4, and its slice-shares records: orthographic cameras, fields of view 20 and 170, far planes 50 and 200, offsets of 5 and -2 with and without conservative spheres; Unbound's earlier fit was off by up to 4%, and orthographic and narrow cameras' later cascades took spheres from the near plane; the built-in pipeline instead uses a single directional map for orthographic cameras in the measured 6000.0.71f1 profile (Q496, lines cascade controls)). Unmeasured: the caster planes of cascades without conservative spheres and of a pipeline that lowers the culling plane count (the six stored planes are taken), a negative near plane offset (taken as none), a light whose every split is excluded is not culled (no callback), the CullLightmappedShadowCasters rule, and the receiver planes of a camera with a custom culling matrix (the far one is taken along the camera's view, the others from the matrix). Point and spot light cullings take the same context as a directional light's, one callback per light with all its splits, the light as the view at slice -1 (a directional light's 0), each split's culling matrix the light's projection by view, a point light face's with its vertical axis upright and on Vulkan its depth axis turned too (Q450, brg `local_shadows`: every value and pixel on both devices; Unbound ties the depth turn to reversed depth, an assumption for unmeasured devices). A direct command's draw leaves `unity_DOTSInstancing_IndirectInstanceVisibility` unset without a warning, as the Editor logs none (the shader library reads it only for indirect commands; Q450). Transparent sorting (Q466, brg `sorting`, the Scripting API's BatchDrawCommand.sortingPosition: with HasSortingPosition the index of the first instance's world position in instanceSortingPositions, without it "an approximate depth"): a flagged command's instances are drawn one by one, each sorted at its position by the camera's distance metric among the other draws (measured for transparent draws; Unbound splits a flagged command in every list, so a large flagged opaque command draws instance by instance, unmeasured); an unflagged command sorts as if minus its sortingPosition were its value of the camera's distance metric, the squared distance through a perspective camera and the view depth through an orthographic one, equal values keeping the commands' order (measured with both, brg `sorting` and `sorting_ortho`, Q515: the orthographic threshold lies between 5 and 7 for a renderer at view depth 6 and distance 6.46 to 6.59, taken as the view depth the Scripting API's TransparencySortMode.Orthographic names, https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.0/Documentation/ScriptReference/TransparencySortMode.Orthographic.html; custom-axis sorting unmeasured), and a flagged indirect command sorts at its first instance's position (unmeasured); a flagged command pointing outside instanceSortingPositions is warned about and sorts as an unflagged one (unmeasured). Lightmap texture arrays (Q535, brg `lightmap_arrays`, every value on both devices): a command without UseLegacyLightmapsKeyword takes its lightmaps from its material, as Entities Graphics makes lightmapped materials (LIGHTMAP_ON, DIRLIGHTMAP_COMBINED for directional lightmaps, `unity_Lightmaps`, `unity_LightmapsInd` and `unity_ShadowMasks` arrays) with each instance's `unity_LightmapIndex` slice and `unity_LightmapST`; the material's keyword decides, the IsLightMapped flag does not (measured: a flagged command whose material lacks the keyword is lit by the ambient probe, an unflagged one with the keyword samples its slice; the Scripting API says only that lightmaps "might not work correctly unless this flag is set", https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.0/Documentation/ScriptReference/Rendering.BatchDrawCommandFlags.IsLightMapped.html). Unmeasured: the flag's effect on the header's main-light bit and on lightmapped shadow casters with mixed lights (Unbound counts a flagged command as lightmapped there), shadow mask arrays. Split visibility masks (Q466, brg `split_masks`; the Scripting API's BatchDrawCommand.splitVisibilityMask: the least significant bit is the first culling split, a cascade or a cube face, up to six): a light's shadow caster list for a split draws the commands whose mask has the split's bit, a camera's ignores the mask (measured: masks 1 and 3 cast in the first cascade, 2 and 3 in the second, 0 in none, every pillar is drawn; cube faces and further cascades follow the documented rule, unmeasured). New ShadowDrawingSettings name no split (-1, the release's default, which Unbound had as 0); URP makes one list per cascade or cube face from such settings, and a light's lists without a split take its splits in the order they are made in the culling (measured for URP's two cascades; other orders of making and drawing lists unmeasured). A list whose split is outside the six a mask covers warns and draws every command. Procedural and non-triangle commands (Q559, brg `procedural`: every value and pixel on OpenGL Core and Vulkan, NVIDIA only; not recorded on Metal yet, `residuals-metal.json`): procedural commands draw their element count of vertex ids from 0 with no mesh (a non-indexed command's base vertex is not a first vertex: its ids start at 0, measured; Unbound draws them over a stand-in commands of other counts share, up to 262144 ids, warned and not drawn past that), or their index buffer's indices from their byte offset plus their base vertex, as triangles, lines, a line strip or points; quads, which the devices have no primitive for, draw as the device draws them (points on OpenGL Core, a triangle list over the ids on Vulkan; Metal unmeasured: warned, not drawn); procedural quads without a batch differ (brg `procedural_graphics`, the same on both devices): Graphics.RenderPrimitives and DrawProcedural make a quad of each four ids, and the non-indexed indirect draws (RenderPrimitivesIndirect, DrawProceduralIndirect) are not drawn, each logging the Editor's error "DrawProceduralIndirect can't draw quads if platform does not support quad topology" when a list would draw it (Unbound drew those as quads; CommandBuffer.DrawProceduralIndirect and DrawProceduralIndirectNow refuse them alike, unmeasured); procedural indirect commands take their counts and first vertex or index from their arguments; indirect commands read their mesh's indices as their topology (triangles, lines or points; a line strip and quads unmeasured: warned, not drawn). An indirect command's visible instance buffer window: a raw buffer device (Vulkan) refuses any window with the Editor's error ("BatchRendererGroup had non-zero ConstantBuffer mode parameters when BufferTarget is RawBuffer. ...", logged for every list that reads the command) and does not draw the command; a constant buffer device (OpenGL Core) reads the instances at the window's offset, which Unbound counts into the visible offset (the buffer is bound from its start: a start past the 4096 indices the constant buffer reads is warned about and not drawn, unmeasured). Unmeasured: a procedural command's invalid batch or material (warned, not drawn; the Editor's invalid-ID error names a mesh), indexed procedural line strips (the renderer reads no strip from a buffer's indices: warned), procedural commands' header mesh bounds (zero). Needed on the way: a mesh drawn as a line strip failed the device's validation and stopped the renderer (its pipeline now names the mesh's 32-bit index format). these conditions are unmeasured, the capture has one visible command of the default queue) with a pass of the list's LightModes whose declared variants lack DOTS_INSTANCING_ON, each distinct message once, its renderer skipping the pass (a pass whose variants are not known logs nothing; procedural commands, with no mesh to name, log nothing: unmeasured)), the window-offset message of devices without constant buffer offsets (none measured), the per-draw copy of the constant buffer window on OpenGL (binding the window as the draw's uniform buffer would save it: a performance follow-up). a type undispatched for 64 frames stops being tracked with the Editor's error); GPUDrivenProcessor takes the MeshRenderers GPU-driven rendering may draw (enabled in an active hierarchy, with a mesh, allowGPUDrivenRendering, no Animator culling by its renderers' visibility nor Animation with clips culling by them, no proxy volume, realtime GI vertex stream, anchored blended probes, property block, OnWillRenderObject/OnBecameVisible/OnBecameInvisible script, TextMesh, forced-off rendering, sorting other than the default or animated LOD cross-fading, every material's shader declaring DOTS_INSTANCING_ON; measured with a renderer per rule, and that an OnRenderObject script, additional vertex streams, an Animation without clips and more than 128 materials, with the Editor's error that says otherwise, do not refuse one; lightmapped renderers are taken, as the Editor's drawer takes them (residentdrawer `lightmaps` and `shadowmask`: the URP probe's baked Subtractive scene, non-directional, and Shadowmask scene, directional lightmaps with a shadow mask and a mixed sun's realtime shadows, their ground, wall and block lightmapped, images within 1; Q494): a command with UseLegacyLightmapsKeyword, which the drawer sets on every command, draws with the scene lightmap of its lightmapIndex (LIGHTMAP_ON, its directions and shadow mask; each instance's unity_LightmapST from the group's instance data); the drawer's indirect commands draw on Vulkan. Needed on the way: job struct fields' attributes covering the containers inside them, deferred arrays without safety checks, a job's own list view kept when scheduling bumps the list's secondary version, matrix swizzles of several elements and frexp/ldexp as FXC reads them (glslang mistypes the former and crashes on frexp with an int exponent). Measured with residentdrawer `controls` (Q450): an OnBecameInvisible script, a sorting group on the renderer or above it and a sorting layer other than the default refuse a renderer, a disabled sorting group does not, and a held ShadowsOnly renderer is visible (Unbound's drawer had held renderers in sorting groups). Unmeasured: a sorting group added, enabled, disabled or reparented above a renderer the drawer already holds (Unbound records no change for the renderer, so it stays held), whether the Editor's drawer takes realtime GI renderers without a realtime GI vertex stream (Unbound refuses them; a run-time renderer has no realtime lightmap), the TextMesh refusal (Unity adds no TextMesh beside a MeshFilter), an Animation with clips that is not playing (refused as one playing a clip), the Animator/Animation rule's nested hierarchies, renderers taken with additional vertex streams (drawn with their own mesh's channels: the probe's streams are the mesh's own), whether the Editor also draws a renderer of more than 128 materials on the normal path as its error says (it logs the error and the package's material count warnings at each of two dispatches, Unbound at one), LOD cross-fades (the probe's cross-fading group never fades), small mesh culling, material filters (GPUDrivenProcessor.AddMaterialFilters is warned and ignored), several ObjectDispatchers tracking one type (each keeps its own changes; in the Editor a second one read every frame left the drawer without the renderers made before it) |
| A transparent URP Lit sphere (alpha blended, set up by script) over an opaque object is darker than in the Editor and lacks its highlight | `tests/projects/residentdrawer` (recording) | S | done (Q450): not a rendering difference. The probe's Editor entered Play mode at once, while the Editor's start-up work was still pending (the switch to the project's gamma colour space; URP's re-import of every material when ProjectSettings has no current URP material version); that work reloads every material, and a reloaded material is validated by its shader's material inspector at its next use, so URP's turned the probe's run-time transparent material premultiplied (source factor One with _ALPHAPREMULTIPLY_ON; in a scene whose material kept them, its shadow caster and depth passes also go off). Materials made later, or in a Play mode entered after start-up, keep their script's settings, which Unbound draws: with the runner waiting for start-up, the Editor's sphere matches Unbound (residentdrawer re-recorded: at most 3 pixels over 8, at shadow edges; a scratch scene on OpenGL Core: no channel over 1). Measured on the NVIDIA driver only |
| The Editor's start-up material reload: Play mode entered while the Editor still starts (its colour space switch, URP's material re-import) reloads every material, and its shader's material inspector validates each at its next use, run-time materials made by then included | none | S | not reproduced by decision (Q450, Q466): Unbound keeps materials as scripts set them, as an Editor does whose Play mode is entered once it has started; only an Editor entering Play mode during its start-up shows the reload (measured: tests/projects/residentdrawer's earlier references, a scratch scene on OpenGL Core). On a URP 17 sample's demo scene (its materials as the Editor saved them) a capture entering Play mode after 300 Editor updates differs from one entering at once only along edges (no change of overall brightness: the temporal upscaler's jitter phase follows the Editor's frame count), so no surface changes there; probe runners that enter Play mode from a batch-mode method wait for the start-up work where run-time materials are compared (residentdrawer) |
| Render queue of a Shader Graph material as Play mode starts: the pipeline's shader tag was set only at the first render, so the first scene's materials validated against the default queue 2000 (a transparent graph material with `_QueueOffset` -50 got 1950 and drew with the opaques: refractive water read the opaque texture before the ground and went dark) | `Graphics/Srp/SrpCore.cs` (`ActivateConfiguredPipelineTag`), `Runtime/PlayerLoopDriver.cs` | S | done for Unity 6000.0.71f1 (tests/projects/graphqueue, windowed Editor that has drawn its views, NVIDIA driver): the configured pipeline's tag is active before the first scene loads, no pipeline is made until the first render; 9/17 values, 8 named residuals: the Editor keeps the saved state of this graph's materials that `Resources.Load` reads, Unbound validates every loaded project material (which materials the Editor validates on load is not resolved: follow-up; Q539 added a URP Lit control, `LitStray`, which the windowed Editor with URP does not validate either, so the rule is not specific to Shader Graph, while materialvalidation's batch-mode built-in Editor validates Standard materials; unresolved: the two captures differ in Editor mode (windowed or batch), pipeline (URP or built-in), OS and graphics device, and graphqueue's render and re-import before Play. [ShaderGUI.ValidateMaterial](https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.0/Documentation/ScriptReference/ShaderGUI.ValidateMaterial.html) says the Editor validates a material when it loads it into memory; this windowed URP Editor does not, a measured difference from the documentation). The Scripting API ([Shader.globalRenderPipeline](https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.0/Documentation/ScriptReference/Shader-globalRenderPipeline.html)) gives no timing: this is measured, not documented. Unmeasured: a project that removes its pipeline asset before the first render keeps the tag (no pipeline was made, so none is cleaned up) |
| A program's cbuffers bound as uniform buffers within the device's size (Q391) | `crates/unbound-shaders` (`fit.rs` `binding_layout`, `Compiled::uniform_bindings`), `crates/unbound-app/src/render/compiled.rs` | W | done (Q391, tests/projects/brg `forward_plus`, `forward_plus_plain`, measured on the NVIDIA driver only): Unity binds every cbuffer as a uniform buffer of its own; Unbound packs a program's cbuffers in declaration order into uniform bindings of at most 64 KB each (the size a device binds as one uniform buffer, WebGPU's default and the most an HLSL cbuffer holds), a cbuffer whole in one, each further binding 256-byte aligned in the draw's uniform data, so a program reads the same values as from one block per cbuffer. URP's Forward+ Lit (its tile buffer alone 64 KB on Vulkan, where URP sizes its light data for 256 lights) now draws on the Vulkan device, and with DOTS instancing on OpenGL Core, pixel for pixel the Editor's on both devices (they ended the run before: a refused bind group on Vulkan, a program that did not link on OpenGL Core). A program whose bindings a device cannot take (one cbuffer over its uniform buffer size, or more bindings than it binds beside the per-draw uniform, at most 8) draws with the error shader and a warning naming the shader, its keywords and its cbuffers. Open, as a performance follow-up: a constant buffer bound by a cbuffer's name (URP's Forward+ buffers, `SetGlobalConstantBuffer`) is copied from its CPU copy into every draw's uniform data (64 KB of tiles per Forward+ draw on Vulkan) instead of being bound itself |
