| Buffers held in structs (URP's Adaptive Probe Volume `APVResources`) and more than 16 textures in a stage (Q418) | `crates/unbound-shaders/src/buffer_members.rs`, `crates/unbound-app/src/render/compiled.rs` (`texture_bindings_refused`), `vendor/wgpu-sampler` (`gl-device.patch`) | S | done for Unity 6000.0.71f1 on the OpenGL Core device (tests/projects/shaderresources, 11/11, measured on the NVIDIA driver only): every URP Lit variant with `PROBE_VOLUMES_L1` failed glslang (a `StructuredBuffer` member of a struct) and drew with the error shader. A buffer member that every assignment in the program gives the same global buffer is that buffer (HLSL resolves a resource held in a struct statically); a member given several globals or anything else is left for glslang to report. The OpenGL backend binds the context's texture units per stage (32 on the measured driver) where it bound 16: URP Lit with probe volumes binds 21 (the pipeline was refused and the renderer stopped). A program over the device's sampled textures or samplers per stage now draws with the error shader and a warning instead. Unity's own compiler refuses more than 16 sampler states in a stage. A program binds one sampler per sampler state it samples through (Q468), not one per texture: on devices where programs sample through the samplers they name (Vulkan, Metal) textures sharing a sampler (`_BumpMap` through `sampler_BaseMap`, terrain splats through `sampler_Splat0`) take one, and a declared sampler nothing samples through binds none; OpenGL Core samples each texture through its own sampler, a unit each. The renderer's main texture group takes one more only in a program that samples the main texture through its own sampler (Q507, Q512): a program that samples it only through another sampler (an inline state) or only loads it reads it as a texture of its own without a sampler, so 16 sampler states, the main texture's inline one among them, bind on a device of 16 (a pipeline layout without the group draws beside one with it on each of the machine's GPU backends: `render::layout_gpu_tests`, with Vulkan's later groups set again after the gap, vendor/wgpu-sampler/PATCHES.md; such a program's material bind group is rebuilt when draws sharing it change main textures, not cached per main texture: queued), and only a program drawn as OpenGL shading language gives a texture sampled through several samplers a further texture binding for each (Q507; Vulkan and Metal bind it once). On wgpu's Metal device (16 samplers per stage) the URP sample's Terrain/Lit, water Shader Graphs, Lit with `_METALLICSPECGLOSSMAP _NORMALMAP` and SpeedTree9 passes were refused with 17 to 23 samplers; they bind at most 9 now and draw (measured on the arm64 Mac; the macOS Editor's own sampler counts are not measured) |
| Shader targets and GPU feature requirements per variant: `#pragma target` and `#pragma require` with and without keywords (Q394) | `crates/unbound-shaders/src/targets.rs`, `crates/unbound-app/src/render/executor.rs` (instancing keyword) | S | done (Q394, tests/projects/shadertargets on OpenGL Core and Vulkan, 139/139 each (Q463 controls included), measured on the NVIDIA driver only): a line with keywords applies to the variants with any of them (the Unity 6000.0 Manual, Targeting shader models and GPU features in HLSL); Unbound read the last `#pragma target` for every variant (URP Lit's `#pragma target 4.5 DOTS_INSTANCING_ON` made all its variants 4.5). Measured: a variant's target is the highest its applying lines give, in any order (2.5 without a plain target); plain 4.0 compiles as 3.5 and plain 4.6 and 5.0 as 4.5 in a program without geometry or tessellation stages, a keyed line keeps its target; required features raise the target on their own (`mrt4` 3.0, `2darray` and `instancing` 3.5, `geometry` 4.0 (keyed), `compute` and `msaatex` 4.5, `tessellation` 4.6 (keyed), `compute` with `tessellation` 5.0 (keyed); `integers` and `cubearray` nothing); `SHADOWS_SCREEN`, `SHADOWS_SOFT`, `DIRLIGHTMAP_COMBINED`, `DYNAMICLIGHTMAP_ON` make a variant 3.0 and `PROCEDURAL_INSTANCING_ON` 4.5; a variant requiring sparse textures draws with the error shader on both devices (though `SystemInfo.supportsSparseTextures` answers true; the keyed variant remains magenta with a valid Fallback or a later SubShader, with a direct fallback-program control); the `SHADER_AVAILABLE_*` defines are the variant's features and the device's own (3.5's on OpenGL Core, 4.5's on Vulkan, which the earlier per-target table left out), never the stages', cube arrays only through `#pragma require cubearray` (the earlier table, after the Manual's pragma target reference, gave them to 4.6), and `SHADER_TARGET_AVAILABLE` is defined; `es3.0`, `es3.1`, `gl4.1` targets apply nothing on these devices (the Manual's reference describes `gl4.1` as 4.0 with cube arrays and tessellation); a material's `INSTANCING_ON` selects no variant (the draw decides); actual `CommandBuffer.DrawMeshInstanced` selects target 3.5 versus 2.0 for the same program's `DrawMesh`, with the instancing keyword bit set; plain `require geometry` and `require tessellation` leave target 2.0 when their stages are absent. A target value or feature name Unity does not know is left out of every variant with a warning, once per shader file and name, naming the file (Q398; what the Editor reports for such lines is unmeasured). Unmeasured: a material's `INSTANCING_ON` through a renderer's draw (only `Graphics.Blit` recorded) and a global one, compute kernels (now 5.0's features without the stages', so `SHADER_AVAILABLE_TESSHW` is no longer defined for them, and `SHADER_TARGET_AVAILABLE` 45), the other features whose variants may be refused (taken as compiled), an unconditional sparse requirement that makes the whole SubShader unsupported, the stereo and device capability keywords (Unbound enables none), Metal (taken as OpenGL Core's features) |
| Indirect draws' `startInstance` and instancing cost | `Graphics/GraphicsDraws.cs`, `IndirectDraws.cs`, `CommandStream.cs`, `crates/unbound-app/src/render/executor.rs` | W | done: every indirect path (DrawMeshInstancedIndirect, DrawProceduralIndirect with and without indices, their CommandBuffer forms, RenderMeshIndirect, RenderPrimitivesIndirect, RenderPrimitivesIndexedIndirect) draws `SV_InstanceID` from 0 whatever the start instance, as the OpenGLCore Editor does (tests/projects/cmdbuffers `render.indexed.startinstance.*`); the arguments go through one pooled buffer without ending the render pass. Pool growth and reuse across 257 draws and a fragment-written arguments buffer are covered by `cmdbuffers indirect.pool.*`. Instanced calls copy their property block once and stream each array once per hand-over (render protocol 12) |

#### Rendering rules measured on one release

These answers of `Runtime/Compat.cs` give the measured behaviour to exactly 6000.0.71f1
(`Compat.RenderingMeasured`). Every other release, including other 6000.0 patches, takes the
unmeasured answer in the last column until a probe records it.

| Rule (`Compat.`) | Measured by (6000.0.71f1) | Other releases (unmeasured) |
|---|---|---|
| `RenderingMeasured`: world-space skinning retains its renderer's winding separately from the submitted model | samplerstates `RendererFaces.skinnedRenderer*`, `RendererFaces.commandSkinned*`, NVIDIA OpenGL Core/Vulkan | only the submitted model determinant is corrected |
| `RenderingMeasured`: Graphics and CommandBuffer blits execute shader passes without framebuffer GrabPass capture | textureformats `rt.padding.grab*`, renderer control; NVIDIA OpenGL Core | earlier all-pass selection including grabs |
| `DeviceTextureReadback`: device-specific ReadPixels conversion and signed RGB16 async clamp | textureformats `rt.readControl.*`, `rt.R16*`; NVIDIA OpenGL Core/Vulkan | framebuffer byte conversion, sampler padding and no RGB async clamp |
| `ImmediateMeshKeepsWinding`: DrawMeshNow and CommandBuffer.DrawMesh preserve submitted winding | samplerstates `StencilFaces.*`, `RendererFaces.*` on OpenGL Core and Vulkan, NVIDIA only | determinant correction as for renderers |
| `MeshInstanceBatchLimits`: mesh-instancing IDs restart at 511/1023 transforms or 64 KiB of properties | cmdbuffers `graphics.meshinstances.*.batch.*` | sequential instance IDs, with a once-only warning |
| `GraphicsFenceFrames`: a CPU fence reports passed two frames after it ran | cmdbuffers `fence.*` | passed as soon as the GPU has passed it |
| `ZeroIndexedInstancesDrawOne`: `RenderPrimitivesIndexed` with 0 instances draws one | cmdbuffers `render.indexed.instances.zero` | draws none |
| `SkyAmbientWithoutLightColor`: the ambient projection of the sky draws it with `_LightColor0` zero (any sky material; only procedural skies with every disk mode were measured, so leaving out the colour rather than only the disk is a hypothesis until a sky tinted by `_LightColor0` is captured) | indirectspec, Q105 ambient controls | the sky projected as drawn, sun disk included |
| `SingleSubpassClearFillsOutputs`: a subpass with several color outputs of which exactly one clears clears them all to its color | urp `subpass_mrt`, `subpass_mrt_cleared`, `subpass_mrt_mixed` | each clearing output to its own color, the others kept |
| `RenderingMeasured`: texture readback conversion fills missing color components with 1, for byte, normalized, half and float sources | renderapi `volumeReadback.*`, 2D and 3D targets | R16/RG32 normalized sources fill with 1; other sources keep sampler padding |
| `ShaderKeywordsMeasured`: the built-in keyword registry and types | shaderkeywords | every name a global keyword, with a warning |
| `RenderingMeasured`: automatic render-target mips become visible when a subsequent draw prepares changed framebuffer attachments; public GenerateMips rejects automatic targets | renderapi `volumeMore.*`, `volumeAuto.*`, 2D and volume controls | automatic mips update after each render pass; GenerateMips is accepted |

A Don't Clear camera's intermediate that does not take the screen's depth starts with cleared
depth (measured for partial screen cameras and explicit full-rect HDR-to-byte targets,
cameraexplicitdepth). Explicit targets with image effects follow the same rule unmeasured.

### Physics, particles, navigation, terrain

Done: PhysX 4.1 bodies, shapes, forces, queries (NonAlloc, Check, ComputePenetration), layer matrix,
simulation modes, collision/trigger messages, 3D joints, CharacterController, WheelCollider,
Cloth; Box2D bodies, colliders (incl. composite/tilemap), queries, materials; the particle modules
in ROADMAP; NavMesh queries/builds/agents/avoidance; terrain heights, collider, details.

| Gap | Where | | Impact |
|---|---|---|---|
| Visual Effect Graph: import and the API that needs no simulation | `Editor/VFX/*`, `VFX/*` | W | done for Unity 6000.0.71f1 with URP 17 (tests/projects/vfx, 1878 Editor values: the compiler's expression tables, exposed properties and their order, types, spaces and texture dimensions, events, the imported objects and their file ids, typed `Has`/`Get`/`Set` with the Editor's errors, texture dimension checks, object-typed defaults and serialized overrides, clones, event attributes with several typed attributes, what a resource accepts and refuses of a compiled graph and keeps afterwards, which asset type filters list a graph file, the manager's time steps). Documented (Scripting API 6000.0, [VisualEffect](https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.0/Documentation/ScriptReference/VFX.VisualEffect.html), [VFXEventAttribute](https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.0/Documentation/ScriptReference/VFX.VFXEventAttribute.html)), each with a control where the state had diverged: `ResetOverride` "restores the default value that the Visual Effect Asset specifies" (`scripted/reset/*`); `HasFloat` "checks if the Visual Effect can override a float with the name you pass in" and `GetTextureDimension` the "expected texture dimension for a named exposed texture" (`*/has/*`, `*/dimension/*`); `playRate`'s default 1.0 (`*/playRate`, defaultstate); `CopyValuesFrom` "copies the values from a VFXEventAttribute to the one you call this function from" (`attributes/layouts/*`, both directions between two layouts). Observed in the captures, not documented: errors for unknown names and wrong types, a texture of another dimension refused, an effect without an asset starting with `OnPlay`, the exposed list's order, and which overrides an asset change keeps (the saved ones and, over them, the current ones; saved when the component is loaded or serialized, as by a clone). The package's compiler runs unmodified; only its own postprocessors are told of an import (Unbound has no post-import pass otherwise). Measured with the package's quad output only; a Shader Graph output's material variant is built without the parent's GPU instancing lock (warned). Under the built-in pipeline the package compiles nothing, as in the Editor (not probed). A built `VisualEffectAsset` (an AssetBundle's) loads as a live object of its own with its compiled graph read from the build (tests/projects/vfx `bundle/*`: its events and exposed properties, an effect's systems and state): its settings, expressions and constants (objects among them taken from the bundle), exposed properties, buffers, events and systems with their tasks. The programs a build holds are compiled for its graphics device; Unbound compiles programs from their sources, so the asset runs with the programs of the project's graph at the path its bundle's container lists it under (recorded when the bundle's file is read, so an asset kept after `Unload(false)` keeps it) when that graph compiles to the same graph (the same expressions, systems, tasks, mappings, buffers and their layouts, events, exposed properties and compiled version), taken when an effect first runs it, and says once which graph it runs with; without such a graph, for a graph a bundle holds only as another asset's dependency (its container lists no path for it: the usual case of a prefab listed in a bundle with its graph pulled in), for a container name that is not the graph's project path (an address or another naming), or for a graph of another runtime data version, it does not run and says why (unit tested; follow-up: find the project graph of such a graph by other means than its container path). Also open: a bundle's prefab whose VisualEffect uses a graph of the same bundle file answers on the prefab asset itself as without a graph (its instances are right). Not detected: a project graph changed after the build only in values written into its blocks (those are part of its programs, not of the compiled graph): the asset then runs with the project's values (the log names the graph). Not measured: a player's built assets (only AssetBundles are read here), and the built compute shaders themselves (their documents are still listed as not read). |
| Visual Effect Graph: simulation | `VFX/VisualEffect.Runtime.cs`, `VFX/VFXSpawnerSystem.cs`, `VFX/VFXOutputEventSystem.cs`, `VFX/VFXParticleSystem.cs`, `VFX/VFXExpressionEvaluator.cs`, `VFX/VFXManager.Update.cs` | W | done for Unity 6000.0.71f1 with URP 17 for particle systems spawned from the CPU (tests/projects/vfx `sim/*` and `spawner state/*`, recorded in a windowed Editor): the frame's steps per update mode with the manager's fixed step and longest step, spawn contexts (loops, delays, counts) and their blocks (constant and variable rate, single and periodic bursts, attributes set, custom callbacks), the effect's random sequence, events (`Play`, `Stop`, `SendEvent`, the initial event, prewarm), `Reinit`, `Simulate`, `pause`, `AdvanceOneFrame`, `playRate`, the culling flags, `culled`, `aliveParticleCount`, `HasAnySystemAwake`, `GetParticleSystemInfo`, `GetSpawnSystemInfo`, `VFXSpawnerState`, `VFXExpressionValues`, and the initialize and update kernels the graph compiled to, dispatched on the GPU. The CPU side runs in managed code next to the API (expressions need curves, gradients, objects and script callbacks), the kernels on the GPU as generated. The kernels are written for batches of instances of one asset; an effect here is a batch of one instance (the data each instance's kernels read is the same; the Editor's batching of draws and dispatches is not reproduced). Documented (Scripting API 6000.0, [VisualEffect](https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.0/Documentation/ScriptReference/VFX.VisualEffect.html); Visual Effect Graph 17.0 manual, [Visual Effect Graph Asset](https://docs.unity3d.com/Packages/com.unity.visualeffectgraph@17.0/manual/VisualEffectGraphAsset.html)), each with a control where the state had diverged: `playRate` "a multiplier that Unity applies to the delta time when it updates the VisualEffect" and `AdvanceOneFrame` "if VisualEffect.pause is true, this method processes the next visual effect update for exactly one frame" (`sim/control`, frames 14-25); `Simulate` "fast-forward the visual effect by simulating all systems for several step counts using the specified delta time" (frame 26); `Play`/`Stop` send the play and stop events (frames 6, 10) and `initialEventName` is the event sent "when the VisualEffect awakes, or when you call VisualEffect.Reinit" (frame 30, `sim/scene Overridden`); `aliveParticleCount` "the sum of all alive particles"; `culled` "not visible from any Camera" (`sim/behind*`); Exact Fixed Time "update the visual effect multiple times per frame if the frame time is longer than the Fixed Time Step" (`sim/rate exact`); the culling flags' three options (`sim/behind`, `behind bounds only`, `behind no cull`); the prewarm's step count and delta time (`sim/rate prewarm`). Observed in the captures, not documented: an effect counts as culled until its first update and runs that update whole, in the first frame as later; a system that kills particles counts -1 until its count is first read back from the GPU, then once in 60 frames; the system getters answer nothing for a name they do not find and throw only for a particle system of the wrong kind or without running systems; a new `VFXSpawnerState` loops without end, and one of a system starts as a new loop; a spawn count of -1 or less asks for all the room a system has; a value a script gives a property stays, in the first frame as in any other (`sim/override *`, `sim/expressions others`, `scene`, `copy`, `late`); an effect disabled, or on an object made inactive, keeps its systems as they were and has its renderer disabled, and one enabled again starts over: after frames it was off in, its next update takes the initial event without simulating (no camera took its renderer in the frame before), and disabled and enabled within a frame it simulates at once (`sim/toggle*`); a value no expression evaluated yet has every bit set; `VFXManager.GetComponents` lists the enabled effects of active objects, one without an asset included. The expression operations are recorded through a spawn block written as a script, whose inputs are fed by operators over exposed properties and read with `VFXExpressionValues` (`sim/expressions*/inputs/*`: arithmetic per component and type with division by zero and NaN operands, rounding to even, comparisons, casts, bit operations, branches, fixed and per-evaluation random numbers, the effect's and the game's time, the spawn state, matrices from position, angles and scale, their affine inverse, transpose and product, and vectors through them), the blocks that set an event attribute through `vfxEventAttribute`, one spawn context starting and stopping another through `sim/chain`, and a texture a kernel samples through `sim/texture*`. `Matrix4x4.Inverse3DAffine` gives the same single-precision inverse and, for a matrix whose determinant's square is under 1e-25, false and a zero matrix (`math/inverse affine/*`, with determinants on either side and of each sign). Not measured: `resetSeedOnPlay`'s new seed on each play and the system seed (no control reads them yet); the update modes that ignore the time scale (the Editor's unscaled time under a capture frame rate is the wall clock's); effects created inactive or never enabled; the effect's bounds for culling are its systems' boxes with their padding (bounds computed from the particles: the bounds row); everything on the GPU was run on the NVIDIA driver only. An effect whose graph uses what is not done yet does not simulate and says what at load: a particle kept as long as the strip it spawned (`stripAlive`), mesh outputs as systems, and the expression operations without a control or not done: a matrix's axis (made only by blocks of initialize and update contexts, whose values no control reads), noise, a skinned mesh renderer's mesh, vertices and root bone, and a texture's format (no operator of the package makes it). A mesh's vertex, index and triangle counts, its indices, its vertices' channels sampled on the CPU (decoded from each vertex format; colours taken from sRGB to linear whatever their format; nothing for a mesh without read/write access, which keeps its counts, though the package's documentation says 0) and read by programs through its vertex and index buffers (Set Position Mesh on vertices and triangles, Sample Mesh), a readable mesh's buffers gaining `Structured` once it is an effect's value (vertices) or a program reads it (indices; the device's answer, measured on OpenGL Core only), the programs reading zeros of a mesh without read/write access, a texture's depth (an array of cubemaps: six a cubemap) and a buffer's stride (a program reads none of another stride) match the Editor (`mesh operations/*`, `draw-mesh*`); `SetMesh` and `SetTexture` refuse null. Event attributes read in a spawn context (Get Attribute, Current) are the spawn context's own as the blocks before left them, in this update or the last, an event's attributes replacing them and one without giving the asset's initial values; a loop duration read so is the attribute the context holds when it plays, and one read shared by two contexts holds what either evaluated last (`event reads/*`). A read reaching past the spawn event's attributes makes the effect refused. Matrices built from rows, columns and axes and split into rows and columns, the inverse of any matrix (a camera's viewport point in the world; a matrix without an inverse gives zeros), a frame's position, angles (not made positive, of its rotation as it comes: a frame without a rotation of its own gives one not of unit length, which a script's `Quaternion.eulerAngles` would normalize) and scale (of its affine part), colour spaces (by the sixths of the hue circle; a hue outside the circle is not wrapped into it) and a texture's width and height (zero for none or one destroyed while set) and a buffer's count (kept for one released while set; a buffer released before it is set sets none) match the Editor (`operations/*`); known residual: a frame holding a NaN gives an x angle with the sign bit set where the Editor's has none (whether the rotation of such a matrix or the angle step differs is not isolated; the bits of `Matrix4x4.rotation` are not measured for such matrices) (the main camera's values: see "Visual Effect Graph: cameras"). Curves and gradients are sampled where expressions are evaluated (`sim/expressions*/inputs/*/c00`, `r00`; a curve's segments as cubics in the time from their first key, or where the curve ping-pongs as a sum of Hermite terms in the Editor's order, which both round differently from Unbound's `AnimationCurve.Evaluate`: `AnimationCurve.EvaluateCubic`, measured between the keys, where the curve loops after them, on its last key and where it ping-pongs before them; whether `AnimationCurve.Evaluate` called from a script rounds the same way in the Editor is not measured) and baked for the programs into the effect's texture (128 half-float texels a row, four curves to a row and a row to a gradient; a row whose values are larger than a half float holds is stored scaled with its scale; which row and channel a curve or gradient gets is a layout of Unbound's, since the programs find it only through the values baked with it), which the kernels and outputs sample as the package's code does (`draw-over life` with curves replaced from script, one of a single key and one whose keys span more than zero to one, `draw-hdr` with a fixed gradient stored scaled, and `draw-loop end` with the last sample of curves that loop after their last key only: images, exact); a curve another object replaces is baked again. Not measured: baked curves that ping-pong or loop before their first key only, a sampled curve that loops before its keys or ping-pongs after them, weighted and stepped keys in a sampled curve, null curves and gradients, gradients of one key or blended perceptually (tests/projects/vfx README). A curve or gradient baked among the expressions of a spawn event is not done: the graph is refused and says so. Output events (`events/*`). Documented (Scripting API 6000.0, [VisualEffect.outputEventReceived](https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.0/Documentation/ScriptReference/VFX.VisualEffect-outputEventReceived.html): Unity recycles the event attribute set it hands over; Visual Effect Graph 17.0 manual, [Output Event](https://docs.unity3d.com/Packages/com.unity.visualeffectgraph@17.0/manual/Context-OutputEvent.html): the context sends an event whenever connected spawn contexts trigger it), with controls where the state diverged: every event of an effect hands over the same attribute set, holding the attributes of the event at hand (`same` rows, `events/kept attribute` a frame later). Observed in the captures: in each step, each spawn context feeding an output event that asked for at least one particle raises it once, in the order of its inputs (contexts of one name make one event: two inputs firing in one step raise it twice), the events of a step in the order of the systems and those of a frame in the order of its steps; the attribute set holds all the attributes of the spawn context that raised it (at their defaults where its blocks set none); events arrive after the scripts' `LateUpdate` and before the end of the frame; without a listener the step's events are dropped (a listener added later gets none of them); a listener's exception is logged and the step's next events are still handed over; a sole listener that removes itself leaves the step's next events each logging a call through nothing (`events once`); a culled effect raises none after its first update (which runs whole) and a paused one none until it plays. Not measured: a sole listener removed in a frame's earlier step while a later step of the frame raises events (Unbound drops that step's events without a log, as for any step without a listener), when, within the effects' update, the events are handed over (Unbound hands a step's events over at the end of the step, so a listener's `Stop`, `pause` or `SendEvent` takes effect from the effect's next step), a culled effect whose asset does not cull its simulation, events of prewarm steps or of `Simulate`, an asset replaced after events were handed over. A system that kills particles sleeps until it first spawns (`draw/over life`: its first count is asked for the frame after). A system whose kernel does not compile stops with an error and the effect's other systems keep running; without a renderer (a headless run) no kernel runs and the systems go on with the counts they keep themselves. Known residual of the probe: a coroutine waiting for the end of the frame resumes in the first Play frame in its capture and from the second in Unbound (the rule of tests/projects/eofstartup; what makes the two captures differ is not isolated). |

Open: outputs of a system that sorts one list for all its outputs (the compiler's global sort, for outputs that share their sort; refused), outputs of one system whose generated shaders share a name with differing sources (no label or letter: refused, as Unbound finds generated shaders by name; two graphs of one file name in different folders, whose shaders share names too, are not refused yet), lit outputs (the pipeline's lighting and probes per draw), motion vectors, strips through an indirect buffer that is not sorted (strips: the strips row), meshes, points, lines and cubes, Shader Graph outputs, shadows cast by an effect, and the `VFXManager` camera hooks (`PrepareCamera`, `ProcessCameraCommand`, camera buffers). Soft particles are drawn as the Visual Effect Graph: cameras row says (measured with outputs drawn directly; an indirect output's are drawn the same way, not measured). An output that is not drawn says so once for its asset with the reason (lit, soft particles and passes other than the measured ones are read off the shader the graph compiled to, at import: the pass list admits only what is measured, the two defines exclude what URP 17 is known to add, so another input a later package adds to the unlit forward pass would have to be added here), its system is simulated all the same, and `unbound doctor` lists the project's effects. Effects therefore cast shadows through their outputs' shadow pass, unlit outputs too (the warning that they do not is gone), an effect that casts shadows is culled by its bounds in the lights' caster lists as it is by cameras (one whose shadow reaches the view is not culled, `draw/offscreen caster`), and lit outputs are shadowed although their renderer says it receives none (the generated shader samples the shadow map itself). `VisualEffect.SetTexture`, `SetAnimationCurve`, `SetGradient` and `SetMesh` throw `ArgumentNullException` (naming `t`, `c`, `g`, `m`) when given null (`SetMesh` measured with a property the effect has and with a name it lacks, the three others with a name it lacks), and `MissingReferenceException` when given a destroyed texture or mesh; the property keeps its value (`null/*`). Mesh outputs also draw through an indirect buffer: one indexed indirect draw of each submesh the mask names, its arguments computed from the list's count by the package's arguments kernel with the submesh's index count, first index and base vertex, described anew when the mesh changes; pixel for pixel for unlit and lit wedges blended over what is behind them, which sort by default (`mesh sorted`, `mesh lit sorted`), opaque ones from the update's unsorted list (`mesh listed`), and a mesh of one submesh set in place of one of two while the effect plays (`mesh sorted` frame 10). An output of several meshes (or of meshes chosen by distance) compiles to one task per mesh with the output's shader: not measured, not drawn, says so. An output whose context takes a particle's size from a curve and its colour from a gradient over its life samples them in its own vertex program (`curve output`, pixel for pixel): the package's curve sampling keeps half floats in floats, which fitted programs may now do (unit tested); before, such an output drew with the error shader. Shader Graph outputs (`Output Particle ShaderGraph Quad`, the composed output) are drawn by the shader the graph compiled to: the render pipeline package's sprite graphs for particles, lit by 2D lights and unlit, through the 2D renderer, including the normals pass of a 2D light that lights by normals (before, such an output was not drawn: its pass 'NormalsRendering' was refused), and an unlit graph through the forward renderer, pixel for pixel (`sprite`, `sprite point`, `sprite normals`, `sprite unlit`, `graph unlit`); an output without a Shader Graph through the 2D renderer too (`plain 2d`). The 2D renderer draws an effect among sprites by its renderer's sorting order before its distance to the camera, as the [2D renderer sorting](https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.0/Documentation/Manual/2d-renderer-sorting.html) manual page says ("Unity renders sublayers in numerical order"; distance only within a sorting layer, a sublayer and a render queue), and a changed order takes effect (`sprite sorting`, frames 5 and 10). Observed: a graph given to `Output Particle Quad` through that output's own Shader Graph setting draws nothing and the effect's renderer has no material for it (`sprite classic`, the same here); the composed unlit graph output's renderer casts shadows (`renderer/graph unlit`). The graph compiler gives an output a material variant only when it compiles a graph for editing, never for an import: such an output stays refused. An output lit by 2D lights is refused where lit outputs are (through an indirect buffer that is not sorted, and as strips); an unlit Shader Graph output of strips is drawn, not measured. Open: lit outputs through an indirect buffer that is not sorted, and outputs of several meshes or of meshes chosen by distance: not measured, so such an output is not drawn and says so; motion vector passes (an effect whose graph has one has its renderer set to object motion vectors, so a pipeline pass that leaves such renderers to its motion vector pass leaves the effect's other outputs out too: not measured), and the order in which several outputs of an effect are drawn where it shows (the material list's order is the documented rendering order; the draws still follow the graph). | Visual Effect Graph: GPU events | `VFX/VFXParticleSystem.Events.cs`, `VFX/VFXParticleSystem.cs`, `VFX/VFXIndirectArgs.cs` | W | done for the measured cases, Unity 6000.0.71f1 with URP 17 (tests/projects/vfxevents: 477 Editor values and 79 images, pixel for pixel on the NVIDIA driver). Documented (Visual Effect Graph 17.0 manual): the [GPU Event Context](https://docs.unity3d.com/Packages/com.unity.visualeffectgraph@17.0/manual/Context-GPUEvent.html) "allows you to spawn new particles from particular Blocks in Update or Initialize Contexts" (controls: every graph spawns from an update context, `at birth` from an initialize one), and the [Trigger Event Block](https://docs.unity3d.com/Packages/com.unity.visualeffectgraph@17.0/manual/Block-Trigger-Event.html) spawns each frame, at a rate per second, over the distance a particle moves, or when it dies (controls `always`, `rate`, `distance`, `on die`; `On Collide` is not measured). Observed in the reference, not documented: a system spawned by GPU events is initialized on the GPU alone, in every step that the system spawning into it is awake: that system's kernels count the step's events and the particles that triggered them in a list, the engine computes the initialize kernel's thread groups from the count on the GPU (the package's own kernels, the entries shared with indirect draws) and dispatches it indirectly, after the spawning system's kernels of the same step; more events than the spawned system has room for are dropped by its kernel (`always`); a system may trigger several events into several systems (`two`) and a spawned system may trigger in turn (`chain`); the spawned particles take attributes from the particle that triggered (position, colour). The spawned system is awake while the spawning one is; its alive count is read back from the GPU as any killing system's is (-1 in the effect's sum until the first answer, then every sixty frames): the graph compiler gives a system spawned by GPU events particles that can die and a list of the dead also when no block gives them a lifetime (`keep`, `keep room`: its room less its dead ones; a system without such a list, which the compiler writes for one of an initialize context alone that only passes events on, is not measured and is refused). The list's count of all steps so far, from which spawned particles get the numbers that seed what they draw at random, goes on through `Reinit` and through the effect's object being inactive and active again (`reinit`, `toggled`: the sparks after either are not a new effect's), while everything else of the effect starts over, the spawned systems' particles and dead lists included (`keep reinit`, `keep toggled`: a full system empties and fills again); `Stop` stops the spawners and nothing of the events (`stopped`); a paused effect's particles go on triggering at every frame, and what they spawn neither ages nor moves until the pause ends (`paused room`: the frames after the pause hold the particles spawned during it; the graph's update context does not skip steps of no time, and one that does is not measured); with the block's `Clamp To One` off a particle triggers several events in a step (`unclamped`). Strips spawned by GPU events: the strips row. Not measured: `On Collide` triggers, a spawned system reading its event's count, an effect whose spawning system sleeps or is culled while the spawned one lives, several effects of one graph, another play rate and a time scale of zero, several steps in a frame (the exact fixed time step, a prewarm, `Simulate`), events into systems whose outputs draw through an indirect buffer, are lit or are meshes, more events in a step than one row of thread groups holds, the built-in pipeline, and other devices than OpenGL Core in the Editor. |
| Visual Effect Graph: particle strips, and when alive counts are read | `VFX/VFXParticleSystem.Strips.cs`, `VFX/VFXAliveReadback.cs`, `VFX/VFXParticleSystem.cs`, `VFX/VFXOutput.cs`, `crates/unbound-shaders` | W | done for the measured cases, Unity 6000.0.71f1 with URP 17 (tests/projects/vfxstrips: 859 Editor values and 99 images, pixel for pixel on the NVIDIA driver). Documented (Visual Effect Graph 17.0 manual): the [Initialize Context](https://docs.unity3d.com/Packages/com.unity.visualeffectgraph@17.0/manual/Context-Initialize.html)'s Data Type "Particle Strip: The system spawns particle strips" with "the fixed amount of particles per particle strip"; the attribute `particleIndexInStrip` is "the index in the Particle Strip Ring Buffer where is located this element" ([standard attributes](https://docs.unity3d.com/Packages/com.unity.visualeffectgraph@17.0/manual/Reference-Attributes.html); control `taper`); and of [instancing](https://docs.unity3d.com/Packages/com.unity.visualeffectgraph@17.0/manual/Instancing.html): "A group of effects that share buffers is called a batch", "Instancing is enabled by default, except on Visual Effect Graph Assets that use features that do not support instancing", "If instancing is disabled for an effect, the instancing system allocates a batch with a single instance", on an asset (Instancing Mode Disabled) or a component (Allow Instancing). Observed in the reference, not documented: a strip system has no list of dead particles: a new particle takes the next place of the strip it is spawned into (the initialize context's `stripIndex`, control `index`; of a system spawned by GPU events, the strip numbered as the particle that spawned it, `trails`) and is dropped when the strip is full or the system has no such strip (`keep`, `trails keep`, `index` from frame 40); after a step's update kernels the package's own kernel (`Shaders/UpdateStrips.compute`, run unmodified) moves each strip's first particle past the dead ones and counts the alive, for strips whose particles die only (`single`, `stopped`: a strip that empties starts again). A strip output draws itself as quads from each particle to the next, a segment less than each strip's room (`both` also draws the particles one by one; `image*`: the image stretched, once per segment, swapped); an alpha blended one sorts its particles by default and draws the strip from the sorted list, through an indirect buffer like any sorted output (`alpha`: a half transparent strip that crosses itself, each particle of a colour and at a depth of its own, so the order of the segments shows). Alive counts: a strip system whose particles die answers the count its kernels keep, read back from the GPU; one whose particles never die what was spawned, also what a full strip dropped (`keep two`). The Editor reads the alive counts of one system for all the effects of a batch at once: every effect of the batch then awake gets a new count, the next question comes sixty frames after the answer, and a system that wakes has one asked the frame after, which puts off a question that was due (`wakes`: four effects, three waking in consecutive frames, get their counts two frames later than `dying`'s; a batch in which some effect wakes every frame is, by that rule, not asked while that lasts: not measured beyond three frames): so the counts of the effects of an instanced graph change together (`single`, `reinit`, `toggled`, `stopped`; `dying`, `dying reinit`: a graph without strips), and an effect on its own keeps its own sixty frames (`alone`: its asset turns instancing off; `dying apart`: its component does not allow instancing; a graph with GPU events: tests/projects/vfxevents). Each effect keeps buffers of its own here: a batch's question is one request for each of its effects, sent in the same frame. The strip helpers of the package declare functions that return a `const` value, which the HLSL front end took for constant expressions: such functions compile as returning a plain value (unit tested). Not drawn, said once by the effect: strip outputs through an indirect buffer that is not sorted, a strip system's particles one by one through an indirect buffer, lit or Shader Graph strip outputs, line strips, and any output but quads of a strip system's particles. Not simulated, said at load: a particle kept as long as the strip it spawned (`stripAlive`). Not measured: additive strips, sorted strips under a perspective camera or another sort criterion, the tiling mode Custom, other orientations than Face Camera Position, `stripIndex` computed per particle, `particleCountInStrip`/`spawnIndexInStrip`/`stripIndex` read by blocks, strips of a sleeping or culled system, several steps in a frame, more effects of a graph than a batch holds (all effects of an asset are taken as one batch here, whatever its capacity), effects made after their batch emptied or while it runs, an effect culled or asleep when its batch's counts are asked (it takes the batch's question for its own, as its place in the batch does), and instancing turned off in the Editor's preferences. |
| Visual Effect Graph: bounds computed from the particles | `VFX/VFXParticleSystem.Bounds.cs`, `VFX/VFXParticleSystem.cs`, `VFX/VisualEffect.Runtime.cs` | W | done for the measured cases, Unity 6000.0.71f1 with URP 17 (tests/projects/vfxbounds: 351 Editor values, 344 matching and 7 recorded residuals, and 33 images, pixel for pixel on the NVIDIA driver). Documented (Visual Effect Graph 17.0 manual): the [Initialize Context](https://docs.unity3d.com/Packages/com.unity.visualeffectgraph@17.0/manual/Context-Initialize.html)'s Bounds Setting Mode "Automatic: Unity calculates the bounds automatically" (controls: every graph of the probe but `ManualAway`), and its Bounds Padding. Observed in the reference, not documented: the system's last update kernel reduces the box around the spheres of the particles alive, in the effect's space also for a system in world space (`world`, `world turned`), over every thread group of the kernel (`many`: 150 particles), into a buffer that is emptied before a frame's last step and read once a frame, when every effect's steps are done (six words into the system's own array: nothing is allocated in managed code, but each system awake waits for the GPU once a frame, where the Editor reads without waiting: one read for all systems, or one that does not wait, is a performance follow-up); the box read the frame before is taken then and grown by the padding the system has now (`padded`: a padding set at frame 20 is in that frame's box). So `GetParticleSystemInfo` and the Editor's `GetComputedBounds` answer the box of the frame before and the renderer, whose bounds are taken during the steps, the one before that; without a particle alive the box is the padding around the effect's origin (`stopped padded`; `stopped`, without padding, a zero box), a system asleep is not read and keeps its box (zero, without the padding, before it first wakes, when the padding it answers is zero too: `stopped padded` at frames 2 and 3), and the effect is culled by the box (`away`, against `manual away`); on a turned effect the box is along the effect's axes and the renderer's world box around it (`turned`). An effect's bounds are the union of its systems' corners taken back as a centre and extents, which rounds a single system's box in its last place (every `renderer=` value; unit tested with the Editor's numbers), also for bounds the graph gives (`manual away`, whose box has numbers that are not round). `VisualEffect.GetComputedBounds` and `GetCurrentBoundsPadding` (members the Editor declares for its own use) answer for a system that computes its bounds; the Editor does not survive the padding question about a system that computes none, where Unbound answers that system's padding. Recorded residuals: (1) the Editor runs the effects of one instanced graph as a batch, and where a system kills no particle the reduction's thread groups span the batch's effects: the first effect of a group gets the box of every particle in the group, each in its own effect's space, and the others none (`shared`, `shared second`); Unbound gives each effect its own box, and reproducing the batch's packing is a follow-up; (2) the box of a world-space system on a turned effect differs by up to 6e-5 along x and y at an effect standing at x = 1300, the last places of a matrix product that cancels, and by one unit in the last place along z: they follow the shader compiler's arithmetic (`world turned`, whose three rows are left out whole; `world`, not turned, and `turned`, in local space, are exact). Not measured: a graph saved while its bounds were being recorded (bounds computed, the graph's used: taken so here), several steps in a frame and frames without a step, paused effects, prewarm and `Simulate`, strips and systems spawned by GPU events, scaled effects, a system that falls asleep while its box still stands, the padding answered after a reinitialisation, culling flags that stop a culled effect's simulation or bounds update. |
| Visual Effect Graph: events wired to an Initialize context | `VFX/VisualEffect.Runtime.cs`, `VFX/VFXParticleSystem.cs` | W | done for the measured cases, Unity 6000.0.71f1 with URP 17 (tests/projects/vfxdirect: 236 Editor values and 28 images, pixel for pixel on the NVIDIA driver). Documented (Visual Effect Graph 17.0 manual, [Events](https://docs.unity3d.com/Packages/com.unity.visualeffectgraph@17.0/manual/Events.html)): "The Spawn and Initialize Contexts use Events as their inputs", and to "catch a payload in an Initialize Context, use Get Source Attribute Operators or Inherit [Attribute] Blocks" (every graph of the probe inherits the event's position and colour). Observed in the reference, not documented: such an event is the system's spawn event, taken when the effect processes it (in the frame it is sent from `Update`, also while the effect is paused: `paused`) and initialized in that frame's step: it spawns the whole part of the `spawnCount` its attributes ask for, as far as the system has room (`hit`: 2.7 spawns two, 0.5 none, a hundred the room, and a count below zero all the room; 2.7, 0.5 and below zero alike in `on play`, whose particles never die; `keep`: a full system takes none), one particle for an event sent without attributes (`hit` at frame 5), and the particles inherit the event's attributes (each event of a frame its own: `hit` at frame 15). The effect's start event wired so spawns when the effect starts, when `OnPlay` is sent, on `Play` and after `Reinit` (`on play`); one event may feed several systems (`two`), and a system may have a spawn context as well, both spawning in one frame (`both`). Event attributes belong to the asset that made them: `SendEvent`, `Play` and `Stop` refuse attributes made by an effect of another asset with an `InvalidOperationException` before anything is sent, for every kind of event, and take those made by another effect of the same asset (`shared`). Not measured: events to an effect that is culled, inactive or not started, or sent from elsewhere in the frame than `Update`; several steps in a frame; attributes inherited other than position and colour; strips or automatic bounds fed by such an event; an event wired through a subgraph. |

| `Simulate` without restart on a stopped system | `Particles/ParticleSystem.cs` (`StopSelf`, `AdvanceStoppedClock`), `ParticleSystem.Simulation.cs` (`Advance`, `AdvanceEmissionStep`, `CollectBursts`) | S | done for Unity 6000.0.71f1 (tests/projects/particleemission, 17243/17243 records; `longCall/false/*` and the added `stoppedSimulate/*`, `stoppedResume/*`, `stoppedLongStep/*`, `disabledBurst/*`, `mixedRemainder/*`, `stoppedChild/*` and `stoppedHeld/*` controls; `stoppedHeld/*`, Q397, recorded with the macOS Editor and reproduced byte for byte by the Linux Editor: a resumed system whose start delay is left counts it down while its clock is still at 0, each call of 0.05 s, shorter than the delay, leaving the clock and the rate where they were until the delay is used, the step that ends it advancing the clock and emitting by the rest; one whose clock a stopped call already moved past 0 keeps the delay for good, each call advancing by its excess over the delay only and emitting by that advance; matched as implemented): `Stop` with either behaviour stops emission whatever the system did before, also one that never played or whose GameObject was inactive, and only a playback start clears it, so a non-restarting `Simulate` afterwards advances the clock without emitting and leaves the system paused; `Play` then resumes at that time. A fresh system that was never stopped emits. A stopped system keeps what is left of its start delay (`StopEmittingAndClear` no longer drops it; one that never played takes its start delay first): a step no longer than the delay leaves the clock where it is, a longer one advances it by the excess. Only an emitting system whose advance starts at playback time zero counts its delay down (a fresh playback; its step that ends the delay spreads its rate births over the rest of that step); one resumed at a later time with delay left keeps it for good, advancing by the excess of each step (not at all with steps no longer than the delay) and spreading births over the whole step. A never-played Birth sub-emitter stopped with its parent stays at time zero. A burst cycle whose time passes while the system does not emit (stopped, or its emission module off) does not fire when emission resumes in that loop; later cycles do. Fixed-step and variable-step calls share one remainder: what a fixed-step `Simulate` leaves below one step, and the sub-microsecond residual of a variable-step call, join the next advance of either kind until a new playback (a variable-step call keeps the equal steps of its own time and steps the carried time last); with it a resumed rate counts the particle due just after a loop end. A random start delay of a system that never played is unmeasured |
| Sub-emitter rotation inheritance and particle storage | `Particles/ParticleSystem.cs` (`RefreshParticleStorage`, `has3DParticleRotations`, `hasNonUniformParticleSizes`), `ParticleSystem.Simulation.cs` (sub-emitter emission, `CurrentSize3D`, `InitParticle`), `ParticleTypes.cs` | S | done for Unity 6000.0.71f1 (tests/projects/particleseedaxis, 110/110 records; the 48 values of `manual_*_15_*` and the added `inherit_*`, `storage_*` and `rotation_emit` controls): `InheritRotation` adds the parent particle's z rotation to every child and its x and y rotations only to a child whose own start rotation is 3D. What a system's particles keep beyond a z rotation and a uniform size (3D rotations: a 3D start rotation, shape `alignToDirection`, separate-axes rotation over lifetime or by speed; non-uniform sizes: a 3D start size, separate-axes size modules; rotation speeds: either rotation module) is decided from the modules when the system updates (also by a `Simulate` call shorter than a step), emits from a script or takes `SetParticles`, not when a module changes, and not by a script's `TriggerSubEmitter` into it (a parent's Birth, Death, Collision or Trigger emission takes the storage the child's modules ask for: Unbound's choice, unmeasured). `has3DParticleRotations` and `hasNonUniformParticleSizes` report it. A child that never updated since a module asked for more therefore drops its own and the inherited x and y rotations, takes its x size on all axes, and keeps no rotation speed; a parent with uniform-size storage passes its x size. Particles a script supplies through the 3D setters (`SetParticles`, `Emit` with `EmitParams`) keep 3D storage until a playback restarts without particles. A particle emitted from a script has its rotation modules' speed at once. Unmeasured: whether a particle's speed-based rotation (`rotationBySpeed`) at birth uses its start velocity, and how `SetTriggerParticles` with 3D setters affects the storage (it does not change it) |

The gravity probe resolves 46 prior differences; all 58 random sub-emitter count records now compare exactly. Playback state includes the advancing module cursors. Open: scripted Emit newborn buffer order and the rate batch's 31 Birth lifetime permutations, Birth emission timing for non-constant parent lifetimes in Simulate and played Birth origins (inheritance in Play: particlerender inherit/*), and nonzero angular-velocity reference values after rotation scenarios; every raw difference stays visible. Done in Q83 (particlerandom inherit/*, lbes/*, override/*, moving/*; particlerender sheet/*): Inherit Velocity's and Lifetime by Emitter Speed's random values and the texture sheet's frame over time, start frame and random row take module salts like the other modules; Inherit Velocity Initial adds constant modes to the start velocity once and applies curve modes to the birth emitter velocity over the life as animated velocity; Lifetime by Emitter Speed maps the speed into the range as speed·scale + offset, its factor at least 0.0001; with a custom emitter velocity the emitter counts as moving with it, whatever its transform does: newborns sit back along it from the current position and emission over distance counts its distance (particlerandom moving/*). Open: a frame's distance births start as one batch in Unity, youngest first, per internal step in Unbound (moving/distance, same count and ages); the custom-velocity offset and distance when the time advanced differs from the frame's (simulation speed other than 1, PauseAndCatchup catch-up, fixed-step Simulate remainders); Inherit Velocity in a scaled custom simulation space (rotated only). Done in Q260 (particlerandom collision/*): collision bounce, lifetime loss and dampen draw from four per-system lanes seeded like the force lanes; each collision pass (an internal step's update, a start batch) draws, once it knows its N hits, one value per lane per group of four hits for bounce, then lifetime loss, then dampen, hit j taking lane j mod 4; the curves are read at time 0 (Curve and TwoCurves measured, collision/curve and collision/curves). Unspecified order: while a curve is random, the queued particles' trigger and Birth sub-emitter checks run after the pass's other particles. The playback state carries the lanes (Unity's restore unmeasured). Open: the Lights module's random values (not readable from script), other releases; a hit outside a pass (per-particle emission paths) is a pass of its own (unmeasured). See the new probe README for contracts, causes and scope. |

| `NavMeshObstacle` avoidance and carving | `AI/NavMeshObstacle.cs` (ObstacleSystem), `AI/NavMeshCarving.cs`, `AI/NavMeshAgent.cs` (crowd feed, rebuild) | S | carving done for measured cases (tests/projects/navmesh, `run.py unbound`: 227/227 `carve.*` and per-frame `holes` values): box holes are the outline grown by the agent radius with sharp corners (turned box included), with Unity's NavMesh vertices and heights around them; a change is decided after Update (PreLateUpdate) and cut in the next frame's PreUpdate (made in Update of frame N: cut from N + 1; in LateUpdate: from N + 2), a new obstacle is carved at once, carveOnlyStationary keeps the hole for a 0.05 m move, removes it for a 1.25 m move and cuts it again after more than 0.5 s, and turning carving off removes it; agents replan in the frame of the cut. How a hole splits the polygons matches Unity's (tests/projects/navpath `carve.sides`, `carve.mud`: every `polyX.*` piece and the routes round them; navcost `fans.carvebox`): the polygons a hole reaches are taken in groups connected inside the tile, each group's hole is the convex hull of what the hole covers of it, each polygon is cut by the hull's edges into triangles (an edge inside the polygon with the polygon's corner farthest outside it, fans from the hull's corners over the corners between) merged in order into convex pieces of at most six vertices; points within a 64th of a voxel are one vertex, and new vertices take the height of the last cut piece's source detail surface. Measured too (navpath `carve.rules`, 549 of 551 values): a capsule's hole is an octagon round its axis at its radius over cos 22.5 degrees (upright, turned, lying), outlines are grown with one point at corners turning up to about 90 degrees and cut across at sharper ones, an obstacle of no width or depth cuts nothing, holes are cut in order of their centres (x, then z, then y; the larger first round one centre), not in the order the obstacles were made, an obstacle carves the polygons whose detail heights its height span reaches, extended down by the agent height (a top 0.3 m under the floor and a bottom 2.1 m over it do not carve, 1.9 m does), and a turn counts as movement by its angle times the lengths of the obstacle's scale and world extents, a scaling by the scale change times the extents' length (5.7 degrees and 8 % of a 1 m box at threshold 0.1 do, 3 degrees and 4 % do not; 3 degrees of a 2 m box and of a 0.5 m box at scale 2 do, and 4 % of a 2 m box; the hole comes back in the 27th frame). Setting size, centre, radius, height or shape (even to the same value; shape only when it changes) cuts the hole again at the next decision as it is then, and that decision does not count a move (a stationary-only obstacle moving 5 cm a frame while its size is set every frame is cut every frame); a stationary-only obstacle that keeps creeping after a move (5 mm a frame) stays uncut, as it is still only once it stays within a tenth of the threshold of where it was last seen moving. Residual (listed, not gated: navpath `carve.rules` poly.p47, poly.p51): where an exact tie or the order of a cut piece's vertices decides how its remainder is split, the result follows the vertex the built tile polygon starts at, and Unity's floor tiles start at another corner than Unbound's in some tiles (measured: rotating the base polygon of the tiles at negative z by two vertices reproduces Unity's split there); the area cut is the same. Unbound's choice, unmeasured: a cut obstacle not also avoided. A carving change cuts again only the tiles the changed holes reach and replaces them in place; other polygon references are kept. Unbound's choice (unmeasured, as was the earlier replan of every agent): only agents whose corridor, link or polygon lay in a replaced tile rejoin and replan (managed NavMeshCarvingTests.ACutReplacesOnlyTheTilesTheHoleReachesAndTheirAgentsPaths), so an agent whose corridor avoids a tile where a hole is removed keeps its longer way, as Unity's does (navpath `carve.shortcut`: every value matches since agents shorten their path while walking, the walking-agents row). The blocked agent's route past the hole matches Unity's since the path-cost row (Q37); its remaining differences are the agents' movement (as the other agents'). Avoidance: partial, measured against Unity 6000.0.71f1 (navpath `replan.none` retains 17/578 differing values: coordinate errors of 1.4 to 4.7 cm, position distance up to 5.7 cm; the separate avoidance probe retains two failing groups, 3 configurations after Q401 and Q426): enabled obstacles are avoidance obstacles in the agents' crowds (box/capsule as circles, scripted velocity, height span; Unbound's choices, unmeasured). A box is at most 16 circles, each at most sqrt(2) half-widths (longer boxes warn once); only circles that can be a walking agent's neighbour are fed, and an agent whose 32 neighbour candidates still overflow with obstacles among them warns once; Managed validation found the six-circle 5.5 m wall can stall an agent outside the box; no Unity capture establishes parity for that case. |
| NavMesh path search picks another route than Unity where Detour's edge-midpoint A* costs disagree with Unity's | `AI/DotRecast/DotRecast.Detour/DtNavMeshQuery.cs` (UpdateSlicedFindPath, PlaceNode, PolyHeuristic), `AI/NavMesh.cs` (PathSearch) | S | done for measured Unity 6000.0.71f1 cases (tests/projects/navcost: every `strip.*`, `bake.*` and `carve.*` route sweep, 31 sweeps over area costs and path ends on baked and carved polygons; navmesh `paths.expensiveWater` and the blocked agent's route corners; managed NavMeshPathCostTests on the probe's strip polygons). Documented (AI Navigation 2.0 manual, Areas and Costs): cost is distance times the area cost of the polygon, nodes are placed on polygons, the result is not always the shortest path. Measured: a polygon's node is placed once, when the search first reaches the polygon, at the portal point nearest the node it is reached from (in 3D, kept 5% of the portal from its ends); later cheaper ways change its cost and parent, not its place; the heuristic of a polygon is 0.999 times the distance from the end to the polygon; `FindPath` and the sliced search are one search. Straight paths (tests/projects/navpath `cost.*`, `hole.*`): Unity's funnel tests the portal ends as offsets from the apex with components within 1e-4 counted as zero, makes a corner only where a portal lies strictly beyond a side (a path through a portal end exactly has none), tests both sides before narrowing either, and makes off-mesh connection ends corners where the path crosses onto or off a connection, also in line; a start within 2 mm of the first portal begins past it. Residuals: one carved piece of navcost `fans.carvebar` starts its triangle fan at another vertex than Unity's (listed, not gated: same shape; two polygon corners lie equally far outside the hole's edge and Unity's hull corners differ from Unbound's in the last float bit, so each picks the other corner); a line grazing a mesh vertex that the builders place about 2e-6 apart, on either side of the line, keeps its corner in Unity (navpath `two.p1`, listed); the straight path still drops a corner the path passes within about 1e-4 of slightly later than Unity in 8 navcost values (Known insignificant residuals). The runtime raster-bounds correction (Q376) resolves the two initial `replan.topofar` corner counts over a floor cut by thin water strips: `a.given` and `mask.given` are now 7 in both engines, with all 1687 values matching the unchanged reference. Measured (navpath `carve.shortcut`, matching): Unity's agent keeps its way round when a carved gap opens in tiles its path does not cross. |

| Runtime links (`NavMesh.AddLink`) whose end lies in another NavMesh tile than their start never connect (a runtime-built NavMesh is tiled from the world origin, so any link across a tile edge such as x = 0 or z = 0) | `AI/NavWorld.cs` (AddLinks) | S | partial, measured against Unity 6000.0.71f1 (Q361 ran all six navigation probes and 233 managed navigation tests): a link's end is classified against the tile its start lies over, as DotRecast's own tile builder classifies baked connections, so an end over that tile or one of its eight neighbours lands there (both ways when bidirectional); a start on a tile edge is added to the tiles on both sides, each copy classified against its own tile. Each tile also reserves three link slots per link end over it or within the link snap radius of it, for the ways back of the links that land in it: more than that builder's two for baked connection ends, because a start on a tile corner is added to all four tiles round it and up to three of those copies land in the same tile besides its own. Since the placed NavMesh data row: an end lands in the tiles it lies over (in each NavMesh's own frame), of any NavMesh and however far from its start (navpath `place.links`: a 9 m link and a 2 m wide link onto a copy moved, and moved and turned 30 degrees, over 5.33 m tiles); an end just past the NavMesh within the snap radius of its polygons lands on them. Open multilayer ownership and capacity scope is now measured by the Q385 navpath layers diagnostic, documented below. The reservation still assumes polygons lie inside their tile bounds up to float rounding and ground links stay within the builder's slot budget; neither assumption establishes a safe general capacity bound. The existing route and landing controls were run in Q361; their remaining query differences are listed below. Measured (Unity 6000.0.71f1, tests/projects/navlinks `crosstile`): a NavMeshLink added at run time across a baked NavMesh's tile edge (x = 42.67) connects and is crossed both ways, as in Unbound. Link ends (Unity 6000.0.71f1, tests/projects/navpath `link.snap`, navlinks `snap`, `snapnear`, `snapfar`): an end attaches to the nearest polygon within the agent type's radius across (2 m for a 2 m type) and its climb in height, every link alike; a partial path ends on the ground, never on a link whose far end is not attached. Wide links (`width` > 0; navpath `link.wide`, `link.wideoff`, navlinks `wide`): each end is a segment of the link's width along its up crossed with its direction, attached to every polygon it crosses and to no other (no snap across: an end 0.2 m off the NavMesh does not attach), the part over each polygon kept with the link; paths cross straight at their lateral offset, held to the parts over the NavMesh; an agent's nextOffMeshLinkData is reported from its path's crossing point, and it crosses straight across from where it reaches the link; a tile's links grow when a wide link needs more than it reserved (an end over more than 32 polygons of a tile is cut short, with a warning). A path leaving a wide link beyond its width turns at the far end of that end (`link.wide` a3: Unity's funnel swaps a portal seen reversed but keeps its points); crossings are where the path's line comes nearest the link's end; a wide link's ends attach in every tile they reach (`link.widetile`, across the tile edge at z = 0; a link starting on a tile edge is in both tiles, as point links, so its ends are attached twice: two equal connections). A ground portal seen reversed from the funnel's apex (behind it, in a U-turn) is unmeasured. Listed, not gated: `link.wide` retains two values, `gb1.2.cur.start.z` 5.984523 in the Editor versus 5.817135 in Unbound (16.7 cm), and `cur.at.z` 5.86303425 versus 5.817135 (4.6 cm). Correcting which landing polygon traversal uses does not resolve this approach discrepancy; see the Q342 residual row. |

`speedmid` takes a speed change during that approach on the next update, and `stopmid` pauses its progress and keeps its desired velocity until resumed (Q361: 9327 total failures against the unchanged reference, previously 13414; 223 managed navigation tests); past that its desiredVelocity is along the link at its speed and steeringTarget is the link's end while on the link; the update whose time passes the end carries the agent on past it at the crossing's velocity onto the NavMesh, and it leaves with a velocity of its speed in the direction it moved from the link's end (height included), which turns towards its plan at its acceleration; managed CompletingALinkAtThePathsEndMovesTheTransformAtTheEarlyUpdate); ResetPath on a link takes the agent off it at once and at the end of the next update onto the landing side's NavMesh nearest where it was, sliding to a stop at its acceleration, also through a NavMesh change (`resetmid`; Unbound's choices (unmeasured): area cost changes count as link changes; NavMesh data added or removed does not. Q462 renews the single-polygon corridor and clears staleness when an agent without a destination moves, including the pending transform synchronization after Warp or enabling; a settled unchanged position read keeps staleness until the next AI update. An arrived destination is retained and remains stale when moved.
