Agents brake by the distance to their path's end in 3D, from where they stand on the height mesh (the ramp and stairs walkers now match but for the ramp's last frame: `height.agents` 59 -> 18 -> 14/816 values; unmeasured: slopes without a height mesh, and an end offset vertically by more than two radii, which turns braking off). Listed, not gated (follow-up, `height.agents` 14/816 values): the bump walker, ending 0.14 m below its path's end, closes in on it more slowly in Unity (up to 0.09 m ahead of Unbound's mid-way, behind it in the last frames; Unbound's speed swings in its last frames), and the ramp walker reports vertical velocity 0.0210583284 versus 0 at frame 205; the arrival rule remains unresolved as recorded below. Q377 resolves the four under-stair Warp coordinates by retaining the requested transform move after placement: nextPosition follows it along the connected surface when updatePosition is enabled, and the transform follows at the next early AI update. Coupled, uncoupled and re-enabled controls match all 41 values in navbaked `warp.sync`; the original reference rows remain byte-identical. Unbuilt (warned): height meshes of runtime builds (NavMeshBuilder with buildHeightMesh: agents stand on the polygons), terrain heightmaps in a height mesh. Q361 resolves the walking agent's last two link-traversal values: `agent` now matches all 433 values. The independent `height.agents` 14/816 residual remains listed, not gated. SamplePosition matches (`sample`, 385 values; navmesh `sample`, 236): documented (Scripting API 6000.0, NavMesh.SamplePosition), the point is projected onto the NavMesh along the vertical axis before sampling extends to positions around it, so a point over a floor beside a nearer platform samples the floor; paths start and end on the nearer of the two, Raycast and FindClosestEdge on the one below (navpath `nearest.sides`); the area on area boundaries matches (the first polygon of equal height the query's bounding volume tree visits gives it, inferred; carved tiles have no tree, unmeasured). The agent of the additively loaded scene keeps its transform when the coroutine waiting for the load resumes (`agent2`, gated: the coroutine resumes as the load completes, see the asynchronous operation row). The raycast stopped by the area mask and the agent placed when its scene loads match (navcost rows). |
| NavMesh data added at a position or rotation (`NavMesh.AddNavMeshData(data, position, rotation)`, NavMeshSurface on a moved object) loses polygons from queries, and a turned one's tiles do not connect | DotRecast `DtSurface.cs`, `DtNavMesh.cs` (AddTile, RemoveTile, ConnectExtLinks), `DtNavMeshQuery.cs` (QueryPolygons, Raycast), `AI/NavWorld.cs` (Refresh, Place, Attach), `AI/NavMeshBuilder.cs` (BuildTile) | S | done for measured Unity 6000.0.71f1 cases (tests/projects/navpath `place.moved`, `place.adjacent`; managed NavMeshPlacedDataTests). Documented (Scripting API 6000.0, NavMesh.AddNavMeshData, NavMeshDataInstance): the data is added at the given position and rotation. Measured: a NavMesh of 4 x 4 tiles moved off the tile grid, turned 30 and 90 degrees, answers SamplePosition on every tile, rays across tile edges and round walls and paths across tiles as where it was built (rays, nearest polygons and tile lookups in its own frame; straight-path corners in line may drop differently in world axes, as Unity's do); NavMeshes built and added separately do not connect, even where their tiles meet at a tile edge (a path from one to the other is partial); a runtime build ends within one voxel past its bounds (the bounds rounded out to whole voxels, clipped to each tile). Each data instance is a surface with its own tile grid: queries turn their box (clipped to the surface's bounds) into the surface's frame and visit surfaces in the order they were added, tiles along x, then z. Agents walking round a wall's end take the shorter way a few seconds later, as Unity's (`place.moved` walk, every pose; the walking-agents row). Unmeasured: NavMesh data tilted out of the horizontal (queries rank by height along the world's up, agents walk in the world's horizontal plane); Links land on moved and turned NavMeshes (`place.links`); the route a path takes past a link's landing differs from Unity's in some cases (`place.links` m.p.ab, r30.p.ab, r30.w.ab: Unity's bends at a tile corner of the landing NavMesh; m.w.ba: which part of a wide end over two tiles it crosses), the same moved or turned, listed, not gated (the link rows). |
| `NavMesh.Raycast` stopped by the area mask reports mask 0 | `AI/NavMesh.cs` (Raycast), DotRecast `DtNavMeshQuery.Raycast` (`DtRaycastHit.blockedRef`) | S | done for measured Unity 6000.0.71f1 cases (tests/projects/navcost `raycast`, `raycast.agent`; navbaked `raycast`; managed NavMeshRaycastMaskTests): a ray stopped where the filter excludes the polygon beyond reports that polygon's area (also after crossing allowed areas, and starting inside the excluded area, where the ray starts on the nearest allowed polygon), one stopped at the NavMesh's border reports 0, a clear ray its end polygon's area; NavMeshAgent.Raycast the same with the agent's mask. A source with no allowed polygon within the query box (off the NavMesh, or on an area the mask excludes with no allowed polygon near) is no hit: false, position and distance infinite, mask 0 (tests/projects/navpath `ray.area`). Unmeasured: an excluded polygon beyond a tile border (found the same way). |
| NavMeshAgent writes its transform onto the NavMesh as soon as it is placed | `AI/NavMeshAgent.cs` (AgentSystem.SyncTransforms, Place, FollowTransform, OnAddedByScript, baseOffset, updatePosition), `Core/Component.cs`, `Runtime/Components.cs` | S | done for measured Unity 6000.0.71f1 cases (tests/projects/navcost `place.*`, every frame from Awake to frame 14; managed NavMeshAgentPlacementTests): an agent enabled by its scene, by activation or by `enabled`, or warped, is on the NavMesh at once (nextPosition snapped, also in x and z beside the edge) while its transform stays where it was until the next frame's early AI update (PreUpdate), which writes it; AddComponent and setting baseOffset write it at once; a transform moved by script is followed by nextPosition at once and written back at the next early update (the update after the scripts leaves standing agents' transforms alone); an agent not enabled reports its transform as nextPosition. Documented (Scripting API 6000.0, NavMeshAgent.nextPosition): the transform matches the simulated position when Update is called (so every agent standing elsewhere than its transform, also after a script sets nextPosition or completes a link, is written at the early update), and turning updatePosition back on moves the transform to it: measured (tests/projects/navpath `sync.updatePosition`), at the next early AI update, not in the setter; a transform moved while updatePosition was off (in Update or LateUpdate, with or without a changed nextPosition) does not move the agent, one moved again after turning it on does, and turning it off takes in a script move made before. A transform moved by script with updatePosition on (in Update or LateUpdate) is written onto the NavMesh at the next early AI update. Agents are placed within 20 of their radii across and 15 up and down from the NavMesh, as SetDestination maps its target (navpath `snap.reach`, `snap.reachtype`); unmeasured: radii below 0.5, scaled transforms, and the same reach re-placing agents after a NavMesh change (carving included: an agent in a new hole is moved up to that reach away). Unmeasured: Instantiate of an active agent (treated as activation), Move (writes at once, as before), baseOffset set while the transform waits for the early update (written at once), nextPosition of an enabled agent off the NavMesh (its transform). Walking agents move in the early AI update before Update (see the agent update row); the agent whose destination is set at frame 7 drifts in z from frame 12 (avoidance near standing agents, navcost `walk.later`, listed, not gated, as the navmesh walkers). |
| NavMeshAgents move after the scripts' Update (PreLateUpdate), so Update sees them one step behind | `AI/NavMeshAgent.cs` (AgentSystem.Step, PlanAtOnce, Rotate, LeaveLink, velocity, isStopped, Warp, nextPosition, remainingDistance), `AI/DotRecast/DotRecast.Detour.Crowd/DtCrowd.cs` (Update), `DtCrowdAgent.cs` (NextVelocity) | S | done for measured Unity 6000.0.71f1 cases (tests/projects/navcost `loop.*`: 21 lanes at four points of 20 frames, 15 gated, and `walk.*`; navlinks failing values 98403 -> 83752, its warp lanes 1014 -> 812 and 1033 -> 830; managed NavMeshAgentLoopTests). Documented (Scripting API 6000.0, NavMeshAgent.nextPosition): the transform matches the simulated position when Update is called; the player loop's AI systems (PreUpdate.AIUpdate, PreLateUpdate.AIUpdatePostScript) are listed by PlayerLoop.GetDefaultPlayerLoop, their work is not documented. Measured: the whole agent update runs once per frame in PreUpdate.AIUpdate: transforms moved by script are followed, pending paths found, the crowds steer, plan, move (by the plan of the update before), reach links from where the step took them and begin to cross them, transforms and rotations are written; AIUpdatePostScript changes no agent (a request pending in Update stays pending through LateUpdate). SetDestination gives the path in the call when it needs no search: the destination on the agent's own polygon, or, for an agent following a path, on the rest of that path (not behind it); else at the next AIUpdate (also for agents made that frame). The agent turns towards the velocity its next step takes (on an automatic link: along the current approach or crossing phase velocity, holding orientation when that phase is stationary; its kept plan while manual traversal waits) by (1 + that speed) times the angle left per second, at most angularSpeed, so it turns from the update that plans its first step. A velocity set by script is stepped at the next update and reported from then; isStopped slows the agent at its acceleration after the step already planned; Warp stops it at once; setting nextPosition writes the transform at once (Move as before); the update after CompleteOffMeshLink walks on; remainingDistance is infinite on a link. A stale path (navlinks `inactive`: SetDestination after its link was activated) waits for the next AIUpdate too, the agent reporting its current path meanwhile (see the destination row). Unbound's choices, unmeasured: the path given at once is the agent's corridor cut at the destination's polygon; a destination to the side of a walking agent off its path waits; one on the agent's polygon ahead of it while it walks is given at once. Applies to every release; only 6000.0.71f1 is measured. Turning control (navlinks `turning`: 1.5 m/s at 40°/s and 6 m/s at 400°/s): the rule holds (4 of 1604 rotation values off by under 2e-3). Residuals (navcost, listed): after a destination behind a walking agent, the turn while its velocity passes through zero ends 0.47 degrees off and stays so (`loop.redirect`, `loop.redirectOff`, rotation only, from frame 12; the slower velocity of the update that applies the path now matches, see the local avoidance row); a destination re-set to itself picks up a sideways desired velocity for three updates in Unity (`loop.redirectSame`, cause not found: the lane's own neighbours walk parallel to it); the turn after a velocity set by script is up to 0.13 degrees off (`loop.velocity`); see also the open rows below. |
| NavMeshAgent local avoidance: which neighbours and NavMesh edges an agent avoids, and how it samples | `AI/DotRecast/DotRecast.Detour.Crowd/DtCrowd.cs` (BuildProximityGrid, UpdateBoundary, GetNeighbours, PlanVelocity, AvoidanceShare), `DtObstacleAvoidanceQuery.cs`, `DtObstacleAvoidanceParams.cs`, `DtLocalBoundary.cs`, `DtProximityGrid.cs`, `AI/NavMeshAgent.cs` (SizeGrids, steeringTarget), `AI/NavMeshObstacle.cs` (Feed) | S | partial, with measured Unity 6000.0.71f1 mechanisms (tests/projects/avoidance: differing records 1060 -> 754, every set as good or better; navlinks differing values 46187 -> 27134, no lane worse, `fast` 183 -> 1 and `widetype` 148 -> 0; navcost `walk.later` 12 -> 0 and now gated, `loop.redirect*` 128/320 -> 36 (rotation only); navmesh differing scenarios 251 -> 216; managed NavMeshAgentAvoidanceTests). Documented (Scripting API 6000.0 NavMeshAgent: obstacleAvoidanceType, avoidancePriority, avoidancePredictionTime, desiredVelocity "including any potential contribution from avoidance", steeringTarget): agents avoid others and obstacles, more important ones fully. Measured (one rule for every lane): agents choose as neighbours the agents of a proximity grid (cells sized from every agent's diameter and speed within the prediction time, at least 1 m) with each neighbour inserted over its radius and the travel of its current velocity within the prediction time (Q385), around the square of their radius plus their full-speed reach within the prediction time, as or more important than them, heights meeting, not behind them along the way they last meant to go, reachable within the prediction time, the soonest 8; their NavMesh edges are the nearest 8 within their radius plus half their reach, none while the corner they last steered by is their path's end or a link within that reach; sampling keeps a desired velocity with nothing to avoid, else weighs the desired velocity first, then Detour's adaptive rings around 0.7 of it offset right, by 0.2 (desired), 0.1 (current) and 2/(0.1 + time to impact / prediction time), against each neighbour's velocity shared by responsibility (alone against a more important neighbour or one that does not avoid it in turn, half between equals) and against edges facing them (time doubled); a path given between updates shows in steeringTarget and the edge rule from the agent's next update (navlinks `incall`, `pendingold`). Agents without avoidance gather no neighbours (no agents, no obstacle circles) and are never pushed out of overlaps, although the AI Navigation 2.0 manual says for Quality None that collisions resolve (measured: navpath `replan.*`, agents without avoidance started at one point walk on together; `replan.none`, one walks through a NavMeshObstacle capsule); agents that avoid still gather them and avoid them alone (`replan.none`: of two started 0.3 m apart only the one with avoidance is pushed out). Unbound's choices, unmeasured: a stopped agent looks for neighbours and edges as far as its speed setting reaches; obstacle-circle footprints count in the measured prediction profile's grid size; box obstacles retain their existing circle approximation, unmeasured here; other releases retain agent-only sizing; agents of different agent types do not avoid each other (one crowd per agent type). Q385 prediction bounds reduce the avoidance probe from five failing groups/41 configurations to four groups/34 configurations: moving 5 -> 2, long runs 3 -> 0, speeds 18 -> 17; near 10 and overlap 5 remain. No newly failing record is introduced in any set. Baked-mesh agent records improve for climber 196 -> 3 and right 225 -> 1; 212 distinct frame scenarios remain, mainly walker steering and blocked corner timing. All 36 added prediction/finite-input scenarios match two identical GPU Editor captures, preserving every earlier reference row byte for byte. Nonfinite radius and velocity assignments log an error and retain the previous values; invalid or overflowing stored prediction inputs retain point insertion to keep the cell traversal bounded. `Compat.NavMeshPredictionBoundsMeasured` selects this mechanism for 6000.0.71f1; other releases retain the previous point insertion/input handling, unmeasured there. Q385 orientation and endpoint reporting subsequently reduce navlinks to 374 values, including crosstile 120; two now matches. Post steering remains 17 values. Navpath's post-avoidance residual is recorded below. The earlier mechanisms remain unmeasured on other releases; Q385 prediction and input validation apply only to the measured release. Q401: the reported steering target is the first corner found after the step (see the orientation, endpoint and stopping row); this takes the avoidance probe from 34 to 3 configurations (moving 2 -> 0, near 10 -> 2, overlap 5 -> 0, speeds 17 -> 1) with no newly failing record. The three left differ in steeringTarget on their second frame only (Unity 42.67, Unbound the destination at 50). |

Documented (Scripting API 6000.0 NavMeshAgent autoRepath, isPathStale, pathPending; NavMesh.pathfindingIterationsPerFrame): these concern paths made invalid by NavMesh changes and paths being computed; nothing documents shortening a path while walking. Measured: at every update a walking agent with one corner casts a ray to it, with more to its second corner when the straight way there costs at least 1 and less than the way through the first (the agent's own area mask and costs); a ray that reaches the point replaces the corridor's start with the polygons it crosses up to the last of them the corridor holds (`replan.vis`: across water costing the agent 1, not at 10 or with water masked out; a ray ending on a corner vertex inside a polygon off the corridor changes nothing). Once per update, of all agents, the one that has waited longest (walking with more than two corridor polygons, more than 0.5 s, the first in Unity's slot order of equals: freed slots are reused last first) has its corridor searched again from its first polygon to its last for 8 iterations, used only when the search ends within them, its first 8 polygons up to the farthest corridor polygon reached replacing the corridor's start (`replan.topo`: walkers turn back through the mud one per update; one given its path again at 0.4 s is searched with the others, its wait kept; `replan.topofar`: on a floor of many polygons none turns). These searches are not part of pathfindingIterationsPerFrame (`replan.budget`: at 1 iteration per frame, with another agent waiting many frames for its path, the same walkers turn at the same frames). remainingDistance follows the corners found again from where the agent stands after its step (`replan.topo`: the frame a step passes a corner). Unbound's choices, unmeasured: an agent placed again (Warp, a NavMesh change) keeps its slot and its wait; an agent enabled off the NavMesh takes its slot when first placed; a path request pending during the search starts where it was requested (whether Unity's starts from the shortened corridor is unmeasured). Not separately measured: the 1 cm nearest corner, the least cost of 1, the 32 and 8 polygons kept, and the shortcut's range (corners up to about 13 m away). |

| Stacked runtime link ownership and lifetime (Q385, Q519) | `AI/NavWorld.cs` (BindLinks, Unbind, Nearest, AddLinks), DotRecast `DtNavMesh.cs` (AttachesInData, LandsIn, ClimbOf), `DtMeshData.cs`, `DtOffMeshConnection.cs`, `Runtime/Compat.cs` | S | done for point links, Unity 6000.0.71f1, tests/projects/navpath `run.py unbound --profile layers`: 28 of 5826 values in 2 of 30 scenarios differ, previously 523 in 18. Each end of a runtime point link binds to the NavMesh data where it attaches nearest (Detour's nearest-polygon search and attach checks: within the link snap radius across and the agent type's climb, not the data's, up and down) and attaches only there; within that data it attaches as before. A binding lasts while its data stays, also when nearer data is added. When the start's data is removed the start attaches nowhere until data is next added, then binds again (here among the data added, or updated in place, since the removal: the measured case re-adds the same floor; a start rebinding to other data, or after an in-place update, is unmeasured); when the end's data is removed the end binds at once to the nearest polygon left; an end with nothing within reach attaches nowhere; adding the link again binds both anew (the rebind, addCloser and climb controls). Selected through `Compat.NavMeshLinkBindingMeasured`; other releases keep attaching to every tile over an end, unmeasured. Open: the eight-layer wide links keep reachability with 14 corner-height differences each (wide links attach along their ends, unchanged); navpath bent routes (23) and wide-link offsets (2) in the main profile are unchanged by this rule. Across data an end goes to the lowest Detour nearest-polygon rank, which for an end over a polygon subtracts that data's own baked climb; with data baked at different climbs that comparison is unmeasured. Links a bake generated with its data keep attaching in every tile over their ends (unmeasured). An end bound to nothing (a start only while its data has not been removed) tries again at every full rebuild, and when carving cuts a tile anew in which it now attaches (neither end of a link whose start waits for removed data's return counts there) (then the whole NavMesh is rebuilt and every agent rejoins it; a carving change where no such end attaches keeps the partial tile swap); how Unity binds an end under a carved hole is unmeasured. Other geometry changes, carving and releases remain unmeasured |

| AudioMixer Send, Receive and Duck Volume (sidechain ducking) | `Audio/AudioMixer.cs`, `crates/unbound-runtime/src/audio/duck.rs`, `effects.rs`, `mixer.rs` (docs/AUDIO.md) | S | done for measured Unity 6000.0.71f1 Linux profiles: tests/projects/mixerduck gate passes 19857774/19857774 values at 48000 and at 44100 Hz (master floats within ATOL `2e-5`/RTOL `2e-4`, device PCM within one step). Other releases retain the previous unmeasured weighted override selection and gain ramp. Tail-only/same-frame zone edits and contexts outside ZONES.md, LISTENER_SCRIPTS.md and MONO_LIVE.md remain unmeasured. Static Mono returns are also measured in MONO.md. CLOSURE.md lists the complete Q416 scope and open queue rows |

| Clips that animate different parts: legacy Animation blending and fades; Animator blend trees, transition timing; animated bools | `Animation/Animation.cs`, `AnimationPose.cs` (`WriteLegacy`), `AnimatorPlayer.cs` (`SampleState`, `Step`), `Binding.cs` (`AnimatedBool`) | S | done for generic rigs, and for humanoid clips that animate only some muscles, on Unity 6000.0.71f1 (tests/projects/animpartial, 1192 records: 462 from two identical windowed GPU Editor captures on Linux, 730 from identical pairs of batchmode captures of the macOS Editor). Legacy Animation: each channel is shared among the states that animate it (states at a weight of 1e-4 or less add nothing), higher layers first, a layer's sum counting as at most 1 and leaving the rest times (1 − sum) to the layers below, normalized to full weight, so a lone state at half weight plays in full and what no state animates is not written; a clip's rotation curves blend as they evaluate (unnormalized) and the sum is normalized; additive states add their difference from their clip's first keys, by their weight up to 1, to the result or to the current value (where it accumulates), on a Transform's position, rotation (the difference turned towards the identity) and scale only. Fades run at a constant speed from the update after a state is enabled and end when the weight passes its target; a state that stops in an update is sampled once more at its last time with a weight of 1.001e-4; Play stops the others of the layer at weight 0, Stop keeps the weight; a clip played once (or by default) fades out over the length of the CrossFade or Blend that started it before its end (Play: stops a step after it, weight 0); disabling the component keeps its states, whose time catches up when it is enabled again; deactivating the GameObject stops them. Animator: a blend tree child's share of a channel it has no curve for takes the default value with write defaults and the current value without; the state a Play/CrossFade request enters and the state a transition completes into check their transitions from the next update (the default state in the first). Bools (GameObject activity, Behaviour and Renderer enabled, bool fields) are true beyond 0.001 from 0; ints round to the nearest. The 6000.0 Scripting API documents only the fade ([Animation.Blend](https://docs.unity.com/en-us/engine/6000.0/script-reference/unityengine/animation/blend): "towards targetWeight over the next time seconds", other animations not affected); the shares, normalization and fade timing above are this capture's observations. From the macOS Editor's captures (records that do not depend on the OS; the captures' other records equal the Linux reference's): an Any State transition whose condition holds in the update a Play request enters a state is skipped there with the state's own and starts in the next update (`any.request`, against `any.plain` and `any.first`); a Once clip played backwards from its end (speed -1) plays to time 0 and stops there as at its end (`legacy.once.reverse`, also cross-faded in); every enabled legacy state fires the events its time passes, whatever its weight (`legacy.events.zero`: weight 0), also in the update its fade stops it (`legacy.events.fade`; before this Unbound skipped them), and Stop fires none; an update that does not move a state's time fires none of its events: its first after Play, CrossFade or Blend, which samples its start, and those at speed 0; an event at its start fires in the first update that moves its time (`legacy.events.start.*`, at weights 1, 0 and fading in, and at speed 0 for three updates; before this Unbound fired it in the first); humanoid clips that animate only some muscles (`human.*` on the humanoid probe's HumanA: alone, in transitions from one such clip to another and from a whole clip, in 1D trees, on override layers of weight 1 and 0.6 and additive layers of weight 0.6, with and without a humanoid mask, write defaults on and off; also tests/projects/humanoid body.partialOverride, bodyGraph.layerPartial): the muscles a clip lacks play at rest with its weight wherever it plays, matched as implemented. Also measured with the macOS Editor (Q511): a time set on a legacy state (time, normalizedTime, Rewind of one state or of all; measured with one state playing) makes the next update that moves its time fire the events from that time on, one at that very time too, while it plays (`legacy.events.timeset`, `legacy.events.normalizedtimeset`, `legacy.events.rewind`, `legacy.events.rewindall`; normalizedTime a fraction of the length, `legacy.normalizedtime.scaled`), in the update Play starts it and while it is held at speed 0 (`legacy.events.start.timeset`, `.speed0.timeset`; before this Unbound skipped an event at the time set on a playing state); Blend and CrossFade of a state Stop left at its weight and Play of a once clip during the fade out a CrossFade gave it before its end play as implemented (`legacy.stop.blend`, `legacy.stop.crossfade`, `legacy.once.fade.play`); SpriteRenderer flipX/flipY and CanvasGroup interactable, blocksRaycasts and ignoreParentGroups are true beyond 0.001 from 0 like the other bools and CanvasGroup alpha follows its curve, Animator and legacy alike (`flags.*`, `legacy.flags.*`). Unmeasured (implemented by the same rules): a once clip's fade out starting between updates (measured only where it starts a whole update before one: its first update moves the weight by the time past its start and then by the update's time), Any State transitions in the update a transition enters a state and interruptions in the update a request or a transition enters one (skipped with the state's own), a weight set, Rewind or a time set during a once clip's fade out before its end (a weight set ends it, and it starts again from the time past its start at the next update; Rewind and a time set leave it running), states enabled while the component is disabled, where an event handler sees the pose of the update that fires it (Unbound writes the pose first), additive Euler curves, and humanoid clips without muscle curves (body curves alone) on layers |

A Humanoid clip ends at its last key (31 frames at 30 a second: a length of 1.03333342, one single-precision step past the take's end), and the conversion takes ceil(span of the keys / frame length) + 1 samples a frame apart: for some frame counts (7 at 24 a second; 31, 61 and 62 at 30) that division rounds past the count, and the sample after the end halves the last key's slope without becoming a key. a property handle of a Transform property (`BindStreamProperty`) is valid but never resolves: its first read logs the assertion `Transform bound type not supported, binding: '<path>.<property>'` and the warning `Could not resolve '<path>' for property <property>`, and every read throws `The PropertyStreamHandle cannot be resolved.` (`jobTransformProperty.Rig`); all these recorded with the macOS Editor. Unmeasured: a component of a partly animated vector (it takes the default pose), a layer or an output of weight 0 over a channel nothing set, a channel the output keeps bound and one only weight-0 inputs animate in an output before, read through `AnimationStreamSource.PreviousInputs` (all read as set), a layer holding a mixer's weight-0 channel at weight 1 over a set channel (it gives the value bound from the scene, not the default pose), Write Defaults (it keeps the values bound from the scene), transforms outside the avatar, Animators without an avatar and the non-human transforms of a humanoid (scene values), a destroyed ancestor that is none of these (the values it last held), a skeleton node moved to another parent at run time (it keeps its path's node), and a Transform property handle never read (no message); a handle animates what its path names: an exposed transform bound with `BindStreamTransform` under its own path is the stream's own from then on and keeps the pose it was bound at with the job's writes, while one with a scene handle or none follows its node (`job.RigExposed`, `jobRead.`, `jobScene.`, `jobNone.`; unbinding the stream handles gives it back to the skeleton, unmeasured), and a handle whose path is a skeleton node's (bound before the hierarchy was optimized, or to an exposed top node) reads and writes the node, which its exposed transform follows (`jobEarly.Rig`, `jobTop.Rig`; after a deoptimization it is the restored bone's again, unmeasured, as is the handle of an exposed transform's own path after one, which keeps its own values beside the restored bone's curves). A stream handle of a humanoid's bone (the hips and the bones below them) is the bone of the stream's human pose (tests/projects/humanoid `bone.*`, twelve jobs over a clip, one per `HumanBoneJob.Mode`, on a rig whose root is scaled by 100; the Scripting API says only that a TransformStreamHandle is the "position, rotation and scale of an object in the AnimationStream"): it reads the pose's bone, also after the job set a muscle (`bone.read`, `bone.humanThenRead`: local values those of the transforms after the update, but for the twist the final pose spreads over a limb); a rotation written through it, local or world, reaches the final pose through the muscles the retargeting gives it, and the stream's body follows at once (`bone.rotateLocal`, `bone.rotateWorld`); the hips' position moves the body (`bone.moveHips`); a position or scale written on a bone below the hips is read back and moves the reads below it, and does not reach the pose (`bone.moveArm`, `bone.scale`). The hips read a scale that is the pose's body scale over their parent's, and positions below them carry it: on that rig, whose avatar holds the root's scale, the hips read a scale of 100 and a bone 0.1 world units from the hips reads 10 units from them (Unbound counts a root scale the avatar does not hold once, its own rule, in managed HumanBoneStreamHandleTests; the Editor is unmeasured there). After the job read the pose back the bones keep their local values and a further bone write still reaches the final pose, while their world positions are read under the root without its scale: the hips an unscaled offset from it (`bone.rotateThenHuman`). A handle follows the avatar the Animator has when the job runs (bound before the avatar is assigned or replaced: Unbound's rule, unmeasured); a rotation written is taken at unit length, a zero one as the identity, and one that is not a number is not taken (unmeasured). Unmeasured: bones of an optimized humanoid (their handles stay with the skeleton's nodes), an avatar with translation DoF (a written bone position reaches the pose through the retargeting there, warns), the hips' scale written (not taken, warns when it differs from the one read), writes with a read mask (every value is written, warns) and the read masks, bone accesses after the job solved the IK or reset to the stance pose, the hips' world position written after the pose was read back, and positions and scales written below the hips as a job above would read them (they are the writing job's). A solve after the job read the pose back works on the skeleton placed under the root without its scale, with or without a bone handle: on the rig's root scaled by 100 the goals are then out of reach and the body ends displaced toward the root, in the Editor as in Unbound (`bone.rotateThenSolve`, a lower arm turned and the pose read back, and `bone.solveTwice`, solved, read back and solved again, recorded with the macOS Editor; the same solve before any read-back, `bone.solve`, matches on every value; the roll split of the straight arm is the residual row `readback-solve-straight-limbs`). A transform below a bone that the avatar's human skeleton does not hold (a prop under a hand) is placed under the bone of the stream's pose, this update: its world values are the bone handle's composed with its own and the transforms' between through the stream, the bone's scale included (`bone.prop`, recorded with the macOS Editor in a job that only reads and reads the hand's handle first; after the job changed the pose or read it back, without a bone handle read before, and for the avatar's bones outside the human skeleton (twist bones) the same placement is taken, and writes through its world values invert it, unmeasured). A manual graph on a rig without its controller plays the clip, alone or under a job, on the plain and the optimized generic rig and humanoid (`graphOnly.*`, `graphJob.*`). An optimized humanoid with a stream handle of a path its avatar's skeleton does not hold plays nothing (`graphJobHand.HumanOpt`, the exposed hand bound with `BindStreamTransform` under its own path; `graphJobAdded.HumanOpt`, an object added below the Animator; `updateHand.HumanOpt`, the hand bound with the controller kept): the Editor logs `Transform 'Hips' for human bone 'Hips' not found` and `Object is set to Optimize Game Object but does not have an Avatar, Animation will not play.`, twice in each evaluation of a manual graph and three times in each `Animator.Update`, no clip, controller state or job runs and the exposed hand stays; with a handle of the Animator's own transform or none it plays (`graphJobRoot.HumanOpt`, `graphJob.HumanOpt`, `update.HumanOpt`). The Scripting API does not describe it. Unbound's reading, not measured beyond those controls: the error is the one `AvatarBuilder.BuildHumanAvatar` gives for the avatar's description on the Animator's hierarchy as it is, so an optimized humanoid that still holds every mapped bone's transform goes on playing; it plays again once the handles are unbound; an update of the player loop logs the pair once (the Editor's count there is unmeasured). Open: on the optimized generic rig with its exposed hand bound, a record that bakes the skin gets the Editor's error `The input bones do not match the skeleton of the Avatar` and vertices that differ between two captures (measured outside the reference; the job controls `job.RigExposed` and `jobRead.RigExposed`, which do not bake the skin, log nothing); Unbound goes on skinning from the Animator's skeleton, without the message. Unmeasured, with a warning: a humanoid's bone handle whose transform is destroyed reads the transform values of its path, not the human pose's bone, and an optimized humanoid that still holds every mapped bone's transform plays with a handle off its skeleton; a handle whose object is destroyed still counts as off the skeleton. | TextCore indexed pair queries and legacy KERN interoperability (Q498) | `Text/FontEngine.Pairs.cs`, `crates/unbound-assets/src/font_features.rs`, `font_textcore.rs` | S | done for the measured Unity 6000.0.71f1 controls (tests/projects/fontpairs: 2835/2835 stable observations; before 651/2835, with 3594 missing/extra/different values). GPOS arrays retain both glyphs and all eight value fields, lookup/subtable ordering, explicit zero pair-set records, both class-definition encodings and extensions. Array class definitions omit zero entries; explicit zero-class ranges are retained. All-record queries visit second classes before covered first glyphs. Indexed/list queries share a reusable terminated buffer with legacy queries. Legacy KERN is independent of GPOS and uses unfitted pixel values; scalar-glyph, list/table and new/all queries retain their distinct measured ordering and duplicate rules. An empty unindexed GPOS list selection falls back to incoming/outgoing KERN records in font units, even if another GPOS pair exists. Controls cover eleven licensed font derivatives, conflicting tables, reverse/self pairs, absent/wide/zero glyphs, nulls, sizes 13/37, cross-API buffer history and FontAsset full imports followed by legacy queries. FontAsset imports scale only the first x advance and preserve the other seven fields. Nonzero KERN format-2/3 controls return empty results, matching the Editor. Class-pair caches retain compact membership and expand only selected rows; a 10,000 by 10,000 class control tests bounded scalar and filtered queries. Compat selects the measured mechanism; other releases retain the previous unmeasured transport and report indexed queries as unsupported. Open: GPOS query flags vary between Editor repeats and remain unclaimed; existing textcore raw flag discrepancies are preserved. Multi-field scalar GPOS pair results also vary, including glyph IDs; that scalar context is explicitly unsupported, while the array queries retain all fields. No universal font/layout/pixel parity is claimed. |

VectorImage drawing: see its own row; TreeView, BaseTreeView, TreeViewItemData and the tree controllers (BaseTreeViewController, TreeViewController, DefaultTreeViewController<T>): done for Unity 6000.0.71f1 (tests/projects/uitkcollections, tree section, every callback, event and row): a hierarchy of item ids with an expanded flag on every node (ExpandAll and expanding with all children mark leaves too), visible rows = nodes whose ancestors are all expanded, depth first; ExpandItem/CollapseItem report even when the state does not change (an item without children does nothing); toggle presses expand or collapse one item, a row pressed with Alt its whole subtree; right/left navigation expands or collapses the selected items, else moves to the first child, parent or next item with children; selection by id expands the item's collapsed ancestors (each reported), not the item itself, unlike the Remarks' "will also expand the selected item(s)" (measured with an item with children); SetRootItems reports itemsSourceChanged, drops every expanded state (measured: an expanded id given again is collapsed) and the rows follow at the next refresh (the same list given again is read again); Move leaves the parents' TreeViewItemData children as they were, as in Unity; rows (indent, Foldout-style toggle, content) named after their classes; tree data (tree-data section): Move as documented (parent -1 the root, child index -1 the last child) and measured (a child index past the end is the last child; an item moved after itself among its siblings counts without itself; into itself, under its own descendant or with an unknown id nothing happens; a parent id that is not in the tree throws ArgumentNullException before anything moves, a child index below -1 throws IndexOutOfRangeException after the item went to the end of its new parent; the selection follows the moved item and is reported again; `rebuildTree` false leaves the view's rows as they were until the next refresh), AddItem (the parent's TreeViewItemData gets the child too; a parent id that is not in the tree adds at the root; an id already in the tree adds a second item, and the id then names the new one, each row keeping its own data; root items that give an id twice do the same; a parent whose data is null does not get the child in its TreeViewItemData; an item added at a child index shows in the rows at once, also without a rebuild) and TryRemoveItem (the parent's TreeViewItemData loses the child, descendants go with it); a move among siblings to a child index below -1 throws and moves nothing (tree-controls section); the rows and GetAllItemIds are a view of the hierarchy as of its last refresh (a move, an expansion, new root items or the tree view's refresh), so items added or removed without a rebuild show there later while parents and children answer at once; reading the rows between a removal without rebuild and the refresh is an error in the Editor and is not reproduced (the probe records the call's answer only); unmeasured: GetTreeCount with an id given twice, the --unity-tree-view-indent custom indent, dynamic height rows; the UXML `item-template` attribute and itemTemplate without bindItem (no rows, as makeItem without bindItem); rows kept across a controller change follow the controller that rebinds them (Unity would fail on the replaced one). tree reordering by dragging (`UIElements/Controls/CollectionDragAndDrop.cs`; tree-drag and tree-drag-positions sections, every pointer position's parent and child index as dragAndDropUpdate and handleDrop see them, the hover bar and its markers, every callback): a drop onto a row adds the dragged items as its last children and expands it; a drop between rows goes under the parent and at the child index given by the nearest rows that are not dragged: any depth from the next row's (0 below the last row) to the previous row's (one more when it is expanded with children), picked by the pointer's x in indent steps after the toggle when several are possible; a drop before the first row is first at the root, and one with no undragged row before it last at the root; several items move in tree order (an item's own child sorts before it when both are dragged, as measured) and keep that order; the view refreshes once, then itemIndexChanged reports each item whose parent or child index changed; an item dropped onto its own descendant moves nothing; a collapsed row hovered for 700 ms (panel clock) with the pointer within 10 points expands, and the end of the drag collapses every item that was not expanded before that (each item with children reports a collapse); while the pointer keeps resting after such an expansion, a collapsed item 0 with children expands after another 700 ms although it is not hovered (measured, tree-controls section, and reproduced); `reorderable` false (the documented default) and canStartDrag false refuse the drag after the pressed row is selected, handleDrop Rejected refuses the drop; unmeasured: drags between two views, MultiColumnTreeView (same controller; with a sorted column the drop is worked out from the rows as shown, unmeasured), auto-scroll while dragging a tree, the Editor-only move cursor; MultiColumnListView, MultiColumnTreeView, Column, Columns, SortColumnDescription(s) and their controllers (`UIElements/Controls/MultiColumn.cs`): done for Unity 6000.0.71f1 (tests/projects/uitkcollections, columns and column-tree sections, every callback, event, row and header element): a header container above the scroll content holding one header column per visible column (sort indicator, then the content: makeHeader/bindHeader or an icon and the title) and a resize handle after each (the last hidden when stretchable columns fill the width); rows of one cell per visible column, made for every column, then bound column by column; widths from the column layout (fixed and percentage widths within min/max, stretchable columns sharing the rest, Grow or GrowAndFill); hiding, showing, adding or removing a column rebuilds the rows; sorting (sortingMode Default orders rows through the columns' comparisons, a tree's items among their siblings; a header click sorts by the column, again the other direction, Ctrl adds it, Shift removes it; several sorted columns show their order; changing only the sorting mode to None or Custom keeps the last order; in a sorted tree each row's indent and toggle follow the unsorted row), dragging a resize handle, Grow mode as the header resizes, primaryColumnName; unmeasured, following the same model: percentage and min/max widths, the resize preview, header templates, icons. reordering columns by dragging a header column (header-drag section, with a MultiColumnTreeView control: a press captures the pointer and hides the resize handles; past 5 points along the header the column under the press is moved, with a preview at the pointer and a drop line in the view before or after the visible column under the pointer by the half it is in, after the last column past the columns; the release moves it in display order and rebuilds the rows, and is not a click; a move short of that is a click; Escape, documented as cancelling, ends the drag without moving, and after a lost pointer capture the release moves nothing; `Columns.reorderable` false, hidden columns keeping their display place, `Columns.ReorderDisplay`, a column hidden or removed while pressed); the header's contextual menu (header-menu section: "Resize To Fit", disabled without visible columns or with a stretchable column in GrowAndFill mode, a separator, one entry per column named by its title, name or "Unnamed Column_<index>", checked while visible and toggling it, disabled for the named primary column and for non-optional columns; `headerContextMenuPopulateEvent` with the visible column under the pointer); ContextualMenuManipulator, ContextualMenuPopulateEvent, DropdownMenu and its items (`UIElements/Controls/ContextualMenu.cs`; menu-api and manipulator sections): a runtime panel has no contextual menu manager, so a right click or the Menu key asks for no menu, sends no populate event and is not consumed (measured; the header's menu is recorded through a populate event the probe sends itself, as a manager would); unmeasured: a panel with a manager (Editor windows; covered by managed tests only), the macOS press rules; VisualElement.PlaceBehind/PlaceInFront moving an element forward now land right before/after the sibling as documented (sibling-order section; they landed one place early); Not implemented: columns, sort descriptions and other UXML objects declared in UXML (`<Columns>`, `<SortColumnDescriptions>`: reported once and skipped, the view is created without them); view data (viewDataKey) is not persisted on runtime panels, as documented ("View data persistence only works in the Editor UI") and measured (Unity 6000.0.71f1, tests/projects/uitkbindings viewdata: ScrollView offset, Foldout value, ListView selection); Editor-window persistence is not implemented |
| UI Toolkit VectorImage drawing and SVG import (`com.unity.vectorgraphics`) | `UIElements/Rendering/UaPainter.Vector.cs`, `UaPainter.Repeat.cs`, `PanelRendering.cs`, `Runtime/Serialization.cs` (importer .meta names), `IMGUI/GUIUtility.cs` | S | done for Unity 6000.0.71f1 (tests/projects/uitkvector: five SVG files imported by the package's own ScriptedImporter, 2.0.0-preview.25, as UI Toolkit vector images, 83 values and 28 panel images): the importer runs with its .meta settings (Unity writes an importer's fields without `m_` and with a lower-case first letter, at every level), every vertex, index, gradient setting and atlas texel of its output; backgrounds by scale mode (scale-and-crop stretches a vector image), size, repeat (each copy cut to the element, no pixel-grid snap), position, tint (multiplying 8-bit vertex colours), opacity; nine slices (image units, edges kept or shrunk together, the slice scale ignored); a vector background is the stencil mask of an element that clips its children; `Image.vectorImage` with each scale mode and uv part (triangles cut to the view); `MeshGenerationContext.DrawVectorImage` (offset, rotation, scale, mirrored winding); gradients through UIR's SVG gradient render type with a per-panel gradient settings texture (rows packed as two bytes the shader reads as high * 255 + low, that is row - (row >> 8), so images whose rows start at 256 or later sample an earlier row, as in the Editor); `GUIUtility.AlignRectToDevice` inside content generation aligns to the panel's pixel grid; a PanelSettings without a theme warns when enabled in or entering Play mode; `Painter2D.SaveToVectorImage` images drawn with their arc data. Q505 adds dynamic point/bilinear atlas pages with padded row allocation, GPU border copies, growth, reference-counted textures, removal/reuse, dirty/reset transitions and custom filters. Shared resized textures retain their allocation until released; dirty copies use current source dimensions. Bitmap text keeps its font atlas, including same-size glyph additions. Numeric values match within the unchanged 2e-5 tolerance. The two vector images improve from mean errors 0.167/0.292 to 0.010405/0.016556, worst 7/9, with zero/two pixels above 8. These remaining edge-coverage differences and the new controls' fringes stay visible in raw image reports; all prior image limits are unchanged. Measured on NVIDIA only. The importer's other outputs are done for the measured cases of tests/projects/svgsprites (108,891 values, 0 differences beyond four listed radial-gradient pixels): vector sprites (with and without gradients, pixels per unit, pivot) as GameObjects drawn with `Unlit/Vector` and `Unlit/VectorGradient`, the UI SVGImage GameObject, the textured sprite and the Texture2D (rendered by the package through `GL` and blits). The internal `Sprite.Create` without a texture, colour and texture coordinate channels kept with the sprite and drawn, sprites made without a texture drawn, the import's main object named after the file (Shaders keep their declared name), an importer's GameObjects kept out of scenes, the 2D Sprite package's hyperlink-click hook kept without windows, sprite IDs (imported from the .meta, or set), and the generated physics shape (`generatePhysicsShape` of a Vector Sprite and a Textured Sprite: the package traces its rendered texture through `SpriteUtility.GenerateOutline` and sets the paths with `Sprite.OverridePhysicsShape`). Open: the 2D Sprite package's sprite editor window registration still warns as unimplemented (a hand-written editor window type needs EditorWindow's default state first, tests/projects/defaultstate); the SVGImage drawn on a canvas; UXML `VectorImage` references measured by Q413: inline Image `--unity-image` URLs, absolute and relative, and Button icons resolve the vector asset. Nine added control images cover resolved pixel and percentage padding on fixed-size rounded ContentBox masks, a PaddingBox control, and a VectorImage overwritten by Painter2D.SaveToVectorImage, unchanged masks before MarkDirtyRepaint, refreshed masks after repaint or overflow changes, and a hidden parent with a visible child. Masks are retained with the element on the measured release; other releases retain the prior unmeasured per-paint path |

space-around with no in-flow items gives an infinite position, kept) (also a tab header's underline). Layout (tests/projects/uitkcollections, columns section): the first child that both grows and shrinks takes a zero flex basis unless a flexible child follows it (flexible children before it that do not grow do not count); an absolute child with display: none keeps a zero layout; a node with overflow scroll measured with an at-most main size keeps its content's size on its main axis (its cross axis is clamped), while a hidden box measures its items within its size (a label in a 20-point cell is measured at most 14 high). Animated reordering (ListViewReorderMode.Animated; tests/projects/uitkcollections animated section, each drag started once earlier gaps settled): the dragged row is lifted under the pointer and a ghost keeps its place, the row it would drop before opens a gap of its height (top padding animated over 500 ms), dropping moves the item to the gap and rebinds the rows as Unity does; unmeasured: drags in a scrolled list or in a dynamic-height list (unit tests check that rows and heights stay consistent). settings, model scale/blend shapes/generic rigs, AssetBundles, Addressables (AssetDatabase mode),
ScriptedImporters, UnityWebRequest core (multipart, certificates, timeouts, file, texture).

| `generateSecondaryUV` (lightmap UVs) | `crates/unbound-assets/src/model.rs`, `model/lightmap_uv.rs`, `model/lightmap_uv/` | S | partial: reproduced for connected parts of any size, charts that touch themselves at a vertex, charts that overlap themselves, and degenerate and non-manifold geometry (tests/projects/lightmapuvs, Unity 6000.0.71f1: UV2 per triangle corner and the vertex count match exactly for Cube, Multi, Pieces, Sphere (720 triangles), Ico (320), Barrel (1008), Rock (5120), Compound, Twins and Cylinder (a large part after another part of one mesh) in all eight variants, Grid and TwoUV in both UV2 and vertex count after normal normalization and attribute splitting; the Torus matches as well). The importer builds the unwrap's input as the Editor is inferred to hold the mesh (control points in file order, an OBJ's in first-use order, equal positions welded; triangles in face order, each its own source; UV0 per corner), derives the pack margin (manual, or calculated from the mesh area, resolution and object scale; the packer halves a margin too large for the charts), then fits the mesh into a unit cube and welds it, segments each connected part into charts (a part of more than 100 triangles first on a copy simplified to a tenth by quadric-ordered vertex collapses, then refined back in three steps, with the Editor's numbering of the coarse facets by position in the part), lays each chart flat from corrected angles by least squares (a vertex where the chart meets itself split per fan, the corners keeping the first fan's UV), cuts a chart that overlaps itself into connected pieces, packs the charts, and splits vertices on the generated UV2. Degenerate triangles are removed before the unwrap and take the UV2 of a neighbour's corners, non-manifold edges and vertices are split, and a mesh with no triangle left is imported empty, as in the Editor (the Repair variant: twelve models match; the empty mesh is measured for one mesh of one sub-mesh). Open: equal-sized charts can be packed in each other's places (Organic at hard angle 30: 4 single-triangle charts, 12 corners up to 0.034 apart; studied and left: the swapped charts' boxes are equal in width and one or two single-precision steps apart in height here, so their order in the packer follows the last step of the box computation; sorting the boxes another way or comparing them in single precision matched fewer recorded meshes (Q475, on macOS against the same reference: single-precision boxes, subtracted or rounded, with a stable sort or an emulation of libstdc++'s `std::sort` break 48 or 49 meshes, height before width 84; no rule found); the charts trade places and the lightmap is used the same); with `weldVertices` off the unwrap gets every point as its own (lightmapuvs NoWeld: 14 of 15 models match by index, Organic has one more packing tie of two single-triangle charts, 6 values 0.067 apart; NoWeldOff and NoWeldRaw match in all 30; measured on FBX, an OBJ's points come in first-use order and are not measured without welding); other unwrap failures (charts that do not fit, positions that are not finite) still warn and keep the file's UV2; not measured: the empty import with mesh optimization off, several sub-meshes, blend shapes or skinning, and a mesh smaller than 1e-6 (it welds to nothing and is imported empty as well). L in baked scenes |

The same sweep triangles feed normal calculation, vertex splitting and lightmap UV generation. Imperfect faces import as the Editor does (Bridge, BridgeObj, Pinch, Flat*, Weld*, Cross): coincident n-gon corners merge, zero-area n-gons give no triangles, triangles and quads folded by welding are dropped, self-intersecting polygons are discarded with a warning, and unused control points keep vertices when mesh optimization is off. Measured on FBX with weldVertices on and off (lightmapuvs NoWeldOff, NoWeldRaw: Bridge, Cross, FlatPoly, Pinch, WeldDiag, WeldQuad, WeldTri); the same rules are applied to OBJ files (unmeasured there with weldVertices off). | Imported mesh optimization order | `crates/unbound-assets/src/model.rs`, `model/mesh_order.rs`, `model/obj_groups.rs` | S | done for Unity 6000.0.71f1, tests/projects/lightmapuvs: `meshOptimizationFlags` reorders each sub-mesh's triangles for a 16-entry vertex cache (PolygonOrder) and renumbers vertices by first use (VertexOrder); with both, either or neither, every recorded mesh matches the Editor in triangles, vertices, UV and UV2 by index (616 of 617 records; the other is the chart packing tie of the row above). Not measured: sub-meshes of more than a million triangles (ordered in equal parts), quad topology (`keepQuads`), skinned meshes and blend shapes by index (they are reordered with their vertices; the blendshapes and modelaxis probes still match), and triangles naming a vertex twice (the importer drops them first). A meta from before the flags (`optimizeMeshForGPU`, lightmapuvs OldOptOff and OldOptOn) keeps the polygon order and turns the vertex order on or off; a meta with neither setting is read as everything (not measured). The polygon order looks at every remaining triangle of the vertices in the cache, so its time grows with the square of the triangles around one vertex: a fan of a hundred thousand triangles takes seconds. Also done (lightmapuvs Names, 80 rows): an OBJ file's meshes follow its `g` groups (objects are not read, `default` without a group, words joined by underscores, a returning or differently cased name is the same mesh, points no face names are dropped), every OBJ mesh is a child of the root, and `sortHierarchyByName` orders siblings without ASCII case and with numbers as numbers for OBJ and FBX alike. Names equal to that order (letter case, leading zeros at the same length) end where the Linux Editor's unstable sort leaves them (`unbound_compat::EditorSort`, GNU libstdc++'s introsort, on every OS: lightmapuvs OrderFbx and Ties, 28 siblings in a third order; the macOS Editor's libc++ orders are not reproduced, Q456); the lightmap UV unwrap's chart borders and the realtime GI system packing take the same sort. Only 6000.0.71f1 is measured; other releases are assumed to use the same algorithms. Not gathered, with a warning: a returning group whose faces without a material would follow faces with one, and statements continued over lines. Not measured: group names with tabs, repeated spaces, trailing comments or letters outside ASCII; files the loader reads by an exporter's own conventions (it joins the groups of files marked as ZBrush exports). |

The face normals' reciprocal is the processor's estimate refined twice, so the Editor's normals differ between architectures in their last bits (the macOS arm64 Editor differs from the Linux reference in 11,264 of 168,664 normals of the probe): x86_64 refines SSE's estimate as the Linux Editor does; other processors divide, and on macOS the probe's normals differ from the Linux reference in their last bits (211/420 cases exact, 142 recorded residuals, 67 cases outside them; not chased, Q456, model cache VERSION 56). OBJ `s` statements follow the importer (`s on` keeps the group, `s 1.5` is 1, `s -1` all bits) and faces are ordered by group; faces without a material use `<group>Mat` with the class defaults. | `SpriteMeshType.Tight` outline; default physics shape | `crates/unbound-assets/src/sprite_mesh.rs`, `sprite.rs`, `crates/unbound-runtime/src/state.rs`, `Graphics/Sprite.cs`, `Graphics/Renderer.cs`, `Editor/SpriteUtility.cs`, `Physics2D/Collider2D.cs`, `Physics2D/TilemapCollider2D.cs` | S | done for the measured Unity 6000.0.71f1 import (tests/projects/spriteimport: 650/650 fields over 65 sprites, exactly as f32; 519/520 before). A Polygon-mode sprite's mesh is always Tight (mesh type and the 32-pixel minimum do not apply), and its default physics shape is one path through the vertices of its mesh's boundary edges, in the order of an edge hash table whose shape reproduces every Polygon control: separate islands and holes follow each other in that one path; a zero-area outline renders the quad and takes its boundary (a texture without traceable alpha or a rect outside the texture take the same quad, unmeasured). Thirteen Polygon controls (concave with an off-centre pivot, islands, hole, 60 vertices, two loops of 160 edges that pin that table's shape, custom and disabled shapes, Full Rect, under 32 pixels, no outline, zero-area outline). Runtime `Sprite.Create` traces the texture as the importer does (tests/projects/spriteoutlines, 12,059 values, zero differences; two independent macOS Editor captures identical, equal to the earlier Linux Editor reference in its 5,332 values): the Tight mesh and texture rect (a rect under 32 pixels on a side stays a quad; extrude 0 and 1 trace alike), and with `generateFallbackPhysicsShape` the traced physics shape; readable and unreadable imported textures alike, and the texels the CPU holds when a script wrote them: a new texture before and after `Apply`, one `Apply(false, true)` made non-readable, an imported readable texture rewritten by `SetPixels32` (applied or not). Only the rect's texels are handed to the trace; an imported texture nothing rewrote is traced from its import (floating-point textures unmeasured: an imported one is not traced, one a script made is traced from its 8-bit reading). A PolygonCollider2D added in Play mode to a sprite's object takes the sprite's physics shape, else the outline a generated physics shape would have (also for Full Rect sprites; holes give extra paths); a TilemapCollider2D's sprite tiles take the same outline (convex outlines measured; the decomposition of an outline with holes into tile shapes is not part of the probe). `UnityEditor.Sprites.SpriteUtility.GenerateOutline` traces a texture rect with its detail, alpha tolerance and hole detection, in pixels from the rect's centre, and `Sprite.OverridePhysicsShape` takes paths in rect space to units from the pivot (a null path throws `ArgumentNullException` and one under 3 points `ArgumentException`, before any path is set; no paths clear the shape; `GenerateOutline` of no texture throws `ArgumentNullException`); the SVG importer's generated physics shape uses both (tests/projects/svgsprites). `SpriteUtility.GenerateOutlineFromSprite` (the PSD and Aseprite importers' generated physics shapes) traces the sprite's rect with its arguments in units from its pivot (the generated physics shape with the importers' arguments); a Polygon-mode sprite's is its mesh's boundary, whatever the arguments; no sprite throws `ArgumentNullException`. Colliders added to an object with a renderer fit its local bounds (through the renderer's matrix into the object's space, so flips and Sliced or Tiled sizes count): a Circle takes the larger half extent, a Capsule the bounds, an Edge a level line across them (the default edge for bounds narrower than about 1.005e-4: 1.004e-4 measured default, 1.006e-4 level, with the centre at 0.5 only; ends that are not finite at the origin), a Polygon the sprite's outline unflipped, else a pentagon of the larger half extent around their centre; a Box takes a sprite renderer's sprite bounds in Simple drawing, flipped, whatever the draw mode (set local bounds instead when there are some; no sprite: the smallest size); an offset whose centre is not finite is zero; without a renderer or with empty bounds the defaults stay. A sprite renderer's local bounds mirror through the pivot when flipped. `PolygonCollider2D.CreatePrimitive` and the default pentagon have radius `scale` (half that before); Box and Capsule sizes and Circle radii keep at least 0.0001 per component, radii at most 1e6. Not measured: a renderer whose object differs from the collider's, the Box's tiling state for Tiled sprites (`autoTiling` stays as set). |

Residuals: the UV distribution metrics (not computed), signed zeros of reparented children's local rotation, 1 ulp of a `LookAt` rotation (rows below). Scene and prefab instances (Q451; tests/projects/speedtree9 668/696 with the six instance transforms carrying the signed-zero residual): a variant of a tree with the edits tree prefabs make (a child added to a level's renderers, a level's shadows off, a material slot remapped, the Rigidbody and a collision object removed, a collider added), scene instances of the variant, of a tree (moved, turned, scaled) and of a project ScriptedImporter's prefab (a script's field and a collider overridden), and a scene object's references into them match the Editor: the managed engine writes the prefab's hierarchy (GameObjects and Transforms, mesh filters, mesh and sprite renderers, LOD groups, Rigidbodies, box, sphere, capsule and mesh colliders, Trees, scripts) as documents the scene expansion uses like any prefab's, the import's meshes and materials referenced by the asset's GUID; another component class fails the asset's instances loudly (the instance is a missing prefab with a warning naming the class). Under URP 17 (Q451; tests/projects/speedtree9urp 310/311 + 1 recorded residual since Q461, two identical Editor captures, OpenGL Core) every material's shader, keywords, queue, tags and properties match after a render, URP's `[MaterialSettingsCallback]` included; in the URP 17 sample's demo comparison the trees draw with URP's SpeedTree9_URP shader graph generated by the Shader Graph package. In a windowed Editor importing the project afresh (the probe's capture since Q461), at the first Play-mode frame `Shader.globalRenderPipeline` is set, the URP sub-shader is active and the imported materials keep URP's MotionVectors pass on (so their import ran with the URP sub-shader active, inferred), as in Unbound, which creates the pipeline before the first render; a batch-mode Editor with no camera has not created it until something renders, so its imports ran with the shader's fallback sub-shader active (queue 2000, no tags, the MotionVectors pass turned off), an artifact of that capture mode (Q451's batch reference). On OpenGL Core the Editor drops a pass whose program excludes glcore (the graph's GBuffer pass), as Unbound does since Q461 (row `Passes whose program excludes the device`). Recorded difference: the extra map's sRGB (the texture post-processing below). Wind (Q461; `Terrain/SpeedTreeWindSimulation.cs`; tests/projects/speedtree9wind 3360/3360, two identical Editor captures, URP 17 and then the built-in pipeline, OpenGL Core, NVIDIA): each wind asset keeps one wind that eases each frame toward the sum of the active directional zones, and a tree's renderer draws with its `SpeedTreeWind` constants (and `SpeedTreeWindHistory` when it draws per-object motion vectors), bit-exact over 420 frames of zones coming, strengthening past 1, turning around, one under a mirrored parent, going, the wind settling, a spherical zone alone, a zone without wind and a wind again; a renderer gets its constants when it first draws while the world has wind (a directional wind, a spherical zone or a wind still moving, not a directional zone without wind; a spherical zone without wind is unmeasured; measured with trees that first draw after the wind settled, under a spherical zone alone and under a zone without wind), and afterwards draws with its wind's constants of each frame, also once the wind has settled; before that the Editor's draw reads what an earlier draw left, Unbound's zeros; gusts take no part (Unity 6000.0.71f1 never turns them on: the probe's tree asks for a gust almost every frame; other releases are unmeasured); `Tree.hasSpeedTreeWind` answers whether a wind asset is referenced. The Editor binds the constants as constant buffers (a renderer's property block shows no wind vectors); Unbound gives them to the draw. Unmeasured: devices without constant buffers. The render pipelines' (core) GPU Resident Drawer path is done (Q492; tests/projects/residentdrawer `speedtree`, 3677/3677 then 5405/5405 with spherical zones, two identical Editor captures, Vulkan, NVIDIA only): a renderer whose object has a Tree with a wind asset is packed with the tree flag, so the drawer gives its instances wind parameters, and `SpeedTreeWindManager.UpdateWindAndWriteBufferWindParams` writes each listed tree's eleven SpeedTree 9 constants at its element of the upload buffer (the previous frame's for a tree that has just become visible; zeros there for a renderer without per-object motion vectors), and zeros for every listed instance while the world has no wind (also outside Play mode, and for the parameters SpeedTree 9 does not use and an instance whose Tree or wind asset was removed after the drawer took it, where the Editor uploads whatever its upload buffer held: unmeasured); a Tree without a wind asset is not a tree for the drawer (unmeasured: the probe's trees all have one); the drawer then copies them into the history parameters each frame (which needed `ComputeShader.SetInts` and the other array setters to copy the array at the call: the drawer fills one scratch array for the wind and the history addresses). Spherical zones are done (Q492; tests/projects/speedtree9wind 12988/12988 with frames 425 to 780 added, earlier rows unchanged; tests/projects/residentdrawer `speedtree` 5405/5405 for the drawer's trees, frames 385 to 470 added; OpenGL Core and Vulkan, NVIDIA only): a tree drawn while spherical zones reach its bounds (its LOD group's tree renderers', else its renderer's) gets a local wind after that draw, when a spherical zone was active at the frame's wind update; each zone in reach blows at its main wind from its position toward the bounds' center raised by a quarter of their height. The local wind starts from its asset's wind (direction, strength, targets, time, noise) and eases each frame toward the directional plus the spherical wind at the tree, its noise moving only while it has strength; out of every zone's reach, while its asset's wind blows, each part's noise position heads straight for the point where it meets the asset's wind's drifting position at four times the drift speed (a meeting later than 15 seconds jumps), and once arrived at the asset's wind (direction, strength, noise within 1e-5) the tree takes the asset's wind again. Measured too: the levels of a tree's LOD group share the group's first level's local wind (a drawn later level joins it at its next draw; the group is reached by its levels' bounds taken together, levels it does not draw included, `SplitGroup`; the order the levels leave it is unmeasured), spherical zones whose winds cancel exactly at a tree starting no local wind (`Cancelled`, measured with no directional wind), a zone coming back in reach while the trees return, a local wind started while its asset's wind is at rest (from rest), and local winds counting as wind in the world (a tree first drawn while only they remain gets its constants). Unmeasured: a spherical zone that exactly cancels the directional wind or sits exactly at the point it blows toward, a zone without wind or radius (no local wind), and several cameras drawing a tree in the frame its local wind starts (they take the asset's wind of that frame). SpeedTree 7/8 wind. More than eight levels (warns and keeps eight, unmeasured); the Editor's post-processing of a tree's textures is done (Q492; tests/projects/speedtree9urp 311/311, its extra-map residual resolved): whenever a tree is imported the Editor changes the import settings of the textures its materials use (a colour map: alpha is transparency, mip maps preserve coverage at alpha reference 0.1; a normal map: the normal map type; an extra map: no sRGB; each map used and naming a file relative to the tree's folder) and saves them into those textures' .meta files. Unbound writes no authored file: a texture's import applies these settings to its own reading of the .meta (its other settings as the .meta says), with the trees that use it among its cache entry's inputs, so a texture whose .meta the Editor has not rewritten (or which has none) imports as in the Editor after it imported the tree; a file another importer imports (a scripted one) is left alone, as the Editor's rule takes texture importers only. Measured: the extra map's sRGB. Not compared with an Editor capture (follow-up): the colour map's settings and a normal map whose .meta has another type (whether the type change brings other settings with it), map paths with `..`, backslashes or other letter case, a texture whose settings a user changed back after the Editor's last import of the tree (the Editor keeps them until the tree imports again; Unbound applies the tree's settings), and a tree's map that a sprite atlas also packs. Also open: a script reading the map's `TextureImporter` settings gets the .meta's own values (the import takes the tree's), and the trees' maps are found once at start-up (a tree rewritten during a run takes effect at the next start) |
